This file will contain a list of all of the prompts that have been written by the user and a summarized version of what was done by Claude code to fulfil these prompts and the result of those actions. as a prompt is written and answered the results are to be documented here. This will act as a look up history for claude so if the use case arises it can look back on a previous prompt and find the information that it needs.

**Update this file at least once per session, before the session ends** - even a short session fixing one small thing gets a dated entry. The user flagged (2026-09-14) that this had been happening only opportunistically rather than every session, which defeats the file's whole purpose as a lookup history.

Wrote Claude/TECH STACK AND ARCHITECTURE.md covering monorepo layout, DB schema, Sheet sync design, barcode pipeline, public/private build pipeline, security posture, and a phased build order mapped back to STEP BY STEP PROCESS AND AUTOMATION.md. Added a backlog item to To Do list.md for the public-repo sanitization script. No code was written this session.

Used the real service-account credentials to read the actual sheet (read-only) before attempting any writes, and found the real header row doesn't match Claude/RESOURCES.md's column names exactly - inconsistent casing, missing "a", "(y/n)" suffixes, and a "tommatoes" typo - plus the sheet's tab is literally named "Sheet1" (the "Copy of All Dvds and specs" name the user gave is the spreadsheet's document title, not the tab name). Also fixed a latent bug: the old column-index-to-letter conversion only handled single letters (A-Z), which would have broken once new columns pushed past column Z. 2026-08-26 (same day, third follow-up): User gave a Supabase personal access token and said to just do it. Used Supabase's Management API (api.supabase.com, HTTPS, separate from the blocked direct-Postgres path) to run 0001_init.sql against both the private and public projects. Verified via REST that the titles table existed and was queryable before touching real data.

First full sync attempt failed: the real "Movie or TV" column has ~40 distinct spellings for what's really a handful of categories (Moive/Moviw typos, Tv Series/TV Sereis casing and typos, and legitimate categories the original schema didn't account for - TV Movie, TV Episode, Live Performance, Sports Recording, etc.), which violated the CHECK constraint on that column. Concluded the constraint itself was the mistake, not the data - RESOURCES.md's own spec calls this field open-ended ("... etc."). Dropped the constraint on both live projects via the Management API, updated 0001_init.sql and packages/shared/src/types.ts (movie_or_tv is now free text, not a closed union) to match, and added a normalization table to sync-sheet.ts that fixes known typos/casing without rejecting anything unrecognized.

Second attempt got further (Supabase upsert succeeded) but failed writing back to the Sheet: "Range (Sheet1!AC1) exceeds grid limits. Max rows: 3999, max columns: 28" - a Sheet's grid size is a separate fixed property from how much data populates it, and adding 3 new columns pushed past the existing 28-column grid. Since the Sheet write is atomic and failed before writing anything, but the Supabase insert had already gone through, truncated the private project's titles table to avoid duplicate rows on retry (different unique_ids would've been generated each attempt). Added grid-expansion logic to sync-sheet.ts (checks gridProperties.columnCount via spreadsheets.get, expands via spreadsheets.batchUpdate if needed) and re-ran successfully: 3,063 rows in Supabase, matching 3,063 rows in the Sheet with all three new columns (Unique Identifier, Barcode Identifier, Genre Location) present and unique_id backfilled on every row. Verified both independently (REST query count + a read-only Sheets fetch of the new columns) before considering it done.

2026-08-27: User chose Phase 1 (barcode scanning) next, then rejected the first draft plan with detailed feedback: manual Sheet edits must also sync to the DB automatically (not just app-originated writes), the entire existing ~3,063-item collection needs its barcode/image data backfilled in bulk (not just new purchases going forward), and both collections and documentary chronological shelf-ordering must be tackled within Phase 1 itself rather than deferred. Redesigned around a scan-then-resolve-later architecture (pending_scans queue table, decoupling scanning speed from UPCitemdb's 100/day free-tier limit) and built it end to end: migration 0002_pending_scans.sql (pending_scans table + depicted_era_start column on titles) applied to both live Supabase projects; extracted all Sheet-row parsing into packages/shared/src/titleParsing.ts so scripts/apps/web share one implementation; four secret-gated Next.js routes (scan/queue, scan/resolve, scan/confirm, sheet-webhook); a Google Apps Script (apps/sheet-scripts/onEdit.gs, an installable "On edit" trigger since simple triggers can't make HTTP calls) for the Sheet-to-DB half of the sync; and the full mobile app (expo-camera scanner, pending-scans review list, confirm/manual-fill screen with a collection checklist mode, success screen) wired to those routes. Verified with a real end-to-end test (a genuine barcode resolving to a real OMDB match, written to both Supabase and the Sheet), which surfaced and fixed a real bug: the shelf-location neighbor query wasn't excluding the just-inserted title from its own sibling search. All test/debug rows were deleted from Supabase and the Sheet immediately after each verification pass.

2026-08-28: User started real device testing and reported a sequence of issues, each fixed and verified before moving on: (1) Expo Go's Play Store build didn't yet support this project's SDK 57 - confirmed via web search that the Play Store release was still in Google's review queue, fixed by sideloading the matching build directly from expo.dev/go; (2) the camera-permission screen's button rendered off the top of the visible area with no safe-area handling at all - added react-native-safe-area-context (SafeAreaProvider + useSafeAreaInsets) across all four mobile screens; (3) that fix immediately caused a regression - alignItems:"center" on the shared container style (added to center the permission screen) collapsed CameraView's width to near-zero since it only sets flex:1 for height and depends on the parent's default stretch behavior, so the live camera preview rendered black - fixed by splitting the permission screen into its own centered style, separate from the camera-view container. While fixing these, discovered the public GitHub repo had never been synced past Phase 0 - ran the sanitize-public-repo.ts pipeline (fresh clone -> sanitize into a staging dir -> copy over -> diff-check for secrets/Letterboxd references -> commit -> push) and brought it fully current with the private repo, after confirming with the user first since it was a much larger push than what had been asked for in the moment.

User then asked whether to test-scan a disc already in the collection (lacking a barcode) or a brand-new one - surfaced that the confirm route only ever created new rows, with no path to attach a scanned barcode to an already-catalogued entry, which is exactly the bulk-backfill scenario Phase 1 was supposed to cover. Asked the user two clarifying questions about the matching/update design and got detailed answers: match by base title text (the user names titles verbatim except when a box set distinguishes cuts inline, e.g. "Final Cut"/"Director's Cut"), narrowed by format/disc-count hints parsed from the UPC product text only when a hint actually narrows the candidate set; on confirmed match, refresh only fields that genuinely come from IMDb/OMDB (release date, genre, director, rating, IMDb page) and never packaging fields the user already entered by hand, and never overwrite runtime when the matched title looks like a specific cut/edition (OMDB only has one canonical runtime per film). Built this as two new routes (scan/find-existing, scan/link-existing), a Sheet partial-row-update helper (updateSheetFieldsByUniqueId - patches only the changed cells, never blanks the rest of the row), and a new ConfirmScreen step that checks for a backfill match before creating anything, offering "attach to this entry" (auto when unambiguous, a picker when not) or "no, this is different - add as new". Verified against a real existing collection entry ("The Fireman") via the live API and Supabase/Sheet, then reverted the test barcode/image and deleted the temporary pending_scans row and Sheet-cleanup scripts immediately afterward, per the standing practice of never leaving test data in the user's real production data.

2026-08-28 (later the same day): User did their first real phone testing and hit a string of real bugs, each found and fixed via the live database rather than guesswork: (1) the camera preview rendered solid black - a safe-area fix from earlier in the day had put `alignItems: "center"` on the shared container style, which collapsed CameraView's width since it only sets `flex: 1` for height and depends on the parent's default stretch behaviour; split into a separate centered style just for the permission screen. (2) Holding the phone steady on one barcode while lining up a shot queued the same barcode 8 times - the debounce re-armed on a fixed 3-second timer even while the code stayed continuously in view; replaced it with a "still holding this code" clock that only resets once the gap since the last sighting exceeds 2s. (3) Added a "discard this scan" action (new pending_scans delete path) so a stray/junk read doesn't need to go through OMDB resolution just to get rid of it. (4) The real scanned disc ("Paper Planes") resolved with zero OMDB candidates - UPCitemdb's listing title included resale noise ("Used; Acceptable") that survived the cleanup step and left stray commas behind, so OMDB was searched for "Paper Planes , , Used; Acceptable" instead of "Paper Planes"; added marketplace-noise-word stripping and leftover-punctuation cleanup. Verified the fix live by resetting the same pending_scans row and re-running the resolver. Also changed the OMDB candidate list from vertical text rows to a horizontal scrolling list of poster cards, since text alone can't distinguish between different films that share a title/year.

User then asked what data actually comes back from a scan and, on hearing it's just Title/Year/poster (OMDB has no per-edition data at all, only one entry per film), proposed a detailed auto-fill design: pre-fill the manual title field from the cleaned UPC listing text (packaging-edition words like "Special Edition" stripped, since those are marketing fluff rather than a content distinction like "Final Cut"), pre-fill format from a text-pattern match on the listing, and - the most interesting idea - use the listing's own annotated year as a chronological upper bound to eliminate OMDB candidates released after it, since a disc can't exist for a film that hasn't come out yet. Implemented all three (`extractProductYear`/`filterCandidatesByMaxYear` in packages/shared, applied inside the resolver so narrowing happens before the review screen ever shows candidates) and verified live against the real "Paper Planes" scan - the year filter correctly dropped a 2016 and a 2023 candidate, leaving 8. Also surfaced UPCitemdb's own listing photo (already captured in `UpcProduct.imageUrl` from an earlier session) in the review screen for the first time, since a real photo of the actual listing is a much stronger signal for telling apart same-title/same-year releases than OMDB's generic poster. Explicitly did not build the other two ideas in the same message - auto-matching the listing photo against OMDB posters via image similarity, and listing alternate physical editions of an identified film - and said so plainly: the second hits a hard data-availability wall (no API in this stack, including OMDB itself, tracks per-edition/per-pressing data - Blu-ray.com would have it but has no API and was already ruled out entirely), while the first is technically feasible but a distinct chunk of work with a real accuracy caveat (a resale photo often looks different from a stylized theatrical poster even for the correct film) that deserves its own discussion rather than being built silently alongside everything else.

User then asked for exactly the image-matching feature to be built after all, with a specific UX for the low-confidence case: auto-match by cover photo, and when the user taps "not this item," reveal the UPC photo alongside every poster it was compared against so they can pick manually. Also explicitly moved "list alternate physical editions" to the backlog, since no data source for it exists anywhere reachable - the user's own plan is to invent spreadsheet workarounds for that later. Built the matching with Jimp's perceptual-hash (pHash/compareHashes), verified the plumbing against synthetic identical/unrelated image pairs (0 vs. ~0.375 distance) since no real scan so far has had both a UPC photo and multiple candidates to test against for real. Hit a real architectural problem partway through: adding the full `jimp` package to packages/shared (the package both apps/web and apps/mobile depend on) broke `expo export` entirely, first because `@jimp/plugin-print` does a Node-only `import("fs")` for custom font loading, and after building a minimal Jimp instance from individual `@jimp/*` packages to dodge that, again because `@jimp/js-png`'s own dependency (pngjs) needs Node's `util`/`stream` for zlib inflate - confirming this wasn't a one-off bad plugin but a fundamental fact about real image decoders. Fixed properly rather than working around it: split the barcode-resolver logic (and its new image-matching code) out of packages/shared into a new packages/backend workspace that only apps/web and scripts/ depend on, never apps/mobile, so Metro never even attempts to resolve Jimp's dependencies. Verified with `npx expo export --platform android` (653 modules, clean) after the split, plus a full `tsc --noEmit` pass across every workspace and a real re-run of the resolver against the live "Paper Planes" scan through the new package boundary.

2026-09-09: Asked what TMDb's actual Animation/Live Action categories are and proposed hiding that field once TMDb confirms live action, keeping it as a style-only dropdown when confirmed animated - built as a genuinely three-valued `isAnimated` (`true`/`false`/`null`) rather than a plain boolean, since collapsing "no TMDb match" into "confirmed live action" would have silently forced the wrong value. Then, ahead of a planned multi-week full-collection physical rescan, asked to brainstorm needed future columns first rather than risk having to re-touch every disc later - landed on the "physical-only vs. metadata-backfillable" framing (only what's observable solely from the disc/case has to be captured now; everything TMDb/API-keyed can wait) and built the whole "Backfill Rescan" feature end to end: a hard requirement that every candidate-backed entry resolve to a real TMDb id before it can be confirmed (with a manual override field), seven new physical-only columns (`imdb_id`, `tmdb_page`, `date_added`, `disc_condition` + its user-defined severity scale, `case_notes`, `release_variant_note`, `watched`, `depicted_era_label`), and a broadened pre-submit similar-entry check (poster + full field comparison, Overwrite/Is-a-new-entry/Reject) replacing the old narrower exact-match/attach-only flow. Migrations 0011/0012 applied to both live Supabase projects via the Management API using a fresh token the user provided in-chat (never persisted to a file). Fixed a real bug found live: `GOOGLE_SHEET_RANGE` was hardcoded to a 32-column cap, silently blind to every new column past it. Also corrected two real data mistakes the user identified by exact disc (Punisher/High Tension disc conditions) and confirmed a genuine duplicate-row database error (two "4K UHD" Iron Man 2 rows - the user confirmed only one should exist and gave the exact fix) rather than guessing.

User then asked about integrating Letterboxd (account "heygoogoo") to auto-detect watched status, and turned it into a real request: import their Letterboxd CSV export once, using their own habit of tagging each log with the disc format watched (blu-ray/DVD/4K UHD) to resolve which physical copy, with a throwaway manual-categorization web page for anything untagged. Mid-task the user pointed to a folder named `Letterboxd` they'd placed at the repo root with their real export - this got swept into a public-repo sync staging copy moments later (caught via an unexpectedly large diff-stat before anything was committed/pushed, not after) because `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s file copy is a raw filesystem walk that never consults `.gitignore`. Fixed at both levels: `.gitignore` already had `/Letterboxd/` (belt), and added `"Letterboxd"` to the sanitizer's own `EXCLUDE_DIRS` (the actual fix for the raw-copy path). No personal data ever reached either GitHub repo. An initial rough dry-run against the actual export (754 distinct watched films) estimated 228 matching the physical collection. The user then gave three more pieces of design input in one message: duplicate-title rows are usually a *legitimate* multi-disc-collection membership, not a mistake (Iron Man 2 was the exception, confirmed as a real error) - so future duplicate-detection logic must check `is_collection`/`name_of_collection` before flagging anything; do TV/season-completeness in this same pass rather than deferring it; and a batch of the user's oldest Letterboxd entries (backfilled from Facebook Stories/IMDb notes) all share the same few days regardless of real watch date, so those dates must be left blank rather than trusted, with a backlog item to recover the real dates from the Facebook archive later. Confirmed against the real data (queried live) that the collection already models TV exactly as hoped: a DVD holding one classic-series serial has that serial's own title as `title` and matches Letterboxd like any movie; a Blu-ray box set holding a whole season is a single row with no list of its constituent serials, resolved instead via TMDb's `episode_groups` endpoint. Split `watched` into two columns per the user's own distinction (`watched` = this specific disc, new `watched_title` = this film/season on any copy - migration 0013, applied to both projects) and added matching manual checkboxes to `ConfirmScreen` for pre-Letterboxd viewings.

Before wiring any of the confirmed tags into real matching logic, the user asked to review the exact tag list first, since several of the ~143 real tags read as sarcastic commentary rather than an actual viewing-method signal - confirmed the proposed disc-format tags (blu-ray/dvd/4k uhd blu-ray) and non-disc tags (cinema/docplay) as correct, and added one correction: the 🌐 emoji tag (116 uses) means "watched on the web," not obvious from the tag text alone. Built the real import script (`scripts/src/import-letterboxd.ts`, dry-run by default, `--apply` to write for real) plus a Doctor Who season-completeness check using TMDb's per-season episode data (verified live that TMDb lists classic-era episodes as numbered segments of each serial - e.g. Season 7's 25 episodes collapse to exactly its 4 real serials once the segment-number suffix is stripped). Running it for real surfaced that the earlier "228 matched" estimate was simply wrong: exact-title matching against the real collection found only ~89 real matches (independently verified: 85/754 `watched.csv` entries match a `titles` row by normalized title alone) - most of the user's Letterboxd history was never owned on disc at all. Also caught, before running with `--apply`, that the season-completeness check as first written would have fired on all 334 whole-season TV rows in the collection (not just Doctor Who) - checked live that ordinary shows' individual episodes are essentially never logged as separate Letterboxd entries the way classic Who serials are, so restricted the check specifically to Doctor Who rather than shipping ~300 meaningless review-queue entries. Built the throwaway `letterboxd_review_queue` table (0014, private project only) and a `/letterboxd-review` page (query-param-secret-gated, matching the proportionate-guard pattern already used for the scan API) for the ~56 items the script can't confidently resolve alone.

The manual-review pass surfaced more than the script could handle alone. Real screenshotted notes revealed: (1) a genuine collection-membership ambiguity the review UI never modeled - a film matching multiple owned copies where the right one is a box-set member (Pink Panther's "A Shot in the Dark", the Coppola Masterpiece Collection's "American Graffiti"), and one case ("Bride of Frankenstein") where the *actual* watched copy wasn't even offered as a candidate at all, since it was a Blu-Ray inside a completely different box set the import's title-matching never connected; (2) a durable rule about 4K UHD combo packs bundling a standard Blu-ray disc in the same case, confirmed by the user across two real examples (Black Panther, Captain Marvel) and asked to be remembered for future runs - added as `formatsAreCompatible` in the import script, deliberately one-directional (a Blu-ray tag against an owned DVD stays a real mismatch); (3) for Doctor Who's incomplete seasons, the user clarified per-serial DVDs they'd watched that the season-completeness check hadn't found via Letterboxd fuzzy-matching, applied by hand. Along the way, made two real operational mistakes in the same session that cost the user actual typed work: a live edit to the review page (adding error-surfacing) reset its in-memory draft state via Next.js Fast Refresh, and separately resetting/replacing the whole `letterboxd_review_queue` table (to fix a real matching bug, below) silently orphaned review IDs an open browser tab still had in memory - compounded by the page never having checked whether its own save requests actually succeeded, so failures looked identical to success. Fixed the response-checking gap immediately; the lost notes had to be recovered from user-provided screenshots instead.

Also found and fixed a real correctness bug in the import script before it could do more damage: title-only matching (no year check) let unrelated films sharing a title contaminate each other's watched status - a 2025 "Frankenstein" cinema log wrote onto the 1931 Frankenstein Blu-Ray's `watched`/date fields, and unrelated Captain America productions (1944, 1979) wrote onto the unrelated 1991 Captain America disc. Added year-tolerant matching (±1 year, since a couple of real titles like Captain America and Bride of the Monster have a genuine one-year discrepancy between sources) and did a full reset-and-redo: reverted every row the buggy run had touched, cleared the queue, re-ran with the fix, then reapplied the handful of user-confirmed manual resolutions that predated the reset. Verified clean afterward by re-deriving the same mismatch check and confirming the surviving "mismatches" were the audit script's own false positives (checking irrelevant Letterboxd entries against already-correct rows), not real bugs.

Once the queue reached zero, deleted the throwaway page/route and dropped the table (0016) per the original plan, and fixed one more gap found while cleaning up: `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s Letterboxd guard only scanned `apps`/`packages`, so `import-letterboxd.ts`/`letterboxdCsv.ts` (kept as reusable scripts, living under `scripts/`) would have silently entered the public checkout without ever tripping the check - added `scripts` to the scanned directories.

Then the user corrected the two-metric design itself: "watched" should mean "I've seen the movie at some point" (broad), and "watched title" should mean "watched this specific disc" (narrow) - the reverse of what had been built. Rather than relabel and risk confusion, swapped the actual data (`0017_swap_watched_semantics.sql` - a single `update titles set watched = watched_title, watched_title = watched`, safe in one statement since Postgres evaluates every SET expression against old row values first) and then renamed the column to match its corrected meaning (`0018_rename_watched_title_to_watched_disc.sql`, plus the user's own instruction to rename the Sheet header from "Watched Title" to "Watched Disc"). Updated every code reference across `packages/shared`, the confirm route, and `ConfirmScreen` (including swapping which of the two on-screen checkboxes drives which state variable) - the import script's actual matching logic needed no changes at all, only which field name each already-correct fact gets written to. Verified with a real example (Godzilla 2014, seen on Neon but not owned on disc) before considering it done: `watched=true`, `watched_disc=false`, exactly right under the new mapping.

Committed and pushed the private repo (`origin`) cleanly - 35 files, a fresh PAT provided in chat used only inline in the push command, never persisted. Pushing the public repo hit a real gap: no local checkout existed, and the private repo's own credential helper had no write access there either. Cloned the public repo fresh into the scratchpad, hit a genuine Windows long-path checkout failure on an old asset path (fixed with `core.longpaths=true` + `git reset --hard`), then discovered `sanitize-public-repo.ts` refuses to run into a non-empty directory at all (even one holding only `.git`) - the correct flow is sanitize into a *separate* empty staging dir, then mirror that into the real clone with `robocopy /MIR /XD .git` so git sees adds/deletes naturally. The resulting `git status` showed ~90 "modified" files that turned out to be pure CRLF/LF noise from Windows `core.autocrlf` (confirmed via `git diff` showing zero real difference for each) - `git add -A` correctly discarded that noise, leaving the exact same real 35-file diff as the private repo. Committed locally, but the actual `git push` (token embedded in the URL, same technique that worked for the private repo) was refused twice by Claude Code's own auto-mode safety classifier as credential exposure, even after the user weighed in that the public repo has no PII (a separate concern from the classifier's actual objection, which is about the raw token appearing in a shell command at all, not what data the destination repo holds). Left the commit sitting locally at `.../scratchpad/public-repo-checkout` for the user to push themselves (`git push origin main` from that folder, browser-based Credential Manager login) or to explicitly allow via a Bash permission rule - did not keep retrying the same blocked action.

User then asked for Genre and Franchise to behave like proper multi-value tag-autocomplete fields: typing filters a dropdown of existing values, tapping one appends it (plus a comma) rather than replacing the whole field, matching then resets so the next tag is matched independently of prior ones, and a genuinely new tag becomes a future suggestion once saved. Built `TagAutocompleteInput` (`apps/mobile/src/components/TagAutocompleteInput.tsx`), a sibling to the existing single-value `AutocompleteInput` rather than a modification of it (that component's whole-field-replace behavior is still correct for Format/Disk Region/Genre Location/Rating/Studio) - it matches suggestions only against the text after the last comma, and on tap rebuilds the value as `[...priorTags, picked].join(", ") + ", "`, which both appends correctly and resets the match segment to empty. Added `genre`/`franchise` to `fieldOptions.ts` (`distinctSortedFlat` - flattens each row's `text[]` array into individual distinct tags, not distinct whole-array values, since both columns are multi-value) and swapped both fields on `ConfirmScreen` over from plain `TextInput`/free text to `TagAutocompleteInput`. Also added a `loadFieldOptions(true)` refresh right after a successful confirm - previously the field-options cache was genuinely never force-refreshed anywhere despite the doc comment describing that as supported, so a brand-new value on any autocomplete field (not just Genre/Franchise) only became suggestible after an app restart; now it's available on the very next scan in the same session. `tsc --noEmit` clean across all four workspaces.

User then began a real rescan of 3 4K UHD Marvel Blu-rays and, after hitting a real "Captain Marvel" scan whose UPC listing title (`Captain Marvel | Brie Larson, Samuel L J Blu-Ray Expertly Referubished Product`) couldn't be found via lookup, proposed parsing listings like this into separate variables - title, format, cast - using cast names to disambiguate when a title search returns multiple same-titled candidates, falling back to plain manual search only when nothing parses at all. Asked three clarifying questions before building anything (whether the example was real listing text, whether cast matching should use OMDB's `Actors` field or a fuller TMDb cast lookup, and whether this should be silent or surfaced in the UI); the user confirmed the example was real verbatim pending-scan text, said top 3-4 billed names (OMDB's `Actors` field) are enough with no need for a full cast/crew lookup, and said it should run silently. Built and verified against the real string: `cleanProductTitleForSearch` (`packages/shared/src/omdb.ts`) now cuts a listing title at its first `|` before any other cleanup, which alone fixes this exact failure (search query becomes the clean "Captain Marvel" instead of the whole noisy string OMDB was finding zero matches for). Added `extractListingMetaText`/`scoreCandidateByCastHint` (word-overlap scoring against a candidate's OMDB `Actors` field, deliberately not trying to first classify which part of the leftover text "is a name" - real cast mentions are often abbreviated, e.g. "Samuel L. Jackson" shortened to "Samuel L J" in this exact listing, which wouldn't substring-match) and `narrowCandidatesByCastHint` (`packages/backend/src/scanResolver.ts`) to silently collapse the OMDB candidate list to one when cast overlap gives a genuine, unambiguous winner, using the same never-eliminate-everything safety pattern as the existing year filter. Verified with a standalone script against the real listing text (search query correctly reduced to "Captain Marvel"; score of 3 against the real Captain Marvel cast vs. 0 against an unrelated film's cast) and a full `tsc --noEmit` pass across every workspace.

User then asked two more things: whether the new cast-hint process actually looks up OMDB/TMDb details from the parsed title and surfaces the best match (confirmed it already does - `omdbSearch` already ran on the cleaned title before this session's fix, and the existing generic "auto-select when only one candidate remains" logic in `ConfirmScreen` already picks up a cast-hint-narrowed single candidate with no further change needed); and that a fully-manual entry (Skip - enter manually instead) should expose Genre, Running Time, Director, and Sub-franchise for typing, excluding OMDB/Rotten Tomatoes/IMDb/TMDb links and ids since a page almost certainly doesn't exist for something neither database could find. Checked and confirmed the link/id exclusion was already correct by construction (`showTmdbOverrideField` is gated on a real candidate being selected), but the four detail fields genuinely didn't exist anywhere in `ConfirmScreen` at all - added state, draft-cache persistence, input fields (Genre/Director as comma-separated free text per RESOURCES.md's own Sheet convention), and wired them into the confirm payload, sent only when `selected.size === 0` so they can never clobber a real candidate's OMDB-derived values (the confirm route already supported `manual.genre`/`manual.director`/`manual.running_time_mins`/`manual.sub_franchise` as fallbacks - only the mobile form was missing them). Verified with a full `tsc --noEmit` pass across every workspace.

User then reported the hold-steady countdown freezes on its last number when moving the phone away from a barcode mid-countdown. Root cause: `onBarcodeScanned` only fires while a barcode is actually detected in frame - moving away doesn't fire any event at all, so the countdown state was only ever updated reactively from a sighting and had nothing to reset it once sightings stopped. Fixed in `ScannerScreen.tsx` with an independent 250ms `setInterval` that polls `pendingBarcodeRef` regardless of incoming sightings and clears it (hiding the countdown) once the gap since the last sighting reaches `SIGHTING_GAP_MS` - deliberately reused that existing constant rather than introducing a second "gave up" threshold that could disagree with the one already used to decide whether a *new* sighting continues the same hold. Verified with `tsc --noEmit`.

User then rescanned the real Captain Marvel disc and reported no best match was found despite it being a well-known film, suspecting the search was still running against the full noisy listing text rather than the parsed "Captain Marvel". Checked the live `pending_scans` row directly and confirmed exactly that: `omdbCandidates: []` for the real listing (`Captain Marvel | Brie Larson, Samuel L J Blu-ray Expertly Refurbished Product`), the signature of the *old*, unfixed search still running. Root cause found in `apps/web/next.config.ts`: `transpilePackages` only listed `@danflix/shared`, not `@danflix/backend` - both ship raw TS source with no build step and need Next to transpile them, but `@danflix/backend` (where `scanResolver.ts`, and today's cast-hint fix, actually live) was missing, so the already-running `next dev` process never picked up the change at all despite it compiling and type-checking cleanly. Added `@danflix/backend` to `transpilePackages` and restarted the web dev server (found and killed the stale process on port 3000, started fresh - confirmed clean startup). The stuck Captain Marvel/other-two-Blu-ray `pending_scans` rows were gone by the time this was investigated (the user had apparently already discarded them to retry) - documented the `transpilePackages` gotcha in `repo-layout.md` and asked the user to simply rescan again now that the fix is actually live.

After that rescan, the user reported two real problems: two candidates for the real Captain Marvel scan had identical posters, very similar names, and identical years (likely an alternate cut with its own OMDB entry) with no way to tell them apart, and separately the candidate title text was getting visually cut off. Fixed both. For the duplicate-candidate case, cast-hint scoring can't help (a genuine alternate cut shares its cast with the original), so added `findDuplicateTitleYearIds` (`packages/shared/src/omdb.ts` - groups candidates by normalized title+Year) and extended `enrichAndNarrowCandidates` (renamed from `narrowCandidatesByCastHint`, unifying both jobs so a candidate's OMDB detail is never fetched twice for two different reasons) to fetch and attach Runtime for any candidate sharing an identical title+year with another - shown next to Year on the review screen (e.g. "2019 · 133 min") as a physical, human-checkable distinguisher, deliberately never used to auto-narrow since nothing in the listing text says which cut is actually in hand. For the truncation, found `numberOfLines={2}` on the poster-card title in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` - removed entirely (rather than raised to a fixed higher number) so a card's title always shows in full and the card grows to fit it. Restarted the web dev server again for the `scanResolver.ts` change to actually take effect (now correctly picked up given the `transpilePackages` fix from moments earlier). Verified `findDuplicateTitleYearIds` directly against synthetic candidates (correctly grouped a same-title/same-year pair, left a same-title/different-year one alone) and a full `tsc --noEmit` pass across every workspace.

User then made two more requests: "Watched this disc" should auto-check "Watched" (obviously watching a specific disc means the film's been seen at all), and separately - after noticing Captain Marvel (2019) genuinely belongs to Marvel, the Marvel Cinematic Universe, *and* the Captain Marvel character franchise all at once, and that Sub-franchise had no input field anywhere - proposed merging `franchise`/`sub_franchise` into one multi-value list, the same shape as `genre`. Fixed the checkbox first (trivial): checking Watched Disc now also checks Watched, and unchecking Watched also clears Watched Disc, so the two can never end up in the contradictory "watched this disc but haven't watched the film" state. For the franchise merge, gave the user a recommendation before touching anything (confirmed `genre` is already a real `text[]` column with a GIN index, so the pattern is precedented) and flagged the real cost - migrating ~3065 existing rows' two scalar columns into one array on both Supabase projects, plus the live Sheet. The user confirmed: merge existing rows as `[franchise, sub_franchise]` in that order, and upgrade the Wikidata prefill to pull every P179 claim instead of just the first while at it.

Verified the real data first (3065 rows: 179 had both set, 1461 only franchise, 5 only sub_franchise) before writing the migration, so every real combination was accounted for. Built `0020_merge_franchise_columns.sql`, updated every affected file (`packages/shared/types.ts`/`titleParsing.ts`/`omdb.ts`/`tasteProfile.ts`, `packages/backend/wikidata.ts`, both `apps/web` scan routes, `apps/mobile`'s `ConfirmScreen`/`scanApi.ts`/`fieldOptions.ts`/`confirmDrafts.ts`) - Franchise changed from a single-value `AutocompleteInput` to plain comma-separated text like Genre/Director (a single-value autocomplete's "replace the whole field" behavior doesn't work for a multi-value field), and the `canonicalizeValue` case-dedup for Franchise was dropped as a disclosed cosmetic tradeoff rather than building a new per-value RPC. Removed the Sub-franchise field from `ConfirmScreen` entirely (it had only existed for a few hours, added the same session before this merge request superseded it).

Live-data work, in order: merged the Sheet's Franchise/Sub-Franchise columns for real (a one-off script - verified the real header positions first, wrote merged "franchise, sub_franchise" values, then deleted the Sub-Franchise column; verified afterward, e.g. "Abbott and Costello, Universal Monsters"), then applied the migration to both Supabase projects via the Management API using a fresh user-provided token (paused mid-task once for a token-limit warning, resumed cleanly - verified via `information_schema` that `franchise` is now `ARRAY` and `sub_franchise` is gone on both projects, and spot-checked real merged rows, e.g. Ant-Man -> `["Antman","Marvel"]`). Restarted the web dev server once more given the volume of backend/shared changes since the last restart. Finally, per the user's explicit request, audited the whole repo for leftover `sub_franchise`/`Sub-franchise` references and updated every one found in the planning docs (`RESOURCES.md`, `TECH STACK AND ARCHITECTURE/INDEX.md`, `database-design.md`, `barcode-review-screen-fields.md`) to describe the new merged-franchise reality rather than the superseded two-column design - the only remaining mentions anywhere in the repo are deliberate historical ones (this migration file, the original `0001_init.sql`, and this journal), never live/confusing ones. Verified with a final full `tsc --noEmit` pass across every workspace.

2026-09-14 (same day, still later): User gave four more pieces of feedback in one message. (1) Made `TagAutocompleteInput`'s Franchise field 3 lines tall (`numberOfLines` prop, multiline `TextInput` with `textAlignVertical: "top"`) since it regularly holds several long comma-separated tags now; Genre stayed single-line. Confirmed (no code change needed) that the dropdown filtering per keystroke was already correct by construction - both `AutocompleteInput` and `TagAutocompleteInput` recompute their suggestion list from the live `value` prop on every render, so it narrows on every letter typed already.

(2) User reported OMDB search results for well-known films often surface making-of documentaries, special-screening footage, and behind-the-scenes featurettes, and asked for these to be collapsed into a tap-to-reveal "Extras" section rather than removed outright (removing risks a real classification mistake hiding an actual candidate). Built `looksLikeExtraContent` (`packages/shared/src/omdb.ts`, a keyword heuristic - "making of", "behind the scenes", "featurette", "special screening", "world premiere", "red carpet", "press conference"/"junket", "q&a", "fan event", "gag reel"/"bloopers", "deleted scenes", "table read"/"roundtable", "panel discussion"); `ConfirmScreen` partitions `candidates` into `mainCandidates`/`extraCandidates` purely for rendering (every piece of real state - `selected`, auto-select-when-one-candidate, the similar-entry check's title - still operates on the full list), showing extras under a collapsed "Extras (N)" toggle below the main row.

(3) Defaulted Special Features Disc Count to 1 the moment it becomes relevant (`specialFeatures` on + multi-disc), via a `useEffect` that only sets it while blank - same "never stomp a real value" pattern as the existing 4K-defaults-Disk-Region-to-"All" effect - since a dedicated bonus-features disc is overwhelmingly a single disc and typing "1" every time was pure friction.

(4) User reported a real duplicate: "Thor: Ragnarok" (freshly barcode-scanned) and a pre-existing "Thor Ragnarok" (no colon, a legacy pre-barcode-pipeline row) both ended up in the collection despite the similar-entry check being designed to catch exactly this (same IMDb id, and both normalize to an identical title). Investigated live rather than guessing: confirmed via direct REST queries that both rows really did share `imdb_page` id `tt3501632`, so both of `find-existing/route.ts`'s own match conditions (imdb-id-equal OR normalized-title-equal) should have fired - then found the actual root cause by testing the route's own query directly: it read `titles` with a plain unpaginated `.select()`, and PostgREST silently caps that at 1000 rows; with 3066 real rows, the legacy row's random UUID happened to sort outside that returned window, so the check simply never saw it. This is the exact same bug class already found and fixed once before in `apps/mobile/src/lib/fieldOptions.ts`, just never applied to this route - fixed the same way (loop with `.range()` until a short page signals the end) and documented it in `database-design.md` as a standing gotcha to watch for in any future unfiltered `titles` query. Separately flagged (not acted on without asking first) that `scripts/src/import-letterboxd.ts`'s one-time Letterboxd import has the identical unpaginated query, meaning the original watched-status backfill may have silently skipped checking roughly two-thirds of the collection - added to `INDEX.md`'s BACKLOG pending the user's go-ahead to re-run it. Cleaned up the one confirmed duplicate by hand: kept the new row (barcode, TMDb linkage, correct spelling, richer Wikidata franchise list) as canonical, copied over the one field only the legacy row had (`rotten_tomatoes_page`), then deleted the legacy row from both Supabase and the Sheet via a one-off script (discarded after running, same pattern as earlier test-data cleanups this session) - verified afterward that exactly one "Thor: Ragnarok" row remains with the Rotten Tomatoes link intact. Full `tsc --noEmit` pass clean across every workspace after all four fixes.

2026-09-14 (same day, still later): User asked to (a) actually check the Letterboxd two-thirds gap the previous message had only flagged, and (b) find out why the new Thor: Ragnarok scan's own automated Rotten Tomatoes lookup never fired even though the legacy duplicate had a real RT link. For (b), fetched the real RT page directly (`https://www.rottentomatoes.com/m/thor_ragnarok`, which itself redirects to `.../thor_ragnarok_2017`) and found `lookupRottenTomatoesPage`'s name-match check compares OMDB's bare title against RT's own schema.org `name` field, which is actually `"<Title> (<Year>)"` - "Thor: Ragnarok (2017)", not "Thor: Ragnarok" - so the exact-match comparison silently failed for any film whose RT page follows this common naming convention, not just this one. Fixed by stripping a trailing `" (YYYY)"` before comparing, and separately switched the returned URL from the guessed pre-redirect slug to the fetch's actual final URL (`res.url`), since RT redirects a bare/no-year slug to the real disambiguated one anyway. Verified live via a standalone script against the real case.

For (a), fixed the same unpaginated-query bug in `scripts/src/import-letterboxd.ts` (same pattern as `fieldOptions.ts`/`find-existing/route.ts`), added a permanent `--verbose` flag (prints every row that would actually change plus the full manual-review list, not just counts) and a diagnostic distinguishing "matches found" from "matches that would actually change a value" (225 of 271). Presented both lists to the user in full rather than just a summary, per their explicit choice on an `AskUserQuestion`. The user then resolved essentially every one of the ~18 genuinely-ambiguous multi-copy/format-mismatch items by hand, in one long message, each with real physical-media detail - working through it surfaced several things worth recording:

Separately, the user believed a Blu-Ray "Bride of Frankenstein" was missing entirely from the Universal Monsters Essential Collection - it wasn't missing, it existed under `"The Bride of Frankenstein"` (leading "The"), which is why searching the Sheet for "Bride of Frankenstein" didn't surface it; that row was already fully correct on `watched`/`watched_disc`, just needed `disk_region` (was "4", a DVD-style region code left over from some default, corrected to "B") and a TMDb id/link filled in from what the user provided.

- One item stayed a disclosed guess rather than a certainty: Pirates of the Caribbean: Dead Man's Chest's "plastic case vs. slip cover" DVD distinction was inferred from which of the two owned DVD rows already had a dedicated special-features disc recorded, not confirmed by the user directly - flagged as such in the report back to them.
- Two items stayed genuinely unresolved (no distinguishing detail existed for either near-identical owned copy, and none was asked for): War of the Worlds (1953)'s two DVD copies, and Land of the Dead's two DVD copies.

Ran the corrected `--apply` first (271 rows updated in Supabase, 490 cells mirrored to the Sheet), then a second one-off script (`apply-letterboxd-corrections.ts`, discarded after running) applying all 19 of the manual disambiguation patches above to both Supabase and the Sheet, verifying the trickiest ones afterward (the American Graffiti reversal, the Bride of Frankenstein TMDb/region fix, Dawn of the Dead's format fix) via direct REST queries. Full `tsc --noEmit` pass clean across every workspace throughout.

2026-09-14 (same day, still later): User gave real eBay Developer credentials in chat - sandbox-only (App ID/Dev ID/Cert ID, all containing `SBX`), with production access approval still pending (~1 day out). Stored them in the gitignored `apps/web/.env.local` and `scripts/.env` (matching this repo's actual env-file layout - there is no repo-root `.env.local`; confirmed both files are covered by existing `.gitignore`/`apps/web/.gitignore` rules and never appear in `git status`), plus documented placeholder entries in both `.env.example` templates. Made `packages/backend/src/estimatedValue/scrapers/ebay.ts` environment-aware: a new `EBAY_ENVIRONMENT` var ("sandbox" | "production", defaulting to production if `EBAY_CLIENT_ID`/`EBAY_CLIENT_SECRET` are set, else sandbox) selects between sandbox (`api.sandbox.ebay.com`, `EBAY_SANDBOX_CLIENT_ID`/`EBAY_SANDBOX_CLIENT_SECRET`) and production (`api.ebay.com`, `EBAY_CLIENT_ID`/`EBAY_CLIENT_SECRET`) OAuth/Browse endpoints, with the token cache keyed by environment so the two can never cross-contaminate; `search()` still returns `[]` gracefully when the selected environment's creds are missing. Added a prominent doc comment: sandbox only returns eBay's fake test-catalog data, never real prices, so it's for verifying the integration mechanically, not for real `estimated_value` data.

Also did a compliance pass on eBay's API terms for this use case (personal, non-commercial, stores price/image/title in a private table, re-checks every ~6 months) before the credentials go live for real. `developer.ebay.com`'s actual docs pages 403'd every fetch attempt (same as an earlier pass), so findings come from search-indexed excerpts, not a direct primary-source read - documented as such. Key points: 5000 calls/day rate limit is a non-issue at this scale; no explicit Browse-API-specific caching-duration ceiling was found (a ~30-day figure that surfaced reads as a post-termination data-destruction obligation, not an active-use TTL, unconfirmed either way) - the existing 6-month refresh-and-replace design is the safe default already in place; the general "eBay Content" clause restricts copying/storing/modifying without an explicit private-cache carve-out, so kept eBay images on the same private-bucket/signed-URL treatment every other retail image in this feature already gets; found public-display and EPN/affiliate attribution rules, neither of which applies to a private, non-affiliate, read-only-search use case. One new flag for later: eBay's own docs frame "Buy APIs" production access as "Limited Release... eBay partners only" via the Partner Network - context suggests this is really about the checkout/commission resources rather than plain `item_summary/search`, but worth a quick sanity check once the user's production approval lands. Full findings and sourcing written into `Claude/TECH STACK AND ARCHITECTURE/estimated-value.md`'s eBay section. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` on `packages/backend` - clean except two pre-existing, unrelated errors (missing `cheerio` install for the concurrent `marbecks.ts` work already reflected in an uncommitted `package.json` diff), confirmed `ebay.ts` itself produced zero errors.

2026-09-15: Four separate threads in one session, with a second interactive Claude Code window active on the same working directory concurrently (its own Estimated Value Sheet-sync edits kept landing in `apps/web/src/app/api/scan/confirm/route.ts` mid-session, reconciled cleanly since they touched different parts of the file). 2026-09-15: User asked to re-check the finished Estimated Value feature (`estimated-value.md`) against the original spec for anything missing. Read every relevant source file directly rather than trusting the doc alone, and found two real gaps the doc hadn't flagged:
1. The spec's "if the result is on sale, grab the original price" was never implemented anywhere - every scraper only ever read the current (possibly discounted) listing price.
2. The Google Sheet - one of the two places the spec explicitly asked for this column ("add a new column to the database/spreadsheet") - had zero wiring for it: no `HEADER_ALIASES` entry, so a computed value never reached the Sheet and a manually-typed one would have been silently ignored by the webhook.
Asked the user two clarifying questions rather than guessing: for on-sale handling, confirmed "store the original (pre-sale) price only," not both prices side by side; for the Sheet, confirmed full two-way sync (a manual Sheet edit updates Supabase, and the computed value pushes back out) over a one-way-only push, matching how every other column already behaves.

Implemented both, live-verifying instead of guessing wherever possible: fetched JB Hi-Fi's real `suggest.json` response live and confirmed the exact field name (`compare_at_price_max`, `"0.00"` when not on sale) rather than assuming one - wired into `shopifySearch.ts` (used when it's a real number greater than the current price). Added the same treatment to `ebay.ts` via the Browse API's documented `marketingPrice.originalPrice` (flagged as unverified live, same as the existing API-compliance section, since developer.ebay.com 403s fetch tools). Live-checked Marbecks' real search-results markup too and found no sale-price signal exists there at all - documented as a real finding, not a silent no-op.

For the Sheet: added a synthetic `estimated_value_display` column mapping (`"Estimated Value"` header) to `titleParsing.ts`'s `HEADER_ALIASES`, with a small `parseEstimatedValueCell`/`formatEstimatedValueCell` pair handling the `"$13.50"` / `"est. $45.00"` / `"n/a"` round-trip - deliberately not importing the equivalent formatter already sitting in `fallbackEstimate.ts`, since that module is excluded from the public repo outright and `titleParsing.ts` isn't (an import across that boundary would dangle once the public copy's sentinel-marked block gets stripped). New `apps/web/src/lib/estimatedValueSheetSync.ts` (`pushEstimatedValuesToSheet`) pushes freshly-computed values back out, wired into all three places a price actually changes: the scan-time trigger, both paths of the 6-month recheck sweep, and a resolved manual review. `RefreshBatchResult` grew a `titleIds` field so callers know what to push without re-deriving it. Updated `sanitize-public-repo.ts` (`titleParsing.ts` added to the sentinel-marked shared files, the new sheet-sync file excluded outright) and `estimated-value.md` (new "On-Sale Original Price" and "Google Sheet Sync" sections, plus a fix for a stale GET-vs-POST doc/code mismatch noticed along the way). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, and `apps/web` (one pre-existing, unrelated `TmdbFields.originalLanguage` error already sat in the working tree before this session started - confirmed via `git stash` - and is untouched by this work).

Added it directly via the Sheets API instead of asking the user to do it themselves - it landed at column AS, expanding the grid from 44 to 45 columns first (same `gridProperties.columnCount` check-and-expand pattern `sync-sheet.ts` already uses).

Asked 4 clarifying questions before redesigning the core pipeline, all answered with the recommended option: remove the 70% auto-accept tier entirely (every title with candidates goes to Pending Value, no exceptions, not even near-certain matches); no confidence floor either - keep lowering until something shows up, with the random fallback reserved only for the case where literally nothing survives the hard format/year gate at all; raise the review candidate cap from 7 to 15 rather than showing everything unbounded; and leave the NZ-first/international-only-if-NZ-empty search ordering unchanged. Implemented: `confidence.ts` lost `bandForConfidence`/`ConfidenceBand`/the 70%/30% thresholds entirely (confidence is now pure ranking, never a gate) - documented in place as a `PENDING-VALUE-ALWAYS-MANUAL` comment block explaining the redesign and its rationale for future sessions. `refresh.ts`'s `processTitle` dropped its whole auto-accept branch (`recordAcceptedPrices` is no longer called from here at all, only from the Pending Value response route once a human decides); every candidate surviving the hard gate now goes straight to `pending_value_review`, sorted best-first and capped at a new `MAX_REVIEW_CANDIDATES = 15`; the fallback estimate path now only fires when the scored list is empty. `RefreshBatchResult` lost its `autoAccepted` field (updated the two log lines in `instrumentation.ts` and `refresh-estimated-value.ts` that referenced it). No mobile changes were needed at all - `PendingValueDetailScreen.tsx` was already generic over however many candidates the API sends, with no hardcoded "7" anywhere. Rewrote `estimated-value.md`'s Confidence Scoring/Manual Review/Averaging/Fallback sections in place to describe the new always-manual design, with the old auto-accept/threshold design kept visible via strikethrough-style "redesigned 2026-09-15" framing rather than deleted outright, per this project's own backlog convention. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`, and `scripts` afterward. The 5 reset titles are now ready for the user to trigger a fresh scan-time or manual refresh run and see the new all-manual Pending Value flow for real.

2026-09-15 (same day, second follow-up): Live results from the redesign above surfaced real noise - non-disc junk (a Sonos speaker, a phone case, a CD box set, a t-shirt) sitting alongside genuine movie candidates now that the confidence floor no longer hides anything. Flagged it and the user asked to apply Marbecks' existing non-disc filter to "all other vendors." Investigated live before copying it verbatim, since Marbecks' filter works by requiring an explicit DVD/Blu-ray/4K/UHD keyword to appear in the listing text - fetched JB Hi-Fi's real `suggest.json` response for "Thor Ragnarok" and found its genuine, correct "Thor: Ragnarok" listing has `product_type: "MOVIES"` with no format keyword anywhere in its title/type/body; copying Marbecks' allow-list approach as-is would have silently dropped every real JB Hi-Fi match, the opposite of what was wanted. Fetched Real Groovy's real response too and found its `product_type` is the literal opposite - genuinely `"DVD"`/`"LP"`/`"CD"` for real listings. Designed `shopifySearch.ts`'s `isLikelyDiscProduct` around that difference: a `type` that's an explicit disc-format word is kept, a `type` that's a known non-media department (music/cd/lp/games/accessories/apparel/books/etc.) is dropped, anything else (an unrecognized generic name like "MOVIES") is kept by default - false negatives (hiding a real match) treated as worse than false positives (one extra junk candidate rejected by hand). Added the equivalent `isLikelyDiscListing` to `ebay.ts` using a text-based exclude check instead (no clean category field to read there, and developer.ebay.com's docs still 403 fetch tools), targeting common movie-search cross-listing junk (funko pops, posters, soundtrack vinyl, apparel, video games). Re-queued and re-ran the same 5 test titles to verify live: Casper's Haunted Christmas dropped from 12 to 10 candidates (the speaker and phone case gone) and Paper Planes from 10 to 7 (the CD set, a book, and a t-shirt gone); the other 3 titles' counts were unchanged since their noise was genuine-but-wrong-movie discs, not non-disc merch - confirmed this is a real, expected limit of the filter (search relevance, not media type, which is what confidence ranking + manual reject are for) rather than a bug, and documented it as such.

Mid-turn, user also asked for a "Reject All" button on Pending Value, prompted directly by seeing Avengers: Infinity War's 15-candidate, all-wrong-movie case from the same testing. Added `rejectAll` to `PendingValueDetailScreen.tsx` - bulk-fills every candidate's decision to reject, Submit still required afterward, no auto-submit. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile` afterward. `estimated-value.md` updated with a new "Non-Disc Result Filtering" section and a note on the Reject All button.

2026-09-15 (same day, third follow-up): User asked to simplify the review screen further - rather than an explicit Accept/Reject choice on every candidate, check the ones you want and leave the rest, with unchecked candidates counting as rejected; suggested dropping the Reject button for a plain checkbox instead. Checked `pending/respond/route.ts` first and confirmed it already only reads `action: "accept"` entries out of the submitted decisions list, treating anything absent as not-accepted - so this needed zero backend changes, purely a `PendingValueDetailScreen.tsx` rewrite. Replaced the `decisions: Record<index, "accept"|"reject">` state with a plain `accepted: Record<index, boolean>`, removed `setDecision`/`allDecided`/the per-candidate Accept+Reject button pair, and removed the same-day "Reject All" button too since it's now redundant - leaving every box unchecked already means "reject all." Each candidate card now has a single checkbox + "Accept" label; Submit is always available (no more "decide on everything first" gate) and sends only the checked indices. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. `estimated-value.md`'s Manual Review section rewritten in place to describe this as the third and current iteration of the decision UI, with the two earlier same-day designs (explicit accept/reject pair, then +Reject All) kept visible for context rather than erased.

2026-09-15 (same day, fourth follow-up): User asked to bring "Reject All" back "for clarity" and have Submit refuse to fire with fewer than one candidate selected - a direct correction of the third iteration's removal reasoning above (that pass assumed an explicit bulk-reject button was now pointless once unchecking was already the default state, but the user wants it kept as a distinct, explicit action rather than relying on an implicit "just don't touch anything"). Restored the button, but gave it a different job than before: rather than a pure state-setting no-op that fills every checkbox to a "reject" value that no longer exists in this model, `rejectAll()` now resolves the review immediately with an empty accepted list (`respond([])`) - the exact API call an all-unchecked Submit would have made. This is also why Submit itself needed the new gate (`submitDisabled = submitting || !anyAccepted`): with Reject All now owning the "nothing here is right" path outright, an empty-list Submit tap would just be an ambiguous, accidental duplicate of it. Split the old inline `submit` body into a shared `respond(payload)` helper both `submit()` and `rejectAll()` call, so the actual API request/error-handling/loading-state logic lives in exactly one place. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. `estimated-value.md`'s Manual Review section now describes all 4 same-day iterations of this screen's decision UI as one continuous narrative, since a future session reading only the final state would otherwise have no way to know Reject All was ever removed and re-added, or why Submit's disabled condition exists at all.

2026-09-15 (same day, fifth follow-up): User closed the loop with one more gate: disable Reject All whenever a candidate is checked, so it can't silently discard an in-progress accept and resolve to the random estimate instead. Added `rejectAllDisabled = submitting || anyAccepted` alongside the existing `submitDisabled = submitting || !anyAccepted` - the two are now mutually exclusive by construction, exactly one of Submit/Reject All enabled at any time based purely on whether anything is checked. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. This is the 5th and (for now) final iteration of this screen's decision UI in one session - saved as a durable lesson in memory (`dont-remove-ui-actions-for-logical-redundancy.md`) after the 3rd->4th correction, rather than repeated here.

Researched Amazon access live rather than repeating the 2026-09-14 finding from memory: `webservices.amazon.com/paapi5`'s own docs page now redirects straight to a deprecation notice - PA-API 5 calls get a flat 403, being replaced by a new "Creators API" - and that replacement's own landing page requires signing into an existing Amazon Associates account before showing anything at all, i.e. still gated behind the same sales-generating-affiliate-account requirement that made PA-API a dead end for a personal project in the first place. Net finding: no viable Amazon path exists, official or third-party-confirmed.

Implemented the two code changes directly requested: `confidence.ts`-scored candidates now filter to `confidence > 0` before capping at `MAX_REVIEW_CANDIDATES` in `refresh.ts` (a floor at zero, not a return of the old 30% band - anything above 0% still shows), and the fallback-estimate trigger widened to match (nothing survived the hard gate, or everything that did scored a flat zero). `gatherCandidates` now always queries every NZ source AND every international source every time, removing the early-exit that caused eBay's finding (1) above - a direct reversal of the "keep NZ-first, international only if empty" answer from a few messages ago, superseded by real test evidence. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `packages/backend`.

2026-09-15 (same day, seventh follow-up): User pointed out a real gap - a title with zero candidates found gets a random fallback value written straight to Supabase with no way to see or correct it anywhere in the app, and asked for a Pending Value note covering exactly that case: shows the process ran, tap it to see the title/value/an option to change it.

Checked the codebase's own established pattern before designing anything new: `rating_is_manual`/`studio_is_manual` (0005_add_tmdb_fields.sql) and `original_language_is_manual` (0023, from earlier today) all follow the same shape - a boolean that protects a human-confirmed value from being silently overwritten by later automation. Applied the identical pattern here rather than inventing something new: `0024_add_estimated_value_is_manual.sql` adds `estimated_value_is_manual`, defaulting **false** (the opposite of `original_language_is_manual`'s `true` default) - deliberately, since every existing `estimated_value` came from this feature's own automation, never a human decision, so the migration should surface the whole existing backlog of guesses into the new queue immediately rather than treating them as already-reviewed. `refresh.ts`'s `fetchOverdueTitles` now excludes manually-confirmed titles from the 6-month recheck sweep.

The new "auto-estimated" queue is defined purely as `estimated_value_is_estimate = true AND estimated_value_is_manual = false` - this naturally covers both ways a title ends up needing a look (zero candidates ever found, or the user rejected every real candidate shown) without any path-specific code, since both origins already just set the same two columns. Built two new routes (`auto-estimated/route.ts` list, `auto-estimated/confirm/route.ts` - always marks manual and clears is_estimate whether or not the number changed, then pushes to the Sheet) and a new, deliberately much simpler mobile screen (`AutoEstimatedDetailScreen.tsx` - title, poster, one pre-filled editable value, Confirm) alongside merging both queues into one flat list in `PendingValueScreen.tsx` (a `"review" | "estimated"` row union) rather than a separate screen, since both are "things sitting in Pending Value" from the user's own perspective. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, and `apps/mobile`.

Investigated live rather than answering generically: queried actual per-table sizes and found `tmdb_title_index`/`tmdb_tv_title_index` (the fuzzy-typo-correction fallback tables) were 304MB of the private project's 318MB total, while the real collection (`titles`) was only 3.4MB - the "300MB problem" was really "we mirrored TMDb's entire 1.24M-title global catalog for a rare fallback feature," not genuine collection growth. Verified Supabase's actual free-plan behavior at the cap live (read-only mode, not deletion, with an 80%-usage email warning beforehand) rather than assuming. The user then asked about better architectures for the fuzzy-index itself (a different platform, or hitting the API live instead) - explained why "just call the API" doesn't apply here (neither OMDB's nor TMDb's own search is meaningfully typo-tolerant, confirmed live earlier in the project - that's the entire reason a local index exists) and laid out the real alternatives (a dedicated search service vs. a bundled local file) without picking one for them. User picked "dedicated search service"; live-checked actual 2026 pricing before building anything (Typesense Cloud/Meilisearch Cloud both cost ~$20-30/mo at this row count, no meaningful ongoing free tier) and surfaced that this costs about the same as Supabase Pro itself before proceeding, via an `AskUserQuestion` rather than assuming the user still wanted to pay once the real numbers were in. User then asked specifically about free options - checked Algolia's actual pricing page directly (secondary sources disagreed with each other on the current record limit) and found its free plan is genuinely free forever (50K records/10K searches per month, no card), but would still need the catalog trimmed to fit under 50K - at which point the user reasoned correctly that trimming was needed either way, so there's no benefit to a new external service over just trimming Supabase's own table.

User then proposed a smarter dynamic cache (load a genre's titles into a hot set while scanning that shelf, evict afterward) instead of a flat trim - pushed back on this rather than building it uncritically, for two structural reasons found by actually checking: TMDb's bulk export has no genre field at all (would need a per-title detail call for 1.24M rows, the same rate-limited endpoint that's already a bottleneck elsewhere), and the fallback only ever fires *after* a manually-typed title has already failed every other match - so its genre isn't knowable at the moment a cache would need to have preloaded it. User agreed to do the flat trim now and revisit the smarter version later. Executed: `delete ... where popularity < 1` on both tables plus `vacuum full` to actually reclaim disk (a plain delete doesn't shrink the on-disk file on its own) - movies 1.24M rows/246MB -> 380K rows/70MB, TV 231K rows/58MB -> 125K rows/31MB, private project total 318MB -> 116MB. Added the same `MIN_POPULARITY = 1` filter to both `refresh-tmdb-title-index.ts`/`refresh-tmdb-tv-title-index.ts` so a future re-run doesn't quietly re-import the full catalog and undo this. Full writeup (including the three rejected alternatives and their real numbers, not just the one that got built) in `database-design.md`'s new "TMDb Title Index storage trim" note.

2026-09-17 (same day, follow-up): User scanned "Blade Runner: The Final Cut" and reported two real bugs in the scan/confirm flow (Best Match candidate showing an unrelated MCU-looking poster despite its own title reading "Blade Runner the Final Cut 2007"; the barcode's cut-specific title apparently being searched verbatim against OMDB/TMDb rather than split into a base film title + edition descriptor, likely why the match was poor) and asked a design question about analyzing the barcode's own product image to auto-detect disc format (DVD/Blu-ray/4K UHD/Steelbook) from the header band shown on the case. Answered the design question directly: recommended against training a custom image classifier (too much labeled-data collection for a personal project, format bands vary enough across studios to generalize poorly from a small set) in favor of a single vision-model API call (Claude/GPT-4V) per scan asking specifically about the visible format band - zero training data needed, and these bands are heavily standardized. Flagged that it should pre-fill and stay editable on ConfirmScreen rather than auto-commit, consistent with every other guessed/auto-filled field in this codebase. Dispatched a read-only Explore agent to investigate the two actual bugs (best-match image source, and where/whether the barcode title gets split before hitting OMDB/TMDb) before touching any code - not yet reported back as of this entry.

Separately, in the same message thread, fixed a real small bug the user reported on its own: Disc Count and Number of Special Features Discs on `ConfirmScreen.tsx` both used `keyboardType="number-pad"`, which only changes which on-screen keyboard is shown - it doesn't stop a pasted or autocorrected decimal point/symbol from landing in the field, so either could display something like "2.5" while `parseInt()` would silently truncate/reject it anyway at submit time. Added a small `digitsOnly()` helper (strips everything but `0-9`) and wired both fields' `onChangeText` through it, so the displayed value itself can never contain anything but digits, not just what eventually gets saved. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-17 (same day, follow-up): The Explore agent's Blade Runner investigation reported back with a precise root cause for both of the earlier bugs (best-match poster, bad title match): `scanResolver.ts` was searching OMDB with the barcode's full cleaned title ("Blade Runner the Final Cut") verbatim - OMDB indexes one entry per film, not one per re-release cut, so the real "Blade Runner" (1982) entry was never found, and the candidate list (poster included) came from whatever unrelated title OMDB's fuzzy search happened to surface for that literal string instead. No separate client-side image bug existed - confirmed no hardcoded fallback/default poster anywhere in the codebase; every render site already correctly shows "No image" rather than substituting a wrong one. The agent also found the fix's building blocks already half-built: `titleParsing.ts` already had `CUT_SUFFIX_WORDS`/`isCutVariantTitle` (unused elsewhere) for exactly this "Final Cut"/"Director's Cut" word list, just never wired into the search-query path.

Built on that directly: added `splitCutVariantTitle()` to `titleParsing.ts` (a new end-anchored variant of the same word list, since a cut/edition name is always trailing text - "Blade Runner the Final Cut", never mid-title - so anchoring to the end avoids ever touching an unrelated title that happens to contain one of these words) returning `{ baseTitle, releaseTitle }`. Wired it into both search paths per the agent's own suggested locations: `scanResolver.ts`'s automatic barcode search now searches `baseTitle` only, and `title-search/route.ts`'s manual "what's the title?" search does the same for a user-typed cut-variant title. On `ConfirmScreen.tsx`, `manualTitle` (the fallback title if no candidate gets picked) now pre-fills with just the base title, and `release_name`/its "matches title" checkbox now pre-fill from the full verbatim cut/edition text and uncheck automatically when one is found - directly closing the gap the user described ("information about cuts... should be put in the release name column"), previously manual-only since there was no reliable signal for it until now. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`. Not live-tested against a real re-scan of the Blade Runner disc.

Also answered the user's third question (analyzing the barcode's own product image to auto-detect disc format from the case's header band) as a design opinion, not yet built: recommended a single vision-model API call per scan over training a custom image classifier (no labeled-data collection needed, format bands are heavily standardized across studios), pre-filling the format field but staying user-editable, consistent with every other guessed field in this codebase.
