2026-09-17 (same day, follow-up): The eBay research agent reported back - recommended Gemini as the strongest genuinely-free option (no card, official TS SDK, strong at reading printed text/logos - a good match for reading a printed format banner), Groq as a free-but-"preview"-labelled backup, and OpenAI/OpenRouter/Cloudflare all ruled out for various reasons (no real ongoing free tier, a rotating free model lineup, or an unproven fit for custom JSON prompts respectively). The user picked Gemini and provided a real API key directly in chat.

Verified the key live before building anything, rather than trusting its format - it doesn't look like a standard `AIzaSy...` Google API key (starts `AQ.` instead), which given this session's earlier wrong Cert-ID-format guess with eBay was reason enough to actually test it instead of flagging a shape mismatch again. `curl`'d Gemini's own `/models` list endpoint directly: it's real and valid, and exposed a full model catalog running well past this assistant's own January 2026 knowledge cutoff (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.8-flash`, etc.) - picked `gemini-flash-lite-latest`, Google's own always-current alias for its lightest/highest-free-quota tier, matching the user's explicit request for "a model that will give us the amount of prompts that we will need" over the most capable one. Live-tested the full technical mechanism end-to-end via more `curl` calls (a real JPEG, base64-inlined, sent with `generationConfig.responseMimeType: "application/json"` + a `responseSchema`) before writing any code - confirmed this returns clean, unfenced JSON (a plain-text-prompt version of the same test came back wrapped in a ```json code fence instead, which would have needed extra unwrapping).

Built `packages/backend/src/formatVision.ts` (`detectFormatFromImage`), using Gemini's plain REST `generateContent` endpoint via `fetch` - no `@google/genai` dependency added, matching the same "plain fetch over an SDK" convention every other external API in this codebase already follows (ebay.ts, omdb.ts, tmdb.ts). Wired into `scanResolver.ts` as strictly the LAST resort: it only ever runs when `extractFormatHint` (the existing text-based guess) already found nothing, per the user's own explicit instruction that text is always more reliable than a model guessing from a photo - added a `visionFormatGuess` field to `resolved_candidates` for this. On `ConfirmScreen.tsx`, `format` and `steelbook` now fall back to this guess (after draft/existingMatch/text-hint, before the plain "DVD" default) - still a plain editable field either way, no different from any other guessed value already in this screen. Confirmed directly with the user this doesn't need a custom training dataset - a pretrained vision-language model already has ample exposure to standardized retail packaging, so the real lever is prompt wording (describing what each banner looks like), not collecting images.

Mid-build, the user added two more real details about multi-disc banners from direct experience: a 4K UHD banner sometimes also says "+ BLU-RAY" (a second, Blu-ray special-features disc) or "+ BLU-RAY + BONUS DISC" (a third disc, also assumed Blu-ray, never seen otherwise) - and separately, a plain Blu-ray banner can say "+ DVD" for its own bonus disc (a different assumed format than the 4K UHD case, not the same one). Deliberately kept the "what format is an unlabeled bonus disc" assumption out of the model prompt entirely - the model's `extraDiscs` field only ever reports how many extra items the banner lists ("NONE"/"ONE_EXTRA"/"TWO_EXTRA"), never what they are, and a new `deriveDiscConfigFromExtraDiscs` function in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` applies the user's stated real-world rule (Blu-ray alongside a 4K UHD primary, DVD alongside a Blu-ray primary) as plain, auditable app code - easy to correct or extend later if a different real combination ever turns up, rather than needing to reverse-engineer an opaque prompt. This pre-fills disc count, Special Features, special-features disc count, and special-features disc format together whenever a multi-disc banner is detected - all still plain editable fields, per the user's own explicit instruction ("fill out the entries in the form but allow them to be editable by the user in case of errors").

Added `GEMINI_API_KEY` to `scripts/.env.example` (documented), `scripts/.env`, and `apps/web/.env.local` (the real key, gitignored, never committed) - not a private-project-only feature (unlike Estimated Value), so no public-repo exclusion needed, same as OMDB/TMDb's existing keys. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/backend`, `apps/mobile`, `apps/web`. Not live-tested against a real barcode-scanned disc photo yet - the generic image+JSON mechanism was proven live via curl, but real accuracy on an actual disc case (and the multi-disc banner logic specifically) will only be confirmed the next time a barcode scan actually reaches this code path.

User then pointed out the real banner actually reads "4K UHD + Blu-Ray + Digital Download," and a digital download code isn't a physical disc - `extraDiscs` should have been `ONE_EXTRA`, not `TWO_EXTRA`. This ruled out a deployment/staleness problem for the first time all session and pointed to genuine model non-determinism instead - Gemini's default sampling temperature. Added `temperature: 0` to the request's `generationConfig`, restarted once more, and got a clean, correct `ONE_EXTRA` through the real app pipeline this time.

Also downloading the real image directly disproved the assumption behind the user's separate "big grey borders on the left and right" report - the source photo itself has no padding at all (a clean full-bleed portrait cover). Replaced the "Your scanned item" image's style with a new `scannedItemImage` using `aspectRatio: 2/3` (a typical disc-case proportion) instead of a fixed height, and switched both it and "Existing entry"'s image to the same `resizeMode` convention already used everywhere else in this file. 2026-09-18: Two more requests. (1) On the similar-entry (Overwrite/Is-a-new-entry/Reject) screen, show each candidate's `release_name` alongside its title, since two rows sharing the same base title are often actually different releases (a plain DVD vs. a Special Edition) that the title text alone can't distinguish. Added `release_name` to `find-existing/route.ts`'s `SELECT_COLUMNS`/`ExistingTitleRow`, the mobile-side `ExistingTitleCandidate` type, and all three render spots in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` (the single-match banner, the multi-candidate picker list, and the chosen-candidate detail block) plus the one other place in the same file that manually constructs an `ExistingTitleCandidate` object (the exact-barcode-match fast path) - `tsc --noEmit` caught that second spot immediately as a missing-property error, confirming it needed the same fix.

(2) The user scanned "The Day the Earth Stood Still" and found no Rotten Tomatoes link saved despite RT genuinely having a page/score for it - asked to investigate and prevent recurrence. Root-caused directly: `rottenTomatoes.ts` guesses a URL slug from the title text (no real RT search is available - their robots.txt disallows `/search`, and this project has never scraped against a site's own robots.txt) and only accepts the guess if the resulting page's JSON-LD matches both title and score. For this film, RT's real slug is `1005371-day_the_earth_stood_still` (a numeric-ID prefix) - confirmed live via `curl` that the guessed slug 404s, and found two much older Sheet-imported rows for the same 1951 film that already carry the correct link (evidently typed in by hand, long before this automation existed). This is a real, structural limitation of guess-from-title-text, not a one-off bug - the code behaved exactly as designed ("wrong link is worse than no link").

Built `scripts/src/find-missing-rotten-tomatoes.ts` (read-only audit, checks every title with no saved RT link against OMDB's own `Ratings` array) to surface this gap for manual review going forward, since no better automated fix exists within this project's scraping-ethics constraints. Ran it for real against the live collection: **47 titles** have a confirmed Rotten Tomatoes score but no saved link - a real, ongoing gap across the whole collection (old Sheet-import-era rows and freshly-scanned ones alike), not an isolated case, as initially suspected before running the audit. Documented the full finding, root cause, and the "run this occasionally, paste the correct URL into the Sheet by hand" workflow in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`. Gave the user the one already-known correct URL for "The Day the Earth Stood Still" directly rather than writing it into the Sheet myself, consistent with treating the Sheet as the user's own document to edit. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `scripts`.

2026-09-18 (same day, follow-up): User asked to log the Rotten Tomatoes link-gap as a known bug to fix later rather than build a real fix now - added to `Claude/To Do list.md`'s BACKLOG with the audit script command and a pointer to the full write-up. Also asked to be walked through the earlier-proposed Supabase case-image plan - explained it, and the user corrected a real misunderstanding in it: they want BOTH poster images (OMDB/TMDb, for future Movie/TV Pages) and product/case images (barcode scans or accepted Estimated Value candidates, for future DVD Pages) kept side by side, not one replacing the other - pointed to `WEB APP DESIGN.md`'s own DVD Pages section, which already documents this exact split. The "priority" they actually meant was narrower than my first draft assumed: only between the two SOURCES of the case image (barcode scan vs. Estimated Value candidate), never between the case image and the poster.

Revised and built the corrected plan: `0026_add_case_image_path.sql` adds `titles.case_image_path` (a Storage path, same convention as `poster_image_path`) and a new `case-images` bucket, kept deliberately separate from Estimated Value's own `retail-product-images` bucket since that one lives entirely inside the private-only `estimatedValue/` directory excluded from the public repo, while this needs to work in both builds. New general-purpose `caseImageStorage.ts` (fetch-and-upload, signed URLs) mirrors `estimatedValue/storage.ts`'s shape without depending on it. Two write paths feed the same column with priority expressed as a plain "don't overwrite if already set" guard, the same idempotent pattern `poster_image_path` itself already uses - no comparison logic needed: `scan/confirm/route.ts` writes unconditionally on every confirm (a fresh photo of the real physical copy always wins), while `pending/respond/route.ts`'s candidate-acceptance path only writes when `case_image_path` is still null, i.e. only ever reaches a title with no barcode photo at all (typically pre-barcode-pipeline). The accepted-candidate path is a genuine cross-bucket copy (resolve a signed URL against `retail-product-images`, fetch through it, re-upload into `case-images`), since Storage's own `copy()` only works within one bucket. Added `case_image_path` to the shared `Title` type and to `titleParsing.ts`'s "no Sheet column" list (a Storage path is DB-only, same as `poster_image_path`, never something the user edits by hand in the Sheet). `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`.

User then provided a fresh Supabase personal access token and confirmed they wanted this integrated for real. Applied the migration to BOTH live projects via the Management API (not private-only, unlike 0021/0024/0025) - token used only inline in three curl calls, never persisted. Verified genuinely live on both (not just trusting the empty-array success response) via a direct `information_schema.columns`/`storage.buckets` query against each project - confirmed the column and bucket both exist on the private (`lpbsypfwhjluwuosrgnd`) and public (`azfpstsvngohgktjilms`) projects alike.

Also fixed a real regression the user caught: the earlier "fix" for the Blade Runner scanned-item image's grey borders (a hardcoded `aspectRatio: 2/3`, replacing an even earlier hardcoded fixed height) just moved the same letterboxing problem to the top/bottom instead of the left/right, since 2:3 isn't actually this photo's real shape either. Replaced both guesses with `Image.getSize()` reading the photo's own real dimensions and setting the box's aspect ratio to match exactly - the only approach that can't be wrong for a given image, since it isn't a guess at all. 2:3 remains only as the initial/fallback value before the real size loads. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-18 (same day, follow-up): The user found a Supabase Security Advisor warning ("RLS Disabled in Public," critical) for `amazon_provider_quota` - a real gap this session caused: `0025_add_amazon_provider_quota.sql` created the table without ever enabling Row Level Security, unlike every other table in this project. Fixed with `0027_enable_rls_amazon_provider_quota.sql` - `enable row level security` with deliberately NO read/write policies added, unlike `title_price_observations`/`pending_value_review` (which do get a public-read policy for a future public-facing web app): this table is pure internal RapidAPI-quota bookkeeping with no legitimate anon/authenticated use ever, so a full lockdown (service role still bypasses RLS regardless, anon/authenticated get denied by default with zero policies) is the correct shape here, not the public-read pattern the other Estimated Value tables use. Applied to the private project only (confirmed the table doesn't exist on the public project at all, via `to_regclass`, since 0025 itself was never applied there) and verified genuinely enabled afterward via `pg_class.relrowsecurity`, not just trusting the success response. Added to `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s exclusion list alongside 0025. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `scripts`.

2026-09-18 (same day, follow-up): The user reported a real consequence of yesterday's mislabeled-listing find: after submitting the Blade Runner scan, the similar-entry (Overwrite/New Entry/Reject) screen showed two candidates, both format "DVD" - a box-set member and what they suspected might secretly be the 4K version. Since neither looked like a 4K match, they chose "Is a new entry," then later confirmed via the Sheet that the "DVD" one really was the 4K UHD copy - creating an accidental duplicate.

Root-caused with real data, not just theory: queried every Blade Runner row directly and confirmed the real 4K duplicate (`7b43234c...`) genuinely has `format = "4K UHD Blu-Ray"` in the database (not "DVD" at all) and shares the same real `imdb_page` (tt0083658) as the newly-created row - meaning it DID match via the shared-imdb-id path in `find-existing/route.ts`, but was then filtered back OUT by the route's own format-hint narrowing step. That step re-derives `formatHint` via `extractFormatHint(upcText)` from the raw barcode listing text server-side - and this exact listing's own title text says "...Dvd" (the same mislabeling already found and worked around for the vision-detection feature), so the narrowing kept only the box-set's genuinely-DVD member rows and silently hid the one candidate that actually mattered. ConfirmScreen had already correctly resolved the real format via the vision fallback by this point, but that corrected value was never passed to this endpoint at all - the client only ever sent the raw upcText, and the server re-derived its own (wrong) guess from it independently, disconnected from what the screen was actually showing the user.

Fixed by passing the client's own already-resolved `format`/`discCount` state through as explicit `formatOverride`/`discCountOverride` params (`scanApi.ts`'s `findExistingTitle`, `find-existing/route.ts`), preferred over re-deriving from raw text - falling back to the old text-derived hint only when a client doesn't send an override at all. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/web`/`apps/mobile`. Did not touch the two now-duplicated Blade Runner rows themselves (`103eeec2...` and `7b43234c...`) - flagged to the user as a real cleanup step but left for them to decide/action, consistent with treating collection data edits as theirs to make.

2026-09-18 (same day, follow-up, continuing after the session limit was hit): Picked up exactly where the previous session left off. (1) Logged the "scan the cover" idea to `Claude/To Do list.md`'s BACKLOG as explicitly deferred until after Collection/TV series integration, per the user's own sequencing - captured their clarification that scanning the cover and scanning the barcode are independent, not mutually exclusive (either or both), and the concrete field list they expect from a single cover photo alone (title, format, disc count, edition, product image, special-features disc format via 4K UHD-style headers, franchise, an estimated price if a tag is visibly still attached, and rating). Gave the camera-recognition opinion itself in the previous message (favorable - the Gemini format-vision work already proved the core approach, main cost is UX/framing testing, not technical risk).

(2) Built the full offline-mode feature the user spec'd out across several messages: a persistent warning banner (`OfflineBanner.tsx`, on ScannerScreen and both ConfirmScreen states) shown whenever there's no real internet access - deliberately checking `NetInfo`'s `isInternetReachable`, not just whether a network interface is up, since the phone and this app's own LAN-hosted backend can stay reachable to each other even when the wider internet is down (`network.ts`). Confirm is now offline-aware at both points that need the network before the real write (the interactive similar-entry check, and the write itself) - offline, it skips straight to saving the fully-built submission on-device (`offlineQueue.ts`, AsyncStorage-backed) instead of failing with a network error, with a clear message that it hasn't been added to the database yet. An `App.tsx`-level listener fires the moment the connection comes back (`subscribeToReconnect`, added to `network.ts` - fires only on a genuine offline->online transition, never on an online->online network-type change like WiFi to cellular) and attempts every queued item for real: the exact same `findExistingTitle` check a live submission would have run, then either submits immediately (no match) or - per the user's explicit instruction - flags the item as `needs_review` with the match data attached rather than resolving it automatically, since an unattended background sync has no way to make that Overwrite/New Entry/Reject call itself. A new `OfflineQueueScreen.tsx` (reached via a badge-counted "Offline Queue (N)" button on Pending Scans, shown only when the count is nonzero) lists queued/needs_review/error items and lets the user make that deferred decision by hand, reusing `confirmScan` directly rather than routing back through ConfirmScreen's full form (all the field data was already captured at queue time). Installed `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`/`@react-native-community/netinfo` in the previous session; this one wrote all the actual logic. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`. Not yet tested against a real lost-connection scenario on a device - the mechanism is built and typechecks, but genuine offline/reconnect behavior (and AsyncStorage persisting across an app restart) has only been reasoned through, not observed live.
