2026-09-19: User's first real live TV scan (Doctor Who Season 21) surfaced four real bugs plus two feature requests in one message. Investigated and fixed all six:

1. **Running Time was wrongly auto-filled for a TV Series.** `/api/scan/confirm/route.ts` set `running_time_mins` from OMDb's `Runtime` unconditionally, never actually gated on `movie_or_tv` the way RESOURCES.md's own rule says it should be - the n/a-by-default TV Series rule was only ever enforced for a fully-manual entry's fields, never for a real OMDb-matched candidate. Fixed by resolving the final `movie_or_tv` (manual override, else OMDb's own guess) before deciding whether to keep the OMDb runtime, nulling it out only for `"TV Series"`.
2. **A TV title's poster resolved to a completely unrelated film's** (Doctor Who Season 21 showed The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers on Estimated Value). Root cause: `resolveTmdbCandidateById` (used by `resolvePosterUrl`) always called `/movie/{id}` regardless of media type - movie and TV ids are separate TMDb numbering spaces, so a TV id read as a movie id can coincidentally be a real unrelated movie. Fixed by threading the title's own `tmdb_media_type` through, branching to `/tv/{id}?append_to_response=external_ids` when appropriate.
3. **Genre "Adventure, Drama, Family" instead of Sci-Fi** - checked directly against OMDb (`tt0056751`, the 1963 series) and confirmed this is genuinely OMDb's own data for that entry, not a bug (the 2005 revival, `tt0436992`, does say Sci-Fi). Noted as a real gap though: there's currently no way to manually override Genre on a real OMDb-matched candidate at all, same restriction as Running Time.
4. **Candidate poster list scrolling was laggy** - a plain `ScrollView` mounted and decoded every candidate's poster image up front, including off-screen ones, and remote posters are usually much higher-resolution than their 120x168 display size. Swapped both candidate rows (main + Extras) to a horizontal `FlatList`, which virtualizes off-screen cells - no new dependency needed.
5. **Replaced `AutocompleteInput`/`TagAutocompleteInput` with new `SearchableModalInput`/`TagSearchableModalInput`** everywhere they were used (Format, Genre Location, Rating, Studio, Original Language, Rented By Who, Animation/Live Action, Genre, Franchise) - the user liked the new Type `SelectDropdown`'s full-screen feel and asked for the same treatment generally, calling out Genre/Franchise specifically. Same props/matching logic as before, but tapping the field now opens a full-screen modal with a live-filtering text input at the top instead of an inline dropdown; tapping an option fills the text without auto-closing (so several tags can be picked in one sitting), and a Done button or hiding the keyboard closes it. Deleted the two old components outright rather than leaving them unused.
6. **Built a UPC-lookup-quota progress bar for PendingScansScreen**, mirroring the existing Amazon-quota bar pattern: new `upc_provider_quota` table (`0029_add_upc_provider_quota.sql`, day-keyed, RLS enabled from the start this time), `upcQuota.ts`, wired into the one real call site of `upcLookup` (`resolvePendingScansBatch`). Unlike the Amazon quota, this is a core feature in both public and private builds.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across `backend`/`web`/`mobile`. Documented all six in `barcode-review-screen-fields.md`'s TV Scanning section. Still owed: applying migration 0029 to both Supabase projects (needs a fresh token from the user), a manual fix/rescan for the already-wrong Doctor Who Season 21 row (the runtime/poster fixes are forward-only), `Dick Tracy` (1937)'s episode_count, the deferred nested-serial logic, live device testing, and pushing to either GitHub repo.

2026-09-19 (same day, follow-up): user gave a fresh Supabase token and asked three more things in quick succession.

1. **"Does TMDb use genre data? Merge OMDb + TMDb genre so we get all the genre information"** - applied migration 0029 with the new token first. Before merging, queried the real collection's distinct genre values live (`select distinct unnest(genre)`) to check for naming conflicts - found the Genre column already carries substantial manual curation well beyond raw OMDb text (compound genres like "Sword and Sandal"/"Kaiju"/"Wuxia" no API ever supplies, plus real typos/casing drift), confirmed the user hadn't noticed this scope creep, and asked two clarifying questions before implementing: map TMDb's "Science Fiction" to the collection's own established "Sci-Fi", and exclude TMDb's "TV Movie" genre tag (not a real content genre) from the merge. Both confirmed. Built `mergeOmdbAndTmdbGenres` in the confirm route, plumbed `genres: string[]` through `fetchTmdbFieldsById`/`TmdbFields`/`lookupTmdbFields`.
2. The user's own follow-up on the "TV Movie" exclusion: "if a movie is given the TV Movie tag, maybe movie_or_tv should reflect that - but check you're not looking at the wrong Doctor Who first." Verified live rather than assumed: looked up both the classic show (`tt0056751` -> TMDb tv/121, genres include "Sci-Fi & Fantasy") and the actual 1996 TV movie (`tt0116118` -> TMDb movie/15691, genres include "TV Movie") directly - confirmed TMDb's `/find` always resolves via the exact candidate's own imdbID, so there's no cross-contamination risk, and "TV Movie" only ever appears on TMDb's *movie* genre list, never its TV one. Used this to sharpen (never force) the movie_or_tv guess: a second reactive effect in ConfirmScreen bumps "Movie" to "TV Movie" once the TMDb preview arrives with that tag, same backoff-once-edited guard as every other reactive guess on the screen.
3. **UPC quota bug report**: "why does the progress bar say 0/100 when I scanned 3+ titles today?" - root cause was inherent to the self-tracked-counter design shipped minutes earlier: the table didn't exist until this session, so it could never know about earlier calls. Checked UPCitemdb's actual response headers live (`curl -D -`) rather than assuming, and found it genuinely exposes real `X-RateLimit-*` headers (unlike the RapidAPI Amazon providers, confirmed NOT to have this). Redesigned the whole feature same-day: `upcLookup` now returns `{ product, rateLimit }`, and a single-row `upc_quota_status` table just mirrors UPCitemdb's own authoritative snapshot on every call instead of counting anything itself - immediately accurate, including for the day's earlier calls, the moment the next real lookup happens. Superseded migration 0029's original day-keyed-counter shape in place (safe - it had zero real rows) and seeded the new table with a real live snapshot so the bar reads correctly right away rather than waiting on the next scan.

Also, mid-turn: **"the Title field shouldn't be visible unless it's genuinely being modified - like a collection or TV entry - hide it for a plain movie with a linked OMDB/TMDb title."** Changed the Title field's visibility from `!isMultiTitleCollection` alone to also require `selected.size === 0 || showSeasonFields || isCollection` - hidden now for an ordinary single-movie match, still shown for fully-manual entries, TV-ish types (season/part composition), and collection/box-set scans. Confirmed safe by construction: the existing season-composition hard-reset effect keeps `manualTitle` synced to the real candidate title regardless of whether the field renders, so a hidden field's title override still exactly matches OMDb's own title at submit time.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all four packages after every change. Documented all four in `barcode-review-screen-fields.md`'s TV Scanning section. Migration 0029 (final shape) applied and verified live on both Supabase projects.

2026-09-19 (same day, second follow-up): user reported a real scan (Resident Evil: Extinction) came back with no best-match candidate at all and missed auto-fillable detail, and asked me to rework the UPC listing-text parsing rather than guess - "that text has inconsistent formatting and different types of information every time... you might have to rework your parsing." Said they'd give hints on future scans if something's still missed.

Found the actual stuck row directly in Supabase rather than asking the user to describe it: `pending_scans` had the real UPCitemdb listing title `"Resident Evil: Extinction [regions 2,5] - Dvd - - Free Shipping."`, resolved with 0 OMDB candidates. Traced the root cause by querying OMDB directly with both the actual garbled search query the code was producing and a manually-cleaned version side by side - the garbled one genuinely returned "Movie not found!", confirming this wasn't a red herring. Root cause: `cleanProductTitleForSearch` never stripped square-bracketed listing metadata at all (only round-bracketed), and its region-word pattern only matched the singular "region" plus one token, never "regions" plus a comma-list - so `"[regions 2,5]"` survived intact and broke the search. Fixed both gaps, and tested the fix against multiple real historical listings (Paper Planes, Captain Marvel, Blade Runner) plus synthetic edge cases before trusting it, specifically to catch a regression risk an early draft had (a broadened region-list capture that would have also swallowed an unrelated adjacent digit, e.g. mistaking part of "2015" for a region code).

Also built real auto-fill from this, not just the bug fix, per the user's actual framing ("find what information can be taken from that string ... used to auto fill the form"): `extractDiskRegionHint` (`packages/shared/src/formatHints.ts`) now pulls real disc-region codes straight out of listing text ("[regions 2,5]" -> "2, 5", "Region Free" -> "All") and pre-fills ConfirmScreen's Disk Region field - a field that was always left for the user to fill in by hand even when the listing already stated it. Deliberately conservative (word-bounded single-character tokens only, restricted to codes valid for the resolved format) to avoid the same year-digit false-positive risk.

Verified the actual fix end-to-end, not just via isolated regex tests: reset the real stuck pending_scans row back to "pending" and re-triggered `/api/scan/resolve` against the live dev server twice. First attempt still returned 0 candidates despite the code being correct (confirmed by running the real, unmodified functions directly via `tsx`, bypassing Next.js) - traced to the long-running `next dev` process not having picked up the `packages/shared` change. Restarted it (including clearing the same orphaned-port-process gotcha from earlier in the session) and re-resolved: real 4 OMDB candidates came back this time, best match "Resident Evil: Extinction", exactly right.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all four packages. Documented the whole investigation (root cause, the avoided regression, the dev-server-restart gotcha) in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s new UPC LISTING TEXT PARSING section.

2026-09-20: two more requests in one message.

1. "The Resident Evil: Extinction scan I just confirmed got genre_location 'Video Game' by accident instead of 'Video Game Movie' - remove 'Video Game' from the dropdown and fix that row." Queried Supabase directly first rather than assuming: confirmed "Video Game" was used by exactly that one row (and "Video Game Movie" already existed as the real established category on one other row), so fixing the one row is the whole fix - the genre_location dropdown is a live-distinct-values list (SearchableModalInput/fieldOptions.ts), not a fixed enum, so there's no separate "remove from options" step once no row uses the wrong value any more. Wrote `scripts/src/backfill-resident-evil-genre-location.ts` (dry-run by default, same convention as backfill-commando-rental.ts, refuses to overwrite if the row's value isn't exactly what was expected), ran it with `--apply` - updated both Supabase and the Sheet.
2. "Are any non-optional pending-scan fields left un-warned/un-blocked when empty?" - checked and confirmed no, they weren't: a blank Title/Movie-or-TV/Format all silently fell through to a placeholder default ("Unknown Title"/"Movie"/"DVD") in the confirm route rather than actually stopping the submission. Built `getMissingRequiredFields()` in ConfirmScreen.tsx, checked at the top of `handleConfirmPressed` (the one choke point every Confirm-button path already goes through) - blocks submission and lists every currently-blank required field at once. Asked the user directly whether Rating/Studio/Franchise/Original Language (unlabeled but only shown once TMDb comes up empty) should count as required too, since forcing an answer isn't right for genuinely case-doesn't-say situations - the user's own reasoning was that once the program is asking the user to manually supply something OMDb/TMDb couldn't, that IS the one chance to capture it, so yes. Implemented Rating/Studio/Original Language as required-when-shown accordingly, but deliberately left Franchise out of that same bucket despite the question grouping it in - a blank Franchise is very often the genuinely correct answer (most films aren't part of one), unlike the other three, so requiring it would wrongly block any standalone film. Flagged this judgment call back to the user rather than silently including or excluding it.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all four packages after both changes. Documented the required-field validation in `barcode-review-screen-fields.md`'s new REQUIRED-FIELD VALIDATION section.

2026-09-20 (same day, later): User asked to plan and build Collection integration for the scanner app - the schema/confirm-route support had existed since early Phase 1, but the actual scanning flow for creating a collection was only ever partially built (a keyword-guessed `isCollection` flag and a checklist UI that read from a near-always-empty OMDB search of the box's own listing title). Read through every relevant planning doc and the live code first, then asked clarifying questions and got four real decisions: drop the original franchise/director/actor-name OMDB-search idea entirely in favor of a manual per-title entry loop; add a shared physical-fields step (Format, Disk Region, etc.) filled in once for the whole box before that loop starts; Name of Collection as a plain always-editable free-text field, pre-filled from the listing title; and a manual toggle to mark/unmark a scan as a collection, independent of the automatic keyword guess. A design agent then drafted the concrete implementation plan, which surfaced three more real questions, each resolved directly with the user: Genre Location should be a fully **hard-shared** field, not just a shared default - "you can't get an axe and chop a box set into multiple parts" to put them on different shelves; the collection header's own `movie_or_tv` should stay a normal editable field defaulting to the existing plain fallback, since some collections are all-movie and some are all-TV; and Watched/Watched Disc needed a real per-member + header-rollup design rather than being hidden - each title in a collection gets its own Watched/Watched Disc, and "Watched Collection"/"Watched Collection Disc" (the header row's own existing `watched`/`watched_disc` columns, no new schema needed) auto-derive once every member agrees, or cascade down to every member when toggled by hand. Duplicate detection for collections turned out to be a real, scoped feature too, not just a warning: the user wants the existing Overwrite/Is-a-new-entry/Reject choice to fire for a collection or collection-member match, with the matched entry's own collection membership shown for context - but explicitly *never* comparing a collection/member against a standalone title, since owning a film both standalone and inside a box set is legitimate.

Built end to end: `scanResolver.ts` now skips the OMDB search entirely once `looksLikeCollection` flags a listing, rather than running a search that almost never matches a real film. `apps/web/src/app/api/scan/find-existing/route.ts` gained a `scopeToCollections` flag (filters candidates to `is_collection || title_in_a_collection` before matching) and now returns `is_collection`/`title_in_a_collection`/`name_of_collection` on every candidate. `apps/web/src/app/api/scan/confirm/route.ts`'s `overwriteUniqueId` moved from a single request-level field to a per-entry one (`ConfirmEntry.overwriteUniqueId`) - found necessary once "Overwrite" needed to apply to one specific entry within a multi-entry collection submission rather than the whole request; updated every call site across `scanApi.ts`, `offlineQueue.ts`, and `OfflineQueueScreen.tsx` to match. Built a new standalone `apps/mobile/src/components/TitleSearchPicker.tsx` for the per-title add loop, deliberately not a reuse of ConfirmScreen's own inline single-title search block (too entangled with logic that doesn't apply to a collection member). `ConfirmScreen.tsx` got a manual collection toggle (`isCollectionOverride`, persisted in `confirmDrafts.ts`), an entirely separate render branch for collection mode reusing existing single-title state where the meaning genuinely carries over (`manualTitle` doubles as Name of Collection, `movieOrTv` as the header's own type, `watched`/`watchedDisc` as Watched Collection/Watched Collection Disc) rather than declaring parallel state, a header-level duplicate-check screen mirroring the existing one, and `performCreateCollection`/`handleConfirmCollectionPressed`. Along the way, found and fixed a real latent bug the old collection checklist would have had if it had ever actually populated: the single shared `manualFields` object sent to every entry in a multi-select submission would have force-labeled every member of a mixed movie/TV-episode collection with whichever `movie_or_tv` was picked once for the whole set - the new per-entry `manualFields` construction for collections avoids this by design. Also simplified `toggleCandidate`/removed the now-dead `isMultiTitleCollection` (provably always false once collections stopped reusing the ordinary candidate multi-select) from the single-title path, since collections no longer touch it at all.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces (mobile, web, shared, backend, scripts) after every change. Documented the finished design in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section, replacing the superseded franchise-search description (kept for context, clearly marked as superseded rather than deleted). Not yet verified against a real live scan on-device - next session should do a real end-to-end test (toggle on, shared fields, a few real titles via the add loop, watched-cascade, the duplicate check both ways) per the plan's own verification section, then delete the test rows immediately after per the project's standing practice.

2026-09-20 (same day, follow-up): user started real live testing on their phone and reported two real issues.

1. **"Watched Collection Disc selects Watched Collection too, then immediately deselects both."** Root cause, found by re-reading the bottom-up auto-derive `useEffect`s added in the previous session: they computed the header's checked state as `collectionMembers.length > 0 && collectionMembers.every(...)`, so with zero titles added yet, that expression is `false` - meaning the instant a manual toggle cascaded `true` onto the (empty) member list, the same effect re-ran on that empty-array change and read "no members" as "not everything's watched," forcing both checkboxes straight back off. Fixed by skipping both derive effects entirely while `collectionMembers.length === 0` - with nothing to derive a consensus from, the header checkbox is left exactly as the user set it, and real derivation only takes over once a first title actually exists to agree or disagree with it.
2. **"Each title in the collection I'm logging has its own 4K UHD disc and its own Blu-ray special-features disc - does the format setup interfere with that?"** It did: Format/Disc Count/Special Features had been made hard-shared across the whole submission the previous session (one value applied to the header row *and* every title), on the reasoning that "a box set is one physical object" - true for shelf location, but wrong here, since each title in a box set typically has its own separate disc(s) that can differ from the others. Asked the user directly whether to move these to per-title fields, and separately whether the header's own Disc Count should auto-total rather than be typed - confirmed both, with one addition: "a collection can have a special features disk for the whole set and a title can have a special features disk for that particular title," i.e. both levels need to coexist, not one replacing the other.

   Redesigned accordingly: the header/shared step keeps its own Format (the box's general format) and Special Features (+ sub-fields, now relabeled to mean the *collection's own* bonus disc, gated on the toggle alone rather than the old "more than 1 disc" check, which doesn't apply to a header with no single primary disc of its own) - its Disc Count input was removed entirely, replaced by a live-computed read-only total (`collectionTotalDiscCount` = every title's own Disc Count summed, plus the collection's own bonus disc count if it has one). `TitleSearchPicker.tsx` gained its own Format/Disc Count/Special Features (+ sub-fields) for each title, seeded via new `initial*` props from the previously-added title's own values (or the header's shared-step values for the very first title) - cuts down on re-typing the same pattern for a box set where most/all titles share it, while still letting any individual title differ. `CollectionMember`/`ConfirmDraftCollectionMember` gained the four new fields; `performCreateCollection` now builds the header's own format/disc_count/special_features from the computed total and shared-step state, and each member's from its own fields, instead of spreading one shared object into both. Also found and fixed a real pre-existing gap while touching `buildSharedCollectionFields` for this: `release_name`/`releaseNameMatchesTitle` had a real input on the shared-fields step but was never actually included in the object returned to the confirm route at all, silently dropping whatever was typed there on every collection submission since the feature shipped a session ago - added it to the shared bucket.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across `apps/mobile` (and re-confirmed clean on `apps/web` too, unaffected by this pass) after both fixes. Updated `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section to describe the revised per-title-plus-collection-level disc fields in place of the original hard-shared description.

2026-09-20 (same day, still later): user reported the Collection flow's longer field labels ("Special Features (a bonus disc for the whole collection)" etc.) were pushing their own Switch/checkbox clean off the right edge of the screen, and asked to apply the "graphics safe area" principles discussed early in the project - which turned out not to be written down anywhere in `Claude/`, only recovered by asking the user to re-explain it. Their answer: the app targets Android specifically, so no element should sit too close to the top (the front-camera punch-hole) or bottom (the on-screen navigation buttons) of the screen.

Checked first, rather than assuming a gap: the vertical half of that principle is already correctly implemented everywhere via `react-native-safe-area-context`'s `useSafeAreaInsets()` - no actual bug there. The real bug is on a different axis entirely: React Native's flexbox defaults every child to `flexShrink: 0`, so a long `Text` label next to a fixed-size Switch/checkbox in a `row` (`justifyContent: "space-between"`) renders at its own full natural width instead of wrapping, pushing the control off-screen once the label is long enough - exactly what the Collection flow's newer, longer labels started triggering. Fixed by adding `flexShrink: 1` to every label/body text style used beside a control (`label`, `checkboxLabel`, `body` in both `ConfirmScreen.tsx` and `TitleSearchPicker.tsx`) plus a `gap` on the shared `row` style, so a long label now wraps onto multiple lines instead of pushing its control past the visible screen. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile` afterward. Documented both halves of the safe-area principle (the already-correct vertical one and the newly-fixed horizontal one) in `WEB APP DESIGN.md`'s new MOBILE LAYOUT SAFE AREAS section, specifically so this doesn't get lost a second time.

2026-09-20 (same day, still later): user gave four more pieces of feedback on the Collection flow in one message, all implemented.

1. **"Move the collection section to the top of the form."** The Name of Collection/Collection Type/Watched Collection block used to sit after the shared physical-fields step; moved to right after the "This is a collection" toggle, before Shared Details - settling what the collection actually is before asking about its discs.
2. **"If a user selects that they've seen all the disks in this collection, Watched/Watched Disc shouldn't need to show up when logging titles."** `TitleSearchPicker` now takes `impliedWatched`/`impliedWatchedDisc` props (ConfirmScreen's own current `watched`/`watchedDisc` header state) and seeds its own Watched/Watched Disc state from them - when Watched Collection Disc is already checked, both checkboxes are hidden entirely from the add-a-title form (defaulting to true); when only Watched Collection is checked, only "Watched" hides (Watched Disc stays a real, visible question, since watching the film doesn't necessarily mean via this specific disc). Either can still be corrected per-title afterward from the running member list.
3. **"Only show Overwrite/Is-a-new-entry for the whole collection once all titles are added, not per individual title."** This was the biggest change: the per-title duplicate check that ran inside `TitleSearchPicker` at add time (added the previous session) was removed entirely - adding a title now always just adds it, no interruption. `handleConfirmCollectionPressed` (ConfirmScreen.tsx) now runs the collection-scoped duplicate check for the header AND every member at once (`Promise.all`) right before submission, builds a queue of only the entries that actually came back with a match, and presents them one at a time (a new numbered "N of M" resolution screen, reusing the existing poster/field-comparison layout) if the queue is non-empty - an empty queue (no duplicates anywhere) skips straight to submission. `performCreateCollection` changed from a single optional `headerOverwriteId` parameter to an `overwriteDecisions: Record<string, string | undefined>` map (keyed "header" or a member's own `key`), built by the resolution screen as each queue item is resolved.
4. **"Only submit to the database once the collection and all its titles have the right details - one action for everything."** Checked rather than assumed: this was already true - `TitleSearchPicker`'s `onAdd` only ever updates local `collectionMembers` state, no network write happens until `performCreateCollection` sends the header + every member in one `confirmScan` call. The only network activity that used to happen mid-add-loop (the per-title duplicate check, a read) was removed by point 3 above anyway, so there's now zero network activity at all until Confirm.

`tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile` after all four changes. Updated `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section to describe the reordered form, the implied-watched hiding, the deferred collection-wide duplicate queue, and the single-submission guarantee.

2026-09-20 (same day, final and largest pass): user reported the real, already-scanned "Alfred Hitchcock Classics" collection had 19 separate problems, split explicitly into "fix the real data" and "fix the algorithm so this can't happen to future collections" - a mix of real production-data repair and a large batch of new collection-scanning features.

**Investigation first, not guessing**: queried the real Supabase data directly before touching anything. Found the 4 new member rows (Rear Window, Psycho, The Birds, Vertigo) still had their real Letterboxd-derived `personal_rating`/`watched`/`last_watched_date` intact - reading `/api/scan/confirm` directly confirmed why: those two fields are never part of any entry's own write payload at all (only `personal_rating`/`last_watched_date` - `watched`/`watched_disc` ARE sent every time and were genuinely at risk). Also found the real root cause of the header failing to overwrite an old pre-barcode legacy row ("The Alfred Hitchcock Classics Collection 4K"): exact-title-text matching, and no shared barcode (the legacy row predates barcode scanning) - not a mysterious bug, a real limitation of the matching approach. And found the user's own "Studio should be Universal" expectation didn't match any of the 4 real TMDb-resolved production companies at all (Paramount, Shamley Productions, Alfred J. Hitchcock Productions) - Universal is almost certainly the disc's real-world distributor, a different fact TMDb doesn't expose; flagged this back to the user rather than silently forcing "Universal" in.

For the two wrong NZ ratings (Psycho was "R", The Birds was "PG-13" - neither a real NZ code), checked `packages/backend/src/tmdb.ts` directly first: it already correctly filters to `iso_3166_1 === "NZ"` specifically, so this isn't a "falls back to US rating" bug - TMDb's own NZ certification data for these two films is itself wrong/crowdsourced-bad. Searched for an authoritative NZ source before asking the user to just supply the answer (per their own explicit condition - only ask if there's genuinely no way to look it up): found New Zealand's real Film & Video Labelling Body public classification bulletin (fvlb.org.nz) via web search, fetched the actual November 2020 PDF, and found real entries for Psycho (M) and The Birds (PG) approved the same day as several other Hitchcock Blu-ray titles - almost certainly the same release batch. The user then corrected this live with their own physical discs: Psycho is actually R13, The Birds is actually M - used their correction as ground truth over the public database (a different edition/reclassification is plausible).

Built, in order:
- **Overwrite protection for `watched`/`watched_disc`, universal** (`/api/scan/confirm`) - fetches the existing row's own values before an Overwrite and ORs them into the final write, so an Overwrite can only ever add watched status, never remove it.
- **NZ rating validation** (`packages/backend/src/tmdb.ts`) - a real `VALID_NZ_RATINGS` set (G/PG/M/R12/R13/R15/R16/R18); TMDb's own NZ certification is only trusted if it's actually one of these, else treated as no answer at all (reveals the manual field instead of writing garbage).
- **Collection header aggregation** (`/api/scan/confirm`, a real restructuring into build-all-entries-first / aggregate-header / write-all-entries three phases, so the header can read every member's own final resolved values before any writes happen): Genre/Franchise as a union across members, Director as the most-common single value, Studio as most-common-among-members else a new manual header "Distributor" field else "n/a", Special Features derived true if any member has one, Disc Count as the real summed total, Personal Rating as the average when every member is watched+rated, Last Watched Date as the latest among members. Removed the old `isMultiTitleEntry` guard that used to blank manual Rating/Studio/Original Language on any multi-entry submission - a leftover from the old shared-manualFields design that was incorrectly also suppressing the header's own new manual Rating/Distributor fields.
- **Rating field added to the collection header** (ConfirmScreen.tsx) - always visible, required, since there's no TMDb id for a header to auto-fill it from.
- **Per-title Release Name** (`TitleSearchPicker.tsx`) - moved out of the shared bucket per the user's own point about a definitive/remastered edition of one film being bundled into an otherwise-ordinary set.
- **Every member shares the header's own barcode** and **gets `number_of_titles_in_collection` set to match the header** - both simple client-side additions to `performCreateCollection`.
- **Genre Location dropdown filtered by media type** (`filterGenreLocationOptions`, ConfirmScreen.tsx) - the user has started prefixing shelf-location names ("TV " / "COLLECTION " / no prefix) to disambiguate same-named shelves across formats; the dropdown now only shows the locations that apply to whatever's currently being catalogued, for both the collection flow and the ordinary single-title flow.
- **Collection-wide duplicate matching redesigned again** (`/api/scan/find-existing`) - the single-decision flow from earlier the same day still matched the header by exact title text, which is exactly what failed for the real legacy row. Replaced with a genuine fuzzy match (`textSimilarity`, a local Dice-coefficient word-overlap scorer - deliberately not shared from `packages/backend/src/estimatedValue/confidence.ts`'s near-identical one, since that whole module is stripped from the public build and collection matching has to work there too) combining name similarity, a format-match bonus, and - the strongest signal - how many of the new collection's own member titles exactly match an existing member already catalogued under a candidate collection. The single Overwrite decision now resolves both the header AND every member's own overwrite target in one pass, using the matched candidate's own `existingMemberTitles` (returned alongside it) - a member with no exact match among the candidate's existing titles is simply added as new, never blocked.
- **Deliberately deferred, disclosed rather than skipped**: per-member Studio still uses TMDb's own single first-listed production company, unchanged - the user's fuller idea (each member's studio should prefer whichever company is most common across every member's *entire* TMDb company list, not just each film's own first-listed pick) needs `TmdbFields` extended to carry each film's full company list plus a real cross-referencing pass, logged as a real follow-up rather than attempted in the same already-large session.

Verified estimated_value's existing behavior for collection members (item raised twice by the user) needed no code change at all - already null in the DB (never part of the write payload, and `refreshEstimatedValueForTitleIds` already skips `title_in_a_collection` rows) and already renders as literal "n/a" in the Sheet (the codebase's own universal empty-cell convention, `formatValueForSheet`).

Wrote `scripts/src/backfill-hitchcock-collection-fix.ts` (dry-run by default, matches the project's established one-time-backfill convention) to repair the real Hitchcock rows: the header's aggregated fields (computed by hand to match what the now-fixed algorithm would produce), each member's barcode/number_of_titles_in_collection/release_name, the two real corrected ratings, and deleting the old legacy stub row entirely (the new header row is a strict superset of its data, and the duplicate only exists because of the exact-matching gap now fixed for future scans). Dry-ran first, confirmed the expected current state exactly, asked the user directly before running for real given it deletes a row - approved, ran with `--apply`, then independently re-queried the live data afterward (a separate throwaway script, deleted immediately after) to confirm all 6 changes actually landed: 24 Hitchcock-related rows now (was 25, legacy stub gone), header carrying every aggregated field correctly, all 4 members sharing the real barcode/title count, Psycho and The Birds showing R13/M, and every member's own Letterboxd-derived personal_rating/last_watched_date still intact throughout.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces (mobile, web, shared, backend, scripts) after every change in this pass.

2026-09-18 (later the same evening, after both repos were pushed): user tested the real "Alfred Hitchcock Classics" collection and reported 4 more problems: the header's `title` still wasn't in the colon+comma-list format, `special_features` was still false on the header despite all 4 members having it, the collection name needed the same fix as the title, and the Sheet's Estimated Value cell for collection members was blank instead of "n/a".

**Queried the real Supabase data directly before changing anything** (this project's own established convention) rather than guessing from the code - found the Hitchcock header's `last_updated` timestamp matched exactly when the EARLIER `backfill-hitchcock-collection-fix.ts` script ran (`--apply`) hours before, and reading that script's own `HEADER_UPDATE` object confirmed it never touched `title`/`name_of_collection`/`special_features` at all - those three fixes didn't exist yet when that script was written. So items 1-3 were a backfill-timing gap on one already-existing row, not a live-algorithm bug: the confirm route's own `special_features` aggregation (`memberBuilts.some(...)`) was already correct for any collection scanned through the real flow.

Item 4 (blank instead of "n/a") turned out to be a genuinely wrong claim from the PREVIOUS entry above ("already renders as literal 'n/a' in the Sheet ... formatValueForSheet") - that verification was never actually checked against the write path. The real mechanism: the Sheet's "Estimated Value" column is a single synthetic field (`estimated_value_display`, combining two real DB columns) that only ever gets written by the async pricing job's own `pushEstimatedValuesToSheet` - which explicitly filters out every `title_in_a_collection` row before it ever reaches that function, since these rows are deliberately never priced. Nothing else in the codebase ever touches that Sheet cell for a collection member, so it just sits blank forever instead of getting the `formatEstimatedValueCell(null, false)` = "n/a" the formatter would already produce if it were ever called.

Fixed, in order:
- **`title`/`name_of_collection` now get the colon-suffix convention automatically** (`ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s `performCreateCollection`) - previously a fully manual, type-it-yourself convention (per the original design doc); now `<what the user typed>: <every current member's own title, comma-separated>` is generated at submit time for both fields on the header, and members' own `name_of_collection` picks up the same full string (it has to match the header's exactly - it's the fuzzy-match/aggregation join key). The duplicate-check search (`handleConfirmCollectionPressed`) deliberately still searches on the plain typed name alone, not the full generated one - the member-title-overlap signal already covers that ground.
- **A collection member's Sheet "Estimated Value" cell now gets "n/a" written synchronously** (`/api/scan/confirm`, right after each member's own insert/update in the main write loop) - guarded by the Sheet actually having that column (private build only), imports `formatEstimatedValueCell` from the private-only shared module wrapped in its own `ESTIMATED-VALUE-PUBLIC-EXCLUDE` sentinel block.

Wrote a small round-2 backfill (`scripts/src/backfill-collection-followup-fixes.ts`) to fix the real Hitchcock header's own `title`/`name_of_collection`/`special_features`. Before applying, ran a dry-run diagnostic and discovered the wider "n/a" gap actually affects **948 real collection-member rows across 252 distinct box sets** in the whole collection, not just Hitchcock - asked the user how to scope the fix given that was a much bigger blast radius than the report implied; the user chose to fix only the Hitchcock header now (`--header-only` flag added to the script) and let every other collection's members pick up "n/a" naturally the next time each one gets rescanned, rather than a one-time mass Sheet patch across 948 cells. Verified the header's real Supabase row directly afterward - `title`/`name_of_collection` now read "The Alfred Hitchcock Classics: Rear Window, Psycho, The Birds, Vertigo" and `special_features` is `true`.

`tsc --noEmit` clean on both `apps/web` and `apps/mobile` after the fix. Both new files (`backfill-collection-followup-fixes.ts`) added to `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s `EXCLUDE_FILENAMES` (real Supabase UUIDs/collection specifics) before pushing; sanitizer re-ran clean. Both repos pushed - private first, then the public checkout mirrored/committed/pushed (only the `ConfirmScreen.tsx` change actually reached the public repo, since the Sheet "n/a" fix lives entirely inside an `ESTIMATED-VALUE-PUBLIC-EXCLUDE` block and the backfill script is filename-excluded).

2026-09-19 (same night, immediately after): user gave a new rule - "original language for the collection header should be english, the Original language should only be n/a for a collection if not all the titles are of the same language, which is rare." Added a fifth aggregated field to `/api/scan/confirm`'s collection-header block: unlike Director's "most common, empty on a tie", Original Language requires unanimous agreement across every member - the shared language if all agree, "n/a" otherwise (deliberately never a tie-break, per the user's own exact framing).

**Self-caught mistake, fixed the same turn**: while verifying this against the real Hitchcock row, discovered the round-2 backfill above had only updated the HEADER's own `name_of_collection`, never the 4 members' - breaking the exact-match join between them (used by both the collection-header fuzzy match and the Sheet aggregation). Wrote `scripts/src/backfill-hitchcock-round3-fixes.ts` to fix both that oversight (syncs all 4 members' `name_of_collection` to the header's current value) and set the header's own `original_language` to "English" (confirmed all 4 real members already are). Dry-ran, applied, re-verified all 5 rows directly - fully consistent now. Added to `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s `EXCLUDE_FILENAMES`; sanitizer re-ran clean.

2026-09-19 (later session): user asked to run `apps/mobile` and its dependencies so they could test-scan real discs. Started both required dev servers per the standing "both must run" rule (`barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`) - `apps/web`'s `next dev -p 3001` (matching `apps/mobile/.env`'s `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL`, which already pointed at the machine's current LAN IP, 192.168.1.71, so no `.env` edit was needed) and `apps/mobile`'s `expo start`. Watched the web server's log live while the user scanned - `/api/scan/queue` and `/api/scan/upc-quota` all returned 200 across several real scans; the only recurring log noise was a pre-existing, unrelated Edge Runtime warning about `node:crypto` in `packages/backend/src/estimatedValue/refresh.ts`'s instrumentation import, not something this session touched or that affects scanning.

While scans were running, the user asked for a planning-docs read-through for context, then raised a real UX gap in the Collection flow: the colon-suffix full collection name (`<base name>: <title1>, <title2>, ...`) has been auto-generated since 2026-09-18, but only silently, once, at final Confirm - the user never sees the real name build up as titles are actually added. Asked one clarifying question on placement (preview under the Name of Collection field only, above the Titles list only, or both) - the user chose both. Added `previewCollectionName` (`ConfirmScreen.tsx`), a read-only value mirroring `performCreateCollection`'s own generation exactly, recomputed live off `manualTitle`/`collectionMembers` on every render (same pattern already established for the header's live-computed `collectionTotalDiscCount`) - rendered as "Will be saved as: ..." in both chosen spots. `manualTitle` itself is untouched by this, so it stays trivially editable as just the base name throughout. Also corrected the on-screen hint text under the Name of Collection input, which had gone stale since 2026-09-18 (still telling the user to type the colon-list themselves by hand). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. Documented in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section. Not yet pushed to GitHub or verified live on-device by the user.

2026-09-20 (same session, later): user reported finding separate "Universal" and "Universal Pictures" entries while picking a Collection header's Distributor and asked to fix/normalize/merge Studio across the database, Sheet, and app dropdown - explicitly to check back if unsure about any specific merges. Audited the real `studio` column directly (paginated past PostgREST's 1000-row cap) rather than guessing: 891 distinct values across ~3,065 rows, most genuinely distinct real production companies (expected - this field is TMDb's own per-film company name or a manual Distributor fallback), but a real chunk were casing drift, a recurring capital-I typo ("FIlm" for "Film"), and letter-level typos of well-known studios.

Asked three clarifying questions before touching anything: Warner Bros. canonical spelling (user chose "Warner Bros.", the official modern form, over the data's own majority "Warner Brothers"), MGM canonical spelling (user chose "Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer", again the official form over the majority-but-typo'd "Metro Godwin Mayer"), and how to treat parent/sub-brand pairs that might be the same real company at different specificity (Sony family, Dreamworks family, Discovery family, Twentieth Century Fox/20th Century Studios, CBS family, Toho family, ITC family, TriStar Pictures/Productions) - user chose "merge each into its shorter parent name" (which also settled, by the same logic, that Universal Pictures/Paramount Pictures should merge down to plain Universal/Paramount, matching the user's own original example exactly). For three more obscure/uncertain spellings, verified against real external sources instead of guessing: Ashmont Productions (confirmed via web search - the real Bewitched production company, named for Asher+Montgomery; the data's own majority spelling "Ashmount" was actually wrong), Rafran Cinematografica, and StudioCanal (both confirmed via TMDb/Wikipedia/Wikidata).

Built `STUDIO_ALIASES`/`normalizeStudio` (`packages/shared/src/titleParsing.ts`, same shape as the existing `FORMAT_ALIASES`/`normalizeFormat`) covering every confirmed merge group, wired into `parseSheetRowToTitle` (so every future Sheet sync auto-normalizes) and into `/api/scan/confirm`'s `finalStudio` (both the manual-entry and TMDb-auto-fill paths, so a fresh scan can never reintroduce a duplicate spelling going forward - also fixes the Collection header's own "most common studio among members" aggregation for free, since members now always store already-normalized values). Deliberately left alone: any studio with only one real, genuinely distinct production arm (Universal Television, Sony Animation, MGM Television, Marvel Animation vs Marvel Studios, BBC's own Film/Radio/Studios sub-labels vs plain BBC) - not folded into its parent, since these are different real entities, not spelling variants. Also deliberately out of scope: the ~800 single-occurrence values with no duplicate spelling present in the data to merge against (a genuine typo with no counterpart, e.g. "Sandy Howard Prodcutions") - flagged as a possible future full spellcheck pass rather than attempted blind, mirroring the existing precedent of the collection-wide Title misspelling audit.

Wrote `scripts/src/backfill-studio-normalization.ts` (dry-run by default, `--apply` to write for real, Sheet-first same as the existing Format/Rating normalization scripts) - dry run showed 992 rows would change, grouped and shown to the user for a final go/no-go before writing to the real Sheet; approved, applied, then ran `npm run sync:sheet` to push into the private Supabase project. Verified directly afterward: 252 "Universal" (was split across 6 spellings), 276 "Warner Bros." (was split across 4), 63 "Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer" (was split across 5), and "Universal Television" correctly left untouched as its own distinct value. Checked whether the public demo Supabase project had the same duplicates too - its `titles` table returned 0 rows via its anon key (no service-role key is stored for that project in this environment), so nothing could be verified or fixed there this session; left as a disclosed gap rather than guessed at, documented in `database-design.md`'s new Studio normalization note as a follow-up for once that project's data is next refreshed or a key is provided. The mobile app's Studio dropdown needed no separate fix - `fieldOptions.ts` already pulls live distinct values from Supabase, so it reflects the merged list automatically. `tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces. Documented the full reasoning (every merge decision and why) in `STUDIO_ALIASES`'s own doc comment and in `database-design.md`. Not yet pushed to GitHub.

2026-09-20 (same session, later still): user reported a real Collection scan in progress had only 2 physical discs but 4 titles (two titles per disc), and the header's disc-count total was wrongly computed as 4 - `collectionTotalDiscCount` just summed every member's own `disc_count`, which double-counts a disc two titles genuinely share. Asked two clarifying questions before building anything: how to actually capture which disc a title is on (chose a free-text "Disc Number(s)" field per title over a "shares a disc with" picker, since it also cleanly handles a title spanning multiple discs via a list like "1,2"), and whether it should persist to the database or just be a live UI aid for computing the total (chose persisted) - the user's own reasoning for persisting it: the future web app should be able to tell a searcher which disc in a set holds a given title, not just that it's part of the set.

Built end to end: migration `0030_add_disc_number_in_set.sql` (plain text column, both live Supabase projects - not yet applied, needs a fresh Management API token from the user, requested but not yet provided this session) wired through `packages/shared/src/types.ts`/`titleParsing.ts` (HEADER_ALIASES/AUTO_CREATE_COLUMNS/parseSheetRowToTitle, so a future Sheet column and sync pick it up automatically) and `/api/scan/confirm/route.ts` (`disc_number_in_set: cleanFreeText(asString(manual.disc_number_in_set))`, same free-text-passthrough treatment as `release_name`). `TitleSearchPicker.tsx` gained a `discNumbers` field per title (digits/commas only), seeded via a new `nextDiscNumberGuess` in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` - one higher than the highest disc number any current member already uses, so the common "every title gets its own disc" case needs no typing at all, while still being fully editable to type an existing number when a title genuinely shares a disc with one already added. `collectionTotalDiscCount` was rewritten to count the size of the Set of distinct disc-number tokens across every member (parsed from each member's comma-separated `discNumbers`) instead of summing `disc_count` - two titles both listing "1" now correctly count as one disc; a member with a genuinely blank `discNumbers` (shouldn't happen given the seeded default, but handled rather than left to silently corrupt the total) falls back to contributing its own `disc_count` in isolation. `ConfirmDraftCollectionMember` (`confirmDrafts.ts`) also gained the field, for draft persistence across app restarts.

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces. Documented in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section (a new bullet alongside the existing per-title Format/Disc Count/Special Features one) and in `WEB APP DESIGN.md`'s DVD Pages section, per the user's explicit request that this show up in the future web app's design ("Disc 2 of 4" on a DVD Page; the full per-disc breakdown on a DVD Collection Page).

User then provided a fresh Management API token in-chat. Applied `0030_add_disc_number_in_set.sql` to both live Supabase projects via `POST /v1/projects/{ref}/database/query` (the SQL sent as a JSON payload built with `node`, since `jq` isn't available in this environment) - both returned `[]` (success, DDL returns no rows). Verified directly afterward via `information_schema.columns` on both projects: `disc_number_in_set` (`text`) now exists on both. The token was only ever used inline in the two curl calls and the SQL/verification payloads were written to temp files in the scratchpad, deleted immediately after use - never persisted anywhere in the repo. **Not yet verified against the user's actual real in-progress 2-disc/4-title scan** - next step is for the user to finish adding that collection's titles with real disc numbers and confirm the header total comes out to 2, not 4. Not yet pushed to GitHub.

2026-09-20 (same session, later still): user asked to backfill `disc_number_in_set` for the real Alfred Hitchcock Classics 4K set (scanned before this column existed) and separately reported not being able to see the new column in the Sheet at all. Investigated the second issue first: `AUTO_CREATE_COLUMNS` (which would create the missing Sheet header) is only ever consulted by `scripts/src/sync-sheet.ts` - the scan-confirm route and sheet-webhook never create new Sheet columns themselves - and that script hadn't been run since `disc_number_in_set` was added to the table. Ran `npm run sync:sheet`, which added the "Disc Number In Set" header at column AT (safe to run since every row's value for this brand-new column was still null everywhere, so the one-way Sheet-\>Supabase sync was a no-op for it).

For the Hitchcock backfill, queried the real collection data directly first rather than assuming a layout: found there are actually three separate "Hitchcock"-named collections in the real data, and confirmed the "last session" one the user meant is the 4K UHD set (Rear Window/Psycho/The Birds/Vertigo, unique_id `03dffd71-...`) via its barcode-scan history. Each of its 4 members already has `disc_count: 2` and the header already totals 8 - matching the known 4K-UHD-bundles-a-companion-Blu-ray convention (2 discs per film, not shared between films), so this particular set was never actually affected by the double-counting bug from the previous entry, just missing the new field's data. Asked the user directly how the real 8 discs are physically arranged rather than guessing an order - confirmed sequential per-film blocks: Rear Window 1-2, Vertigo 3-4, Psycho 5-6, The Birds 7-8. Wrote `scripts/src/backfill-hitchcock-disc-numbers.ts` (dry-run by default, same query-first/verify-before-write convention as the earlier Hitchcock backfill scripts), dry-ran to confirm current state, applied, then independently re-queried both Supabase and the live Sheet (`AT` column) afterward to confirm all 4 values landed correctly on both sides.

2026-09-20 (same session, later still): user asked to verify that a collection header's "Release Name matches name" checkbox doesn't write the auto-generated colon+comma-list title into `release_name` when checked, and instead keeps just the base collection name (what's actually printed on the disc). Read the code rather than assuming: found the header's checkbox reused the single-title flow's own convention verbatim (`releaseNameMatchesTitle ? null : ...`), where null correctly means "would just duplicate title, so skip it" for an ordinary film. For a collection header this convention is wrong even though it never actually caused the specific failure the user was worried about (the code always wrote either null or the manually-typed text - `nameOfCollection`, the full comma-list string, was never in that expression at all) - but null still silently discards a real, different fact: a header's own `title`/`name_of_collection` always differ from the box's own printed name once the colon+member-list suffix is appended, so "matches" checked should mean "matches the *base* name," not "there's nothing worth storing." Fixed `performCreateCollection`'s header entry to store `baseCollectionName` instead of null when checked.

Checked the real data before considering this done, per the project's own standing practice: of 508 real collection headers, 507 have a null `release_name`, but querying specifically for barcode-scanned ones (`barcode_id is not null`) found only ONE real collection has ever gone through this Collection flow at all - the Alfred Hitchcock 4K set - and its `release_name` was already a manually-typed value ("The Alfred Hitchcock Classics Collection"), never the checkbox default. So the old behavior never actually affected any real row; no backfill was needed, and the fix is purely forward-looking. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. Documented in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s Collections section. Not yet pushed to GitHub.


2026-09-20 (same session, later still): user sent a 12-item batch after testing a real second collection (the new Abbott and Costello "Classic Collectables" box set, 2 discs/4 titles), plus a mid-turn report that "Try Amazon Search" on a Pending Value box set said all providers were exhausted when they shouldn't be. Handled in order of diagnosis-first:

**Root causes found (queried real data / read code before changing anything):** (a) header `disc_count` 4 instead of 2 - `/api/scan/confirm`'s server-side header aggregation still naively summed each member's `disc_count`, silently overwriting the correct client-computed value; the same-day distinct-disc-number fix had only ever changed the client preview. (b) rating "Approved" on all 4 members - OMDB's own `Rated` value (a fallback after TMDb's validated NZ certification) was never passed through `normalizeRating`. (c) shelf-location message naming a member title - `computeShelfLocation` didn't exclude `title_in_a_collection` rows, and members always share their header's genre_location. (d) Amazon "exhausted" - `RAPIDAPI_KEY` only existed in `scripts/.env`, never `apps/web/.env.local`, so every provider returned `error` on a missing key and the waterfall reported `all_exhausted` with zero real calls made. (e) the add-title search needing two presses - `TitleSearchPicker`'s ScrollView lacked `keyboardShouldPersistTaps`.

**Asked three clarifying questions first** (per the standing ask-on-data-modeling rule): what "BOX COLLECTION Person" vs "COLLECTION Person" means (big box sets vs smaller single-case sets, physically stored in separate room areas), whether the header's typed Total Disc Count should include the bonus disc (yes, one combined total, with the bonus disc picked from the same numbered checkbox list), and confirmed the tap-to-edit design.

**Built:** header form now has a typed Total Disc Count + a "Which disc is the bonus disc?" checkbox list; each title's Disc Count + Disc Number(s) inputs were replaced by one "What disc(s) is this title on?" checkbox list (count derived from ticks, stored data shape unchanged) - the old computed-total logic and the server-side aggregation were deleted outright rather than patched; "+ Add a title" disabled until the total is entered; tap a title or its Edit link to reopen the picker pre-filled and save in place; member Franchise now uses the tag dropdown; keyboard closes and search fires on the first press (also on the keyboard's return key); form scrolls to the bottom and shows an animated `IndeterminateBar` while a collection saves; `computeShelfLocation` now only considers standalone titles and headers; `approved` -> `G` alias + OMDB fallback normalized; `filterGenreLocationOptions` recognises the new "BOX COLLECTION " prefix. Item 1 (number pad) became moot - there's no free-text disc field left.

**Real data fixed** via `scripts/src/backfill-collection-batch-fixes-2.ts` (dry-run first, then applied to Supabase + Sheet): Hitchcock header + 4 members -> "BOX COLLECTION Person"; the 4 Abbott and Costello members' rating "Approved" -> "G"; the Abbott and Costello header's disc_count 4 -> 2. Both new backfill scripts (containing real row ids) added to `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s EXCLUDE_FILENAMES. `RAPIDAPI_KEY` copied into `apps/web/.env.local` (never displayed) and documented in `.env.example`; web dev server restarted (the old process had to be killed by PID - TaskStop alone left an orphan on port 3001).

`tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces. Documented in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md` (new bullets under Collections) and `estimated-value.md` (the Amazon env-var gotcha + diagnostic). **Not verified on-device** - none of the new collection UI (checkbox lists, tap-to-edit, loading bar, scroll-to-bottom) has been exercised on a phone yet; Metro should hot-reload it. Disclosed, not silently skipped: OMDB's other non-NZ `Rated` values still pass through the fallback verbatim; the try-amazon route still can't tell "misconfigured" from "exhausted". Not yet pushed to GitHub.

2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user suspected the Abbott and Costello box set got poor Pending Value candidates because its search title was "just the title of the collection" rather than release name + format. Checked the real data instead of assuming: the header's `release_name` is "Classic Collectables Bud and Lou Costello 4 of their best movies" and its stored `pending_value_review.search_title` is exactly "Classic Collectables Bud and Lou Costello 4 of their best movies DVD" - i.e. `buildSearchTitle` already produced release name + format per the estimated-value.md spec, so the query itself wasn't the bug (it just looks like "the collection's title" because the release name equals the base name); asked the user what they expected it to contain instead (still open). Found and fixed a real side effect while in there: `fallbackEstimate.ts`'s `isSpecialEdition` treated any release_name different from the title as a special edition, so every collection header (release_name = base name, title = auto-generated "<base>: <members>") got the special-edition-bumped fallback range - now a collection whose title starts with its release name is not a special edition. `tsc` clean on `packages/backend`. Existing fallback estimates (e.g. the Abbott header's est. $48) are not recomputed until that title is refreshed.

2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user reported "Try Amazon Search" still said all providers exhausted after the earlier fix. Called each provider's `searchWithStatus` directly: `key set: false` - `RAPIDAPI_KEY=` is blank in BOTH `scripts/.env` and `apps/web/.env.local`. My earlier diagnosis (key present only in scripts/.env) was wrong: I had checked with `sed` redacting the value, never its length, then copied a blank line across. Corrected honestly in `estimated-value.md`. Built a `not_configured` outcome (route short-circuits on a blank key before touching Supabase; `TryAmazonOutcome` type + `AutoEstimatedDetailScreen` alert say the key isn't set and no quota was used). `tsc` clean on web and mobile. Still needs the user to supply the actual RapidAPI key.

2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user asked to look into "those other OMDB rating things" (the disclosed gap: OMDB's non-NZ `Rated` values passing through the fallback). 2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user supplied the RapidAPI key as a screenshot (couldn't copy the text). 2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user chose "auto-shorten, then retry" for Amazon box-set queries. 2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user pointed out some box sets are titled like "2 Movie Collection" with the film names in the title itself, so the automatic ": Title1, Title2" suffix shouldn't be added there, the titles could be pre-searched from the name, and such a bland name needs no release name. 2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user noticed many look-alike franchise names (5 spellings of Spider-Man) and asked to merge/normalize them, checking with them where unsure. 2026-09-20 (same session, follow-up): user reported six problems from the Spider-Man "2 movie collection" box and asked for changes so they don't recur. Diagnosed each against the real rows first (findings and fixes are written up in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md` under "SECOND REAL COLLECTION SCAN"): (1) new members didn't overwrite the older entries - the older set had no header row and the titles differed by hyphens/spacing as well as the colon, so member matching now uses IMDb id + a space-blind title key and header-less member groups become candidates; (2) "Animation" stripped from genre everywhere (`removeNonGenreTags`) and swept from 4 other rows; (3) generic-name detection now judges only the text before the first colon, and the suffix rule ignores spacing/hyphens; (4) animation no longer defaults to Live Action for members (TMDb/genre fallback) and the Collection form has an optional shared "Animation or Live Action" field; (5)/(6) the importer already matches these rows - it just hadn't been applied since 2026-09-18 - so the two films and the header got the importer's values via `backfill-spiderverse-collection-fixes.ts` (dry-run, then applied to Supabase + Sheet). `tsc` clean on all five workspaces. 2026-09-19 (later session): the user found filling in a pending scan as slow as typing the row into the Sheet by hand, and asked how to make it faster. Agreed direction: (1) replace the long Confirm form with slide-style screens (one decision per slide, big tap targets), landing on a Summary slide with one big Save button where any line can be tapped to jump to its slide; the fields most often changed by hand (Genre Location, Disc count / Special features, Franchise) each get their own slide; (2) a FRONT-of-case photo (not the back - too much text) read by the existing Gemini vision model (`gemini-flash-lite-latest`, already used in `formatVision.ts`; the user confirmed keeping Gemini over Claude Haiku), saved to the database; web app product-image priority is 1) the photo taken during the scan, 2) the image found from the UPC/barcode search, 3) Estimated Value product images. Plan approved (plan file kept outside the repo). Built so far: Phase 1 for the single-title flow only - new `SlideFlow.tsx` (one slide at a time, progress dots, big Back/Next, edge-swipe), `BigChoice.tsx` (big tap tiles), `SummaryRow.tsx`; `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s single-title JSX re-arranged into slides Summary / Title / Format & region / Discs & special features / Genre Location / Franchise & animation / Details / (only when needed) Needed-from-the-case, reusing all existing state, validation and submit code unchanged. `tsc` clean; not yet tried on a phone. Still to do: the Collection flow in the same slide shell, the front-of-case photo (migration 0031 `case_photo_path`, upload route, Gemini on the photo, web image priority), and docs. Also found the user's phone was on mobile data when Expo "wasn't there" (not a code problem).

2026-09-19 (same session, after the first real scan on the new slide UI): the user scanned the three-film Spider-Man (MCU) 4K box set and reported everything correct except the header's Release Name, which should be n/a because a franchise can have several trilogies, so the name "Spiderman Trillogy Collection" doesn't identify the release. Cause: `isGenericCollectionName` (apps/mobile/src/lib/collectionName.ts) only recognised names like "2 Movie Collection", so the header got the full collection name as its release name. Fix: any collection name (the part before a colon) containing duology/trilogy/tetralogy/quadrilogy/pentalogy/hexalogy - including the "trillogy" misspelling - now counts as generic, so no Release Name is saved; named sets such as "Universal Monsters Collection" are deliberately still not generic. Repaired the real header row (Supabase release_name null; Sheet cell written as "n/a") after a script verified it was the expected row. Estimated Value needed no code change: `verbatimTitleOnly` in packages/backend/src/estimatedValue/searchTitle.ts already falls back to the collection's own title when release_name is empty, so this header now searches by its collection name (and the shortened retry cuts before the colon). The user then started testing the slide layout for a single disc. `tsc` clean on the mobile workspace.

2026-09-19 (same session, feedback round on the slide UI): the user tried the slide layout live. Liked: big buttons for common values with a text field for rare ones; the amount per slide. Changes made: (1) the flow now starts on the best-match page (posters) when the barcode returned candidates, and only starts on the Review "menu" when there are none or the user chooses "None of these - enter it manually instead"; (2) Review is now the LAST slide (Save there, no Next button, Back enabled) and each Review row expands in place instead of jumping to another slide - only Match & type still jumps, and a slide opened from Review shows a single "Back to review" button; (3) the "This is a collection / box set" checkbox now only shows on the match page (or the manual-entry Review), not on later slides; (4) Genre Location has a type-a-new-one field under the big tiles; (5) Release Name, Release Variant Note and Case Notes moved to their own slide, leaving Condition, rented and watched together; (6) Special Features discs use a small +/- stepper, big buttons for DVD / Blu-ray / 4K, and a text field for anything else; (7) new `FranchiseEditor.tsx`: franchise tags as removable blocks, up to three "Franchises that often go with this selection" (computed from how often tags share a title across the collection - `franchiseCooccurrence` added to `loadFieldOptions`), and a blank add-field with the existing-franchise dropdown. Files: `SlideFlow.tsx`, `SummaryRow.tsx` (expandable), `FranchiseEditor.tsx`, `fieldOptions.ts`, `ConfirmScreen.tsx`. `tsc` clean, not yet retried on the phone. The Collection flow is still the old long form; the front-of-case photo is not started.

2026-09-19 (same session, scan lookup bug): the user's Justice League 4K scan came back with the title, barcode and product image but the form treated it as manual entry. Cause (found in the scan's stored resolver output): the barcode listing's title was "Justice League, 5051892211390" - the product's own barcode appended - and `cleanProductTitleForSearch` (packages/shared/src/omdb.ts) left the number in, so the OMDB search was for "Justice League 5051892211390" and returned nothing (status needs_manual, zero candidates). The user asked whether this had been fixed in an earlier session and undone: it had not - git history shows no earlier handling of a barcode number in a title, and none of the earlier title-cleaning rules were changed; it is a new listing pattern. Fix: any run of 8-14 digits is now stripped from the search text (tested: "Justice League, 5051892211390" -> "Justice League"; a real title like "Fahrenheit 451" is untouched). 2026-09-22 (same session, later): the user scanned "Universal Monsters: The Essential Collection" and noticed its real UPCitemdb photo has a wide white margin baked into the image itself, and asked for it to be auto-cropped "for cleanliness." Built `autocropImageBuffer` (new file, `packages/backend/src/imageCrop.ts`) on `@jimp/plugin-crop`'s `autocrop()` (new dependency, same minimal-Jimp-build pattern as `posterMatch.ts`); found live that `cropOnlyFrames: true` silently no-opped on the real photo (it only has left/right padding, and that option requires whitespace on every side before touching any), fixed by setting it `false`. Wired into `uploadCaseImage` so both confirm paths (`scan/confirm` and `pending/respond`) get the fix automatically; any failure returns the original image unchanged, same convention as every other image helper. Then, per the user's immediate follow-up ("so if it is cropped wrong I can give you an early warning"), built a GET pass-through route (`apps/web/src/app/api/scan/case-image-preview/route.ts`) that crops-on-the-fly (nothing cached) so `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s "Your scanned item" preview shows the same crop the confirm step will actually save, instead of the raw white-bordered photo. `tsc` clean across web and mobile; verified live end-to-end (200 + correct cropped bytes, matching a standalone test; wrong secret correctly 401s).

2026-09-23: the user reported the "Your scanned item" photo had gone grey. The crop pipeline itself was fine (replaying the exact real scan through the route by hand, twice, returned a correct cropped photo both times); the web server's own request log showed the real cause - the phone's actual request to the new `case-image-preview` route came back `401`, immediately before the identical URL succeeded when auth was supplied by hand. Root cause: that route gated on the `x-scan-secret` header only, but React Native's `<Image source={{uri, headers}}>` doesn't reliably attach custom headers to the native image-fetch request on Android (unlike `Image.getSizeWithHeaders`, a separate native bridge call that does send them correctly - which is why the image box always sized itself right while the photo inside it silently never loaded). Fixed by having this one route also accept the secret as a `secret` query param (`getCroppedImageSource` in `scanApi.ts` now sends both the header and the query param - only the latter actually works on-device, the header is free to keep for parity). Every other `/api/scan/*` route is untouched and stays header-only, since they're all called via plain `fetch()`, where headers work normally - this is specific to routes consumed by a native `<Image>` tag. `tsc` clean on web and mobile; verified live (query-param-only auth returns the correct bytes, no auth at all still cleanly 401s).

2026-09-23 (same session, later): the user asked what went wrong with the Universal Monsters title, which had a cut-off "Free Shi" fragment stuck to the end of it. Pulled the scan's real stored data: UPCitemdb's (Newegg-sourced) listing title is truncated mid-word at the source - `"Universal Classic Monsters [blu-ray] [region B] [blu-ray] - Dvd - - Free Shi"`, literally missing "pping". `cleanProductTitleForSearch` (`packages/shared/src/omdb.ts`) already strips a complete "free shipping" phrase (from the 2026-09-19 fix for the same listing shape, un-truncated), but the regex requires the whole phrase, so the partial fragment survived untouched into both the displayed title and the OMDB search query - reproduced exactly by running the real string through the function before changing anything. Fixed with a second pass, `stripTrailingTruncatedNoise`, that strips a trailing fragment only when a known suffix phrase's leading word(s) are fully spelled out, followed by a genuine 2+-character prefix of its last word, anchored to the literal end of the string - narrow enough that it can't eat real title text (tested against "Free Solo Documentary Dvd - Fast", a hypothetical listing for a real film starting with "Free", and it correctly left it alone). Re-verified every other previously-fixed title-cleaning case (Resident Evil's un-truncated "Free Shipping.", Paper Planes' condition words, Justice League's barcode suffix, Captain Marvel's `|`-delimited cast text) still comes out correct. `tsc --noEmit` clean across all four affected workspaces (shared, backend, web, mobile).

(already the exact pattern the Collection HEADER's own bonus-disc field uses, `bonusDiscs`/`showHeaderSpecialFeaturesDiscFields` in ConfirmScreen.tsx - found this existing precedent before writing anything new); replace the old typed count field entirely rather than keep it as a fallback; and the user clarified the "total disc count" math is meant to stay automatic/aggregate (a future web-app "disc page" concern, not something this form needs to validate) - the checklist here is just for tagging which disc is which, and a bonus disc's format can genuinely differ from the movie's own (e.g. a Blu-ray extras disc in an otherwise 4K set), so the format field had to stay editable regardless of which disc(s) are picked.

2026-09-23 (same session, immediate follow-up): the user pointed out the new "Format of Special Features Disc(s)" field was redundant - that information is already discoverable from the special-features/format data already in the database, since a bonus disc shared across titles (or bundled onto a title's own movie disc) already has its format recorded wherever it was first entered. Replaced the old `initialSpecialFeaturesDiscFormat` prop, which only ever guessed from the immediately-previous title's own value regardless of whether it was even about the same disc. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-24 (new session): the scan error "Could not find the special_features_disk_number_in_set column... in the schema cache" turned out to be exactly what it looked like, not leftover buggy code - confirmed directly against Supabase (`error code 42703, column does not exist`) that migration 0031 had simply never been applied yet. The user then asked for the real pre-existing collections' missing disc-number data to be backfilled, offering to tell me directly what discs held what. Querying broadly by `special_features=true` and no disc number pulled in ~370 rows - almost entirely unrelated legacy Sheet-imported rows that predate the barcode scanning app entirely; re-scoped to rows carrying a `barcode_id` (the real signal for "actually added through the scanning app," since a collection member always shares its header's barcode), which narrowed it to exactly 3 real collections needing this: the Spider-Verse "2 movie collection", the "Spiderman Trillogy Collection", and the Alfred Hitchcock Classics - 9 member rows total, each already on 2 of its own movie discs. The user's rule for filling them in: "the last disk listed is the disk that has special features, and that is often the case... so that should be set as default." Backfilled all 9 rows accordingly, then added the same rule as a live default in `TitleSearchPicker.tsx` - a `useEffect` that, the moment Special Features is toggled on with nothing ticked yet, defaults the bonus-disc checklist to the highest disc number already ticked for the title's own movie (a single-disc title defaults to that same disc).
