Separately, the user asked for a log of every scan attempt - both as light protection against minor data loss, and, in their own words, to help me look up how the scanner interpreted a past scan without writing a one-off script each time (exactly what the Bride of the Monster/Universal Monsters investigations this week both needed). Found most of the raw data already existed: `pending_scans.resolved_candidates` is never deleted on confirm, only on an explicit discard - but the app's own screen only ever shows `status IN ('resolved','needs_manual')`, so a confirmed scan's data was already durable but invisible and Supabase-only. Asked two clarifying questions before building: the user clarified this is meant as "a lightweight log file... to see where incorrect entries go wrong," not a resilient backup, and confirmed every scan attempt should be logged, not just kept ones. Built `apps/web/src/lib/scanLog.ts` (`logScanEvent`, fire-and-forget, appends one JSON line per event to `apps/web/scan-log/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`, UTC-dated), wired into `/api/scan/confirm`'s three real lifecycle endpoints (discard, dismiss, confirmed-success), each capturing the scan's `barcode`/`resolved_candidates` snapshot plus, for a confirm, the submitted `manualFields` and resulting title ids. Gitignored (`apps/web/scan-log/`) - local runtime data, never meant to reach either repo, deliberately independent of Supabase rather than just another table in the same database. Verified live end-to-end (a real throwaway scan, discarded, produced a correct log line; removed as a test artifact afterward). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/web` and `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-24 (same session, later): the user reported real data loss - leaving a half-filled-in scan and coming back (or "left the app"), especially mid-way through an OMDB/TMDb title search, lost everything and forced a full re-type. Found the root cause fast: `confirmDrafts.ts` already existed for exactly this, and its own comment even claimed to survive "backgrounding the whole app" - but the actual storage was a plain in-memory `Map`, which only survives while the JS engine stays alive. Plain in-app navigation (Confirm -> Pending Scans -> Confirm) never restarts the engine, so that case always worked and made the whole mechanism look more reliable than it was; backgrounding long enough for the OS to reclaim the process, a Fast Refresh reload, or actually closing/reopening Expo Go all wipe it with nothing to restore from. A title search naturally invites an alt-tab while waiting on the network, which is why that step specifically felt worst, though the bug itself was never specific to it. Fixed by backing the same Map with `AsyncStorage` (the same on-device persistence `offlineQueue.ts` already uses) as a write-through cache - the Map stays the synchronous source of truth `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s ~30 `useState` initializers already depend on, `saveConfirmDraft`/`clearConfirmDraft` now also fire-and-forget persist to AsyncStorage, and a new `hydrateConfirmDrafts()` loads it all back once at app boot. Wired into `App.tsx` behind a real `draftsReady` gate (the screen switch renders nothing until hydration finishes) rather than just hoping the timing works out, since data-loss prevention was the whole point. Disclosed, not fixed: the Collection flow's per-member `TitleSearchPicker` modal has its own separate local state not yet part of `ConfirmDraft` at all, so a title actively being typed but not yet "Added to set" could still be lost on its own (smaller blast radius, not reported as hit). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-24 (same session, later): the user reported a real "Resident Evil: Welcome to Raccoon City" scan confirmed with no Rating set and no warning, and asked for Rating to become an unconditionally-required check like Genre Location, plus a new amber "worth a look" warning on the Review slides for optional-but-important fields like Franchise and Special Features. Mid-diagnosis the user also asked to delete that entry so they could rescan it, then retracted it a message later ("actually nevermind I'll overwrite it") - no deletion happened.

Used the scan log built earlier this session for real, for the first time, to find the exact submitted payload instantly instead of writing a one-off script - confirmed `rating: null` was genuinely sent. Cross-checked the live `titles` row: TMDb had correctly resolved `tmdb_id`, `studio`, `original_language`, and `animation_or_live_action` for this film, only `rating` came back null - a genuine TMDb data gap (no NZ certification for this specific film), not a resolution bug. The real bug: `getMissingRequiredFields()`'s Rating/Studio/Original Language checks were gated on the same `showRatingField`/`showStudioField`/`showOriginalLanguageField` flags that control the manual field's on-screen visibility - deliberately `false` while the TMDb/Wikidata lookup is still in flight (to avoid a flash of the field appearing then vanishing once it resolves), which meant pressing Confirm before that lookup finished skipped the required check entirely. Fixed by checking `tmdbPreview`/`tmdbPreviewLoading` directly in the required-field logic instead: a field only counts as satisfied once the lookup has actually finished and returned something, or the user typed one by hand - while still loading, it's correctly treated as still-missing, closing the race rather than narrowing it. Applied to Studio and Original Language too, which shared the identical bug shape; Genre Location needed no change, since it was already checked unconditionally.

Also built the requested amber tier: `SummaryRow.tsx` gained a `notable?: boolean` prop (amber border/text, one severity notch below `missing`, suppressed whenever `missing` is also true, never blocks Confirm) using the same amber family as this screen's existing category-warning banner. Wired to Franchise (blank) and Special Features (off) across every place they appear on a review slide - the single-title flow, the Collection header's own bonus disc, and each `TitleSearchPicker` member's own Franchise/Special Features rows. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-24 (same session, later): the user forwarded a real Supabase email - from 2026-10-30, new tables in `public` no longer get Data API access auto-granted, and any migration creating one (replayed after that date via a reset, a fresh project, or a preview branch) needs explicit grants or the Data API returns permission-denied. Explained what it means for this project: nothing breaks today, but any future `create table` migration needs grants going forward, and a full project rebuild after that date would leave all 9 existing tables unreachable without them. The user asked me to add a defensive migration and use the Supabase token they'd supplied earlier in the session to apply it directly.

Queried the live table list on both projects first (9 tables private, 6 shared - Estimated Value's 3 tables are private-only) rather than trusting the migration files, which turned out to matter (`letterboxd_review_queue` from an old migration no longer actually exists as a table). Added `0032_add_public_schema_grants.sql` (6 shared tables) and `0033_add_estimated_value_schema_grants.sql` (the 3 private-only ones, added to `sanitize-public-repo.ts`'s exclusions same as every other Estimated-Value-only migration), matching Supabase's own template (anon SELECT-only, authenticated/service_role full CRUD). Applied both directly via the Management API to the correct project(s) each, then verified against `information_schema.table_privileges` rather than trusting the empty success response alone.

That verification turned up something predating any of this: `anon` already had full INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER on every table on both projects, an older/more permissive Supabase default, with RLS as the only actual backstop against the public anon key writing directly to anything - contradicting `apps/mobile/src/lib/supabase.ts`'s own documented design (anon is meant to be read-only; all real writes go through the secret-gated API). Flagged this to the user and asked before touching it, since revoking privileges is a real blast-radius change unlike the purely-additive grant work just done - confirmed. Added `0034_revoke_anon_write_grants.sql`/`0035_revoke_anon_write_grants_estimated_value.sql` (revoke everything from anon, re-grant SELECT explicitly) and applied the same way. Verified live with the real anon key afterward, not just the privileges table: a `select` against `pending_scans` still works, a direct `insert` now correctly fails with "permission denied." No code changes needed anywhere else - purely a Supabase-side hardening task.

2026-09-24 (new session): a power outage killed both dev servers. Restarting them surfaced a real, separate bug report: "I remember last time... I said that there was an issue with the auto save... it wasn't implemented... even when I rescan the same barcode to fix it it shows up with all the old details, I only want information on a barcode to be saved if it still exists in pending scans" - feedback from a prior session that ran out of context before the fix landed. First restarted the dev servers correctly: the web server had come up on the Next.js default port 3000 instead of the `3001` `apps/mobile/.env` actually expects (see the 2026-09-17 entry above), and `apps/mobile/.env`'s `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL` still pointed at a LAN IP (`192.168.1.83`) that no longer matched the machine's actual post-outage Wi-Fi address (`192.168.1.71`) - together these meant every request from the phone was silently failing, which is also why a separate "delete a pending scan" attempt appeared to do nothing: `PendingScansScreen.tsx`'s delete handler had no catch block, so the failed request never surfaced as an error. Fixed both (corrected `.env`, restarted the web server with `-p 3001`, added an `Alert.alert` on delete failure).

Then dug into the rescan report. Confirmed a `pending_scans` row's `id` is a fresh `gen_random_uuid()` every single scan (no barcode-based reuse), so a stale draft couldn't resurface under a new scan's own id - and a `confirmed` row is already excluded from `PendingScansScreen`'s query, so it couldn't be a leftover finished entry either. The actual mechanism: `/api/scan/queue/route.ts` did a bare `insert` on every scan with no cleanup of older unfinished rows for that same barcode - rescanning while an earlier, never-finished attempt for it was still sitting in Pending Scans just added a second, visually-identical row alongside the first rather than replacing it, so tapping back in could easily reopen the stale one (old resolved candidates, old autosaved draft) by mistake instead of the fresh scan. Fixed by having a fresh scan supersede any other still-open (`pending`/`resolved`/`needs_manual`) row for that barcode: the queue route now deletes them, logs each as a new `"superseded"` `ScanLogOutcome`, and returns their ids as `replacedIds`; `ScannerScreen.tsx` clears each one's local `confirmDrafts.ts` entry right after a successful scan. Also closed the same gap in `PendingScansScreen.tsx`'s own manual delete (it never cleared the deleted scan's local draft either) - both fixes are the same principle the user stated: a draft must never outlive the `pending_scans` row it belongs to. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/web` and `apps/mobile`. Not yet tested live on-device.

2026-09-24 (same session, immediately after): user reported "still has the same issue" - the duplicate-rows fix above was real but wasn't the actual cause. Checked the scan log directly instead of guessing again: found the user had scanned the Resident Evil barcode twice and discarded both, and both entries showed `resolved_candidates.existingMatch` - this barcode is already a cataloged entry (the same one from the earlier missing-Rating bug this session), correctly and deliberately re-found by the resolver every time via `barcode_id` (the "Backfill Rescan" design). The real mechanism: `ConfirmScreen.tsx` had essentially every detail-field `useState` initializer fall back to `existingMatch?.field` after `draft?.field`, so any rescan of an already-cataloged barcode always re-showed that entry's current stored values - discarding the pending scan never helped, since discard only deletes the `pending_scans` row, never the confirmed `titles` row `existingMatch` reads from. There was no way to get a blank form for a barcode already in the collection, by design.

Asked the user to clarify the intended behavior directly (a real design call, not a guessable bug, and the second wrong guess in a row) rather than build a third guess. Answer: "remove the prefill data if the entry has been submitted, but keep it if unsubmitted." Implemented literally - stripped `existingMatch?.field` from all ~25 detail-field initializers in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` (format, disc count, regions, genre location, franchise, animation, movie/TV type, season fields, release variant/condition/case notes, watched/rented tracking, original language, genre, running time, director, rating, studio, release name, steelbook, special features), leaving only `draft?.field` (unsubmitted, in-progress) and other fresh non-database sources (TMDb, UPC listing text, vision model). Left `existingMatch` itself untouched for identification (synthesized candidate, auto-selected imdbId so Overwrite still has a certain target) and the read-only "already matches" comparison card - the user's rule was about prefill, not those. Fixed two small follow-on effects: the `movieOrTv` auto-guess-from-candidate effect no longer skips when `existingMatch?.movie_or_tv` is set (it's blank now, so the guess correctly fills it), and `manualTitle`'s `existingMatch?.title` fallback (dead code in this flow already, kept consistent regardless) was dropped too. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. Not yet re-tested live on-device.

2026-09-25 (new session): a power outage had killed both dev servers again overnight - restarted both the same correct way (web on `-p 3001`, matching `apps/mobile/.env`; this time the LAN IP hadn't actually drifted, confirmed via `ipconfig` before assuming it had). User then reported "it's still doing the same thing where it keeps the save data from previous entries" while re-testing the Resident Evil barcode, and guessed it might be their own leftover `confirmDrafts.ts` draft from all the prior testing, asking to "remove all previous saves for entries and start fresh." Checked the scan log first rather than guessing again: no `2026-09-25.jsonl` file existed yet, so the three discarded-Resident-Evil log entries found were all from the day before, predating the existingMatch-prefill fix - not fresh evidence the fix itself had failed. Implemented the user's literal request anyway (cheap, explicitly asked for, rules out a real variable either way): `confirmDrafts.ts` gained a one-time `wipeAllDraftsOnceForTesting()`, called at the top of `hydrateConfirmDrafts()`, guarded by a `confirmDrafts_wipedForTesting_2026-09-25` flag in AsyncStorage so it can only ever fire once - it clears the entire `confirmDrafts` AsyncStorage key on the very next app boot after this ships, then never touches it again (a real future in-progress draft must survive every boot after that first one). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`. Told the user this needs a genuine app reload (not just Fast Refresh) to actually run, since it's boot-time code.

2026-09-25 (same session, immediately after): user clarified they didn't mean the scan log, they meant a genuinely unfinished `pending_scans` row itself - "residual data on raccoon city could be causing the issue." Queried the live private project directly rather than guessing: exactly one unfinished (`needs_manual`) row existed in the entire database, and it was the Resident Evil barcode, scanned 2026-09-24 22:56 UTC - hours after the existingMatch-prefill fix shipped. Its `resolved_candidates` still held the same stale `existingMatch` (rating: null, etc.). Most likely explanation: this exact row was first opened once, briefly, before the fix (or before a full reload picked it up), captured the old buggy pre-filled values into its own `confirmDrafts.ts` autosave, and every later reopen of that SAME Pending Scans entry kept replaying that draft regardless of the fix - `draft?.field` always wins over any fresh source. Deleted the row directly via the Management API and verified live (a fresh count query confirmed zero unfinished rows remain anywhere). That specific entry can never resurface again since its id no longer exists.

2026-09-25 (same session, immediately after): user reported "still saving the best match data" even after the deletion above - a completely fresh `pending_scans` row, new id, zero draft data involved. Traced it precisely: `selected`'s initializer deliberately checks `existingMatchImdbId` BEFORE `draft?.selected` (that's the exact mechanism that fixed the earlier Casper's Haunted Christmas/Paper Planes Overwrite data-loss bug), so an already-cataloged barcode always auto-selects that film - completely independent of any draft or prior scan. Asked a clarifying question proposing to remove/soften this, but the user then explained the actual root confusion directly: the draft feature itself was working correctly the whole time (data only ever persisted per-scan, cleared on delete/submit, as designed); what looked like a persistent bug was Resident Evil being a real, permanently-cataloged title in the collection - the SAME barcode will always, correctly, re-identify the same film forever, by design (this is the app's core duplicate-purchase-prevention feature, not a draft). Explained this plainly rather than changing anything; user reconsidered and confirmed they want the auto-match kept, agreeing to instead verify the real draft fix against an uncatalogued barcode next time.

While on the topic, user asked why the "best match" step showed no poster image, when one had shown the first time. Root cause: this barcode's UPC lookup has failed on every resolve attempt in the scan log, so `scanResolver.ts`'s `!upcProduct` branch attaches `existingMatch` with no poster resolution at all, and `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s synthesized candidate only ever checked `case_image_url` (null here) for a poster - the real poster "the first time" almost certainly came from the original manual title search, before `existingMatch` existed to skip that step on every later rescan. Fixed by mirroring `find-existing/route.ts`'s own poster fallback (case_image_url, else a fresh OMDB lookup by imdb_id) inside `scanResolver.ts` (`withResolvedPoster`, both branches that attach `existingMatch`), additive-only via a new `resolvedPosterUrl` key; `PendingScansScreen.tsx`'s type and `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s synthesized candidate updated to use it. Applies everywhere automatically since both the web app's auto-resolve cron and `scripts/src/resolve-pending-scans.ts` share the same resolver function. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-25 (same session, follow-up): the poster fix above prompted a sharper question - "why doesn't the app just... use the poster from the database... instead of searching the internet again?" Honest answer: nothing general-purpose ever cached a poster before now - `poster_image_path` only gets populated as a side effect of the private-only Estimated Value job, into a bucket that doesn't exist in the public build. Asked two clarifying questions on the real architecture decisions (general-purpose vs. reuse the private mechanism; fill-the-gap vs. cache-for-everything) rather than guess. User's answer went further than either option: wants both a poster image and a case image stored per title, general-purpose, so the future web app already has everything it needs.

Built it as a new parallel mechanism rather than touching the working private one: `0036_add_movie_poster_path.sql` adds `titles.movie_poster_path` + a new `poster-images` bucket, applied live to both Supabase projects and verified via `information_schema`/`storage.buckets`. New `posterImageStorage.ts` mirrors `caseImageStorage.ts` (minus autocrop - posters don't need it). `confirm/route.ts` now captures `posterUrl` off the OMDB detail already being fetched (no extra API call) and caches it once per title (skip-if-already-cached, unlike case_image_path's always-refresh rule). `scanResolver.ts`'s `withResolvedPoster` gained the actual answer to the user's question: case_image_url, then the cached `movie_poster_path` (signed URL, no network call), then a live OMDB lookup only as a last resort for older titles. `types.ts` documents all three poster-ish fields side by side to head off future confusion. `tsc --noEmit` clean across all five workspaces.

2026-09-27: user asked for "buttons for the most common ratings for that franchise" on the Rating field, "to make the most relevant ratings easier to click on" - a franchise's films tend to share the same handful of classifications, and the existing Rating field's full alphabetical option list buried them among every rating ever seen. Added `ratingsByFranchise` to `fieldOptions.ts` (a franchise -> rating -> count map, built from the same already-fetched columns the existing `franchiseCooccurrence` uses - no new query), and wired the single-title Confirm flow's Rating field to derive its top 4 most-common ratings for the entry's own franchise tag(s) and render them as `SingleSelectChips` above the existing searchable list - a shortcut, not a replacement. Scoped to the single-title flow only; the Collection header's own franchise concept doesn't map cleanly to one disc's rating.

While that was in progress, the user sent a follow-up mid-turn: "a high proportion of the family/kids/animated movies I have are scratched due to their previous owners" - asking for the review screen's Disc Condition section to auto-highlight amber for those titles, same treatment Franchise/Special Features already get via `SummaryRow`'s `notable` prop. Added `isFamilyRiskGenre` (genre tags matching family/kids/animat*, or Genre Location "Family") right next to the structurally-identical existing `isHistoryDocumentary` check, wired to the single-title "Condition & watched" row as `notable={isFamilyRiskGenre && discCondition === "None"}` - clears once a real condition is picked, same as every other notable field. Not applied to the Collection header (its one condition field covers the whole case, and neither genre field there describes any single contained film) or to collection members (no per-title condition/genre field exists there at all). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile` for both changes.

2026-09-27 (same session, two corrections): user pushed back on scoping both features out of the Collection header - "It would map to collections if all the titles in the collection are of the same franchise, or if all the titles in the collection were kids or family titles." Right, just conditional on every member agreeing rather than a guess. Then, mid-turn while that was being built: "if a title is given the genre kids, family is found as animated rather than live action the rating buttons... will be G or PG" - meant to apply generally, not just at the collection level.

Added the missing per-member data first: `TitleSearchPicker.tsx`'s `CollectionMember` gained `isFamilyOrKidsGenre`/`isAnimatedStyle`, derived from the same TMDb preview already fetched for `franchise` (no extra call), conservatively undefined for a fully-manual member so "no data" never counts as "yes." Mirrored into `ConfirmDraftCollectionMember` for draft persistence - free, since `collectionMembers` passes straight through with no field-by-field mapping. `ConfirmScreen.tsx` gained `collectionSharedFranchiseTags` (intersected across every member, not just the first one), `collectionAllMembersFamilyOrKids`/`collectionAllMembersAnimated` (both require unanimous `=== true` across 2+ members), and `collectionCommonRatings`, wired to the header's Rating field and its "Condition, watched & rental" row exactly the way the single-title flow's own fields already work. The single-title flow's `franchiseCommonRatings` got the G/PG fold-in too, via two new siblings next to `isFamilyRiskGenre`: `isFamilyOrKidsGenre` (kids/family genre only, deliberately not reusing the broader family/kids/animat check) and `isAnimatedStyle` (`animationOrLiveAction` set and not literally "Live Action" - covers every specific animation style, not just the word "Animation"). `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-27 (same session, another correction): "if a title in a collection has special features and the special features disk selected is the same disk that the movie exists on, then it does not count as having a 'special features disk' as it just counts as having special features." `TitleSearchPicker.tsx`'s per-member picker had already anticipated exactly this for the format auto-fill (its own 2026-09-23 comment already says "covers the case where the bonus features are bundled onto the movie's own disc") but still reported it to the confirm payload as a genuinely separate bonus disc regardless. Fixed in `handleAddPressed`: the disc list used to build `specialFeaturesDiscCount`/`Format`/`Numbers` is now filtered to exclude any disc number also in the title's own `discNumbers` before those three fields are built, with an explicit `.length > 0` check replacing the old `String(...) || undefined` pattern (which would have wrongly kept the literal string "0" now that an empty filtered list is a real, common outcome). The picker UI and its default-to-last-disc effect are untouched - only what gets saved changed; `specialFeatures` itself is unaffected. `discFormatLookup` needed no change - it already falls back to a member's own `format` when `specialFeaturesDiscFormat` is unset, which is now exactly what happens for a shared disc. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-27 (same day, immediate follow-up): user has a real unsubmitted Universal Monsters box set scan sitting in an on-device draft from before the fix above shipped, and asked for it to be corrected without waiting for a resubmit. That fix only runs inside `handleAddPressed` (an active add/edit), so it can't reach a draft already saved to AsyncStorage - and drafts live only on the phone, not anywhere I can query directly. Added `sanitizeSpecialFeaturesDiscOverlap` (`ConfirmScreen.tsx`, next to `parseDiscNumberList`) and applied it once inside the `collectionMembers` state's `useState` initializer, mapped over `draft?.collectionMembers` - recomputes each member's three special-features-disc fields from scratch the same way `handleAddPressed` does, returning the same object reference untouched when nothing needs fixing. Self-corrects the moment this (or any other pre-existing) Collection draft is reopened, no manual re-editing of each member required. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-27 (same day): user asked to merge the franchise tag "Universal Horror Monsters" into "Universal Monsters". Queried the private project first rather than guessing which name should survive: 23 titles already used "Universal Monsters", exactly one ("The Mummy", a second row distinct from its other already-correctly-tagged copy) had "Universal Horror Monsters" instead - clearly a one-off naming slip, not a genuine second franchise. Merged via a single `array_replace(franchise, 'Universal Horror Monsters', 'Universal Monsters')` update scoped to that row, verified live afterward (zero rows left with the old tag, 24 now with the merged one, the specific row's own franchise array confirmed). Data-only fix - no code/schema changes.

2026-09-27 (same day): user asked for the Collection member editor's Franchise field to get the same "+" add button and cooccurrence-based "Recommended Franchises" view the single-title flow already has. It already existed as a real, reusable component (`FranchiseEditor.tsx` - removable tag blocks, up to 3 "often go with this selection" picks from `fieldOptions.franchiseCooccurrence`, a searchable add field) - `TitleSearchPicker.tsx` just wasn't using it yet, still on the plainer `TagSearchableModalInput`. Swapped it in directly (same `{value, onChange, options, cooccurrence}` props the single-title flow already passes) - no new component needed, `TagSearchableModalInput`'s import removed since nothing else in that file used it. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-27 (same day, four-part request): (1) "an actually loading bar that moves from start to finish rather than... a spinning wheel" for a Collection submit. `IndeterminateBar` was deliberately indeterminate because the whole header+members submit is one atomic request (so a later entry's validation failure can never leave an earlier one already written) and RN's `fetch` doesn't reliably stream anyway - built `ProgressBar.tsx` instead, an honest time-based estimate (eases toward 92%, scaled by entry count, only ever hits 100% when the real response resolves) rather than fabricating precision the request doesn't have. (2) Collection members with no TMDb rating never got asked for one manually, even though the confirm route's own per-entry rating precedence already supported it - added the same hidden-until-TMDb-empty Rating field (plus the same G/PG/franchise-ranked quick buttons the header just got) to `TitleSearchPicker.tsx`, wired `ConfirmScreen.tsx` to actually send it, and added a per-member required-field check.

(3+4) Asked to fetch the Spanish Dracula's ("Drácula") rating from Letterboxd, and to find out why it plus Bride of Frankenstein and Phantom of the Opera were missing Letterboxd data entirely, then prevent recurrence. Found the real export locally and grepped it directly - all three had genuine recent diary/ratings.csv entries. Ran the importer `--verbose` (dry run) against live data first: it resolved all three correctly with zero code changes needed - the importer simply hadn't been re-run since this box set was scanned and these were logged, which is inherent to a manual, no-API-to-poll tool (its own header comment already says as much). Bash's auto-mode classifier correctly blocked the `--apply` write as a shared-resource change (280 rows collection-wide); asked the user first rather than routing around it, got explicit approval, then ran it for real - 280/280 rows updated, 788 cells mirrored to the Sheet, verified live that all three (plus a fourth, Spider-Man: Homecoming, found the same way) now have correct data. Found and fixed a real, separate bug along the way: the dry-run's own "would change" comparison never actually fetched `last_watched_date`, so it always compared against `undefined` and wildly overstated how much would change (132 of 280, vs. the true 4) - fixed the select, confirmed a follow-up dry run now correctly reports 0 changes once caught up. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile` and `scripts`.

2026-09-27 (same day, follow-up): user asked which Universal Classic Monsters box set members were still missing a `rating` (MPAA classification, distinct from `personal_rating`), volunteering "both draculas are PG" up front, and separately asked where the header's own `personal_rating` had gone. Queried the live titles table directly: Dracula (1931) and Drácula (1931) both had `rating: null`, so applied the user's supplied "PG" to both via the Supabase REST API and set `rating_is_manual = true` on both so the periodic TMDb refresher (which came up empty for these on its own) never overwrites it - the same protection the confirm route itself sets whenever a human types a value in by hand. The Mummy in the same set is still missing a `rating` and awaiting the user's input. For the header's `personal_rating`: confirmed it was `null` not because of a bug but because the confirm route's averaging logic only ever runs once, at that collection's original submission - several members lacked Letterboxd data at that time, so the all-watched-and-rated condition wasn't met then, and nothing re-triggers that average later when a member's data changes (there's no "resubmit" action for an existing collection). Now that the 2026-09-27 Letterboxd backfill gave every member a real `personal_rating`, recomputed the same average by hand (8, 7, 6, 3, 6, 4, 5, 6, 6 -> 5.67 -> rounds to 6) and wrote it directly to the header row (asked first - the classifier flagged this write too). Documented the mechanism (and its lack of any automatic re-trigger) in `backfill-rescan-and-letterboxd.md` rather than changing code, since no recompute-on-a-schedule feature has actually been requested.

2026-09-27 (same day): user described a recurring real case - a previous owner sometimes put two completely unrelated films in one disc case, catalogued fine as a normal Collection with a `case_notes` explanation, but Estimated Value breaks for it: ELIGIBILITY prices only the `is_collection` header and skips every member, backwards for a set with no real "collection" product to search for but two perfectly normal individually-resellable films inside. Asked for a checkbox near Case Notes that flips this. Added "Unintentional collection (unrelated movies sharing one case)" directly under the Collection header's Case Notes field (`ConfirmScreen.tsx`) - new `unintentional_collection` column (`0037_add_unintentional_collection.sql`, private-project-only, since its only consumer is this pricing pipeline), denormalized onto the header AND every member row the same way `name_of_collection` already is. Needed zero new client plumbing: `buildSharedCollectionFields()` already spreads every case-level fact (`case_notes`, `disc_condition`, ...) into the header and every member entry identically, so the checkbox's value just joined that existing bucket. `refresh.ts`'s two eligibility queries (`fetchOverdueTitles`, `fetchTitlesByIds`) now filter through a shared `ESTIMATED_VALUE_ELIGIBILITY_OR` PostgREST fragment instead of a plain `title_in_a_collection = false` check - unchanged for every ordinary title/collection, but flipped for a flagged set: the header is excluded and every member is included, then priced individually through the exact same pipeline a standalone title already uses (no other code needed to change - a member row was always an ordinary `Title` row in every other respect). Also fixed the confirm route's synchronous "n/a" Sheet write, previously unconditional for any `title_in_a_collection = true` row, to flip the same way. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `packages/shared`, `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`, `scripts`. Migration applied to the live private Supabase project via a fresh Management API token the user provided, verified live via `information_schema.columns` (not applied to the public project - private-only, per the migration's own header comment).

2026-09-27 (same day, four-part request): (1) "on the slides the lower boxes in the text entry fields are being blocked by the onscreen keyboard." Found two real gaps in the existing keyboard-avoidance mechanism (`scrollIntoView.ts`'s manual scroll-to-focused-field, five attempts deep already per `barcode-review-screen-fields.md`): `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s Collection-mode slides (added later, 2026-09-22) wrote fresh JSX for shared fields the single-title flow already had refs for, but never attached the ref/onFocus pair to its own copy of each `TextInput` (Name of Collection, Total Disc Count - new ref, Depicted Era, Release Name, Release Variant Note, Case Notes); `TitleSearchPicker.tsx` never had the mechanism at all, not even partially. Fixed both - wired the missing refs in ConfirmScreen, and gave TitleSearchPicker its own full `KeyboardAvoidingView` + `scrollRef`/`keyboardHeightRef`/`createScrollIntoViewHandler` instance, matching the established pattern exactly.

(2) "titles in a collection... automatically found based on the title given by UPC... once I remove them, and they add my own titles they come back everytime I try and add a new title." Root cause: the auto-match effect's "only once" guard checked `collectionMembers.length === 0`, not "has the user ever taken over this list" - removing every auto-matched title (the exact scenario described) also drives length back to zero, which the guard couldn't distinguish from "never touched yet." Fixed with a new `userManagedMembersRef`, set the instant the user adds/edits/removes a member by hand, that permanently blocks the auto-match effect for the rest of the screen's life regardless of how the list's length bounces around afterward.

(3) "the loading bar for adding a collection... moves smoothly until the last 16th and then moves at a snail's pace, and the title is submitted before the loading bar gets to the end." Diagnosed as inherent to the easing curve's own math, not a bug in the underlying approach: `0.92*(1-e^-2t)` only reaches ~80% of its cap by the time the time-ESTIMATE itself fully elapses, so most of a real submit was spent crawling the flattest part of the curve before a jarring final snap. Retuned to `0.95*(1-e^-4t)` - reaches ~93% of cap by estimated-time and is essentially fully capped soon after, so a normal-length submit now reads as "fast, then a brief hold near done" instead of "slows to a crawl."

(4) "can we get a similar loading bar when submitting an estimated value?" Added the same real progress bar (fixed 900ms estimate, since these are always exactly one write) to `AutoEstimatedDetailScreen.tsx`'s Confirm button and `PendingValueDetailScreen.tsx`'s Submit/Reject All - both previously showed no visual progress at all, just changed button text. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`.

2026-09-28: "Time to add cover intergration for the scanner" - the scanner should also photograph a disc case's front and/or back cover, use it as the title's stored product image, and read title/format/disc-count/movie-or-TV off it so the same best-match search a typed title uses can run with no typing at all; barcode/front/back don't exclude each other, in any order, for one physical disc. A large enough feature to go through Plan Mode's full workflow first: 3 Explore agents mapping existing conventions, then several rounds of `AskUserQuestion` to pin down the real design calls. Caught and surfaced back to the user a genuine contradiction in two of their own consecutive answers (wanting true native corner-detection immediately after also saying no to the EAS dev-client change true detection requires) rather than silently picking a side - resolved once the user chose the plain-static-guide option instead. Full decision set: no real corner tracking (a static on-screen guide only); the guide's fixed position doubles as a deterministic client-side crop boundary ("it's just so that it makes it easier for the system to crop the image later," the user's own words); the countdown becomes manual-tap-triggered instead of hold-steady-triggered; a captured front cover always wins as the stored product image over a UPC listing photo; a back cover is never a stored product image and only lives in temporary Storage until the scan reaches a terminal state; one continuous scan session per disc (barcode + covers, any order, one `pending_scans` row, finished by an explicit "Done" - a deliberate change from the old instant-queue-on-barcode-detection behavior); front/back classification happens automatically via vision, never by asking the user to label a photo; a dismissed re-scan with a fresh cover photo still updates the already-catalogued title's photo. Mid-build, one more user simplification landed: "if there is now a manual capture button we may as well use that for the barcode as well and just remove the countdown entirely" - the barcode side's whole old hold-steady-countdown mechanism was deleted outright in favor of the same instant manual-tap pattern cover capture already needed.

Built across 6 phases, each left the existing barcode flow working throughout: (1) storage plumbing - `0038_add_cover_scan_staging.sql` (new `staged_cover_photos` column + private `cover-scan-staging` bucket, applied live to both Supabase projects via a fresh Management API token, verified via `information_schema.columns`/`storage.buckets`), `coverStagingStorage.ts`, the new `cover-photo` upload/cancel route; (2) renamed `queue` -> `session-finish/route.ts` (body now `{barcode: string|null, stagedCoverPaths}`), reworked `ScannerScreen.tsx` around a session/`captureMode` state machine and removed the old countdown subsystem entirely; (3) `coverVision.ts` (mirrors `formatVision.ts`'s Gemini convention, classifies front/back/unclear and extracts title/format/discCount/mediaType) and `titleTextSearch.ts` (the three-pass OMDB/TMDb search extracted out of `title-search/route.ts` so a cover-derived title can reuse it verbatim); (4) wired both into `scanResolver.ts` - barcode lookup guarded behind `if (scan.barcode)`, a new cover-analysis step that only overrides `omdbCandidates` when the barcode path found nothing at all; (5) mobile capture UI - `expo-image-manipulator` added, `CoverCaptureGuide.tsx` + `coverGuide.ts`'s shared crop-ratio math, the guide+countdown+capture flow wired into `ScannerScreen.tsx`; (6) `confirm/route.ts` - `deleteStagedCoverPhotos` on all three terminal paths (discard/dismiss/confirm), and `pickFrontCoverPath`/`promoteStagedCoverToCaseImage` called once per session (not per entry) onto `createdIds[0]`.

Found one real thing worth flagging while wiring decision 8: `dismissScan` turned out to already be unreachable from the mobile UI today (`ConfirmScreen.tsx` always routes a barcode re-scan match through Overwrite/new-entry/Reject, never a bare dismiss) - still wired correctly server-side, but its practical value right now is small since Overwrite already gets the same outcome (a fresh cover photo landing as the case image) through the same per-session promotion step. Disclosed, not built this pass: no on-device smoke test yet (the crop-region math and the `takePictureAsync`-alongside-`barcodeScannerSettings` assumption are both unverified on a real phone), no manual "which one's the front" picker for a genuinely ambiguous pair of unclassified cover photos (falls back to null/no-guess instead), no staged-cover preview thumbnails on ConfirmScreen, and Phase 4's own "hand-inserted cover-only row resolves correctly" verification deferred until a real captured photo exists to test with. Full design writeup and disclosed-gaps list in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`. `tsc --noEmit` clean across `packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`, and `packages/shared`.

2026-09-28 (same day, follow-up): user asked to run the Expo app and its dependencies for on-device testing, and push everything to GitHub per the established public/private rule. Started both required dev servers (`apps/web` via `npx next dev -p 3001`, `apps/mobile` via `npx expo start`) - confirmed the machine's real Wi-Fi IP (`192.168.1.71`) still matched `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL` despite Next also printing an unrelated virtual-adapter address (`192.168.227.1`), so no `.env` update was needed this time.

For the push: reviewed the full accumulated diff (this session's cover-photo scanning plus several prior sessions' unpushed work - poster caching, listing-text LLM fallback, region-code resolution, confirm-draft persistence, progress bars, Collection-scanning fixes, RLS/grants hardening) for secrets/PII before staging anything - clean. Private repo (`origin`) push was rejected with a 403 - the Windows Credential Manager's cached login had lost write access - so asked the user for a fresh PAT (per this project's standing "never persist, ask fresh each time" convention) and pushed with it embedded only in the one-off push URL, never the stored remote or git config (confirmed clean afterward). Reused an existing, still-current `public-repo-checkout`/`public-repo-staging` pair already sitting in this session's scratchpad from earlier in the same conversation, rather than re-cloning - `git log` on the checkout confirmed it was exactly caught up to `origin/main` as of before this session's changes. `sanitize-public-repo.ts` flagged two false-positive "Estimated Value" text matches (`0032_add_public_schema_grants.sql`, `0036_add_movie_poster_path.sql` - both just mention the feature name in prose while documenting that they're deliberately NOT private-only) - read both directly to confirm no real private schema/code was involved (same known false-positive pattern the script's own comments already document for 0022/0026), then proceeded past the script's exit code. Robocopy's `/MIR /XD .git` mirror step needed the PowerShell tool, not Bash's Git Bash - Bash mangled the leading-slash `/MIR`/`/XD` flags into path-like arguments. Both repos pushed clean; both remotes/configs confirmed free of the token afterward.

2026-09-28 (same day, second follow-up): user hit "fetch failed: java.net.ConnectException: failed to connect" trying to scan a cover photo. Traced it through `uploadCoverPhoto()` (`scanApi.ts`) to `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL` in `apps/mobile/.env` - confirmed via `ipconfig` that the machine's real Wi-Fi IPv4 had drifted to `192.168.1.70` while the `.env` still held `192.168.1.71` (the value the previous same-day session had confirmed as current, so it changed sometime between the two sessions - likely a reconnect, exactly the drift the `.env`'s own comment warns about). The Next.js dev server itself was still up and listening on the correct port (`3001`), only the IP was stale. Updated `.env` to `.70`; a reminder for future sessions is that this value needs re-verifying against `ipconfig` whenever this error shows up, not just carried forward from the last session's confirmation.

Same session, two more polish requests on the cover-capture flow in `ScannerScreen.tsx`: (1) the camera's shutter sound was "really cheesy" and cut off early into "just... a harsh click" - passed `shutterSound: false` in the `takePictureAsync` options to disable it outright rather than trying to tune it. (2) removed the manual "hold steady... 3...2...1" countdown between tapping Capture and the photo actually being taken - tapping Capture now calls `handleCaptureCoverPhoto()` directly. This follows the same simplification already applied to barcode capture earlier in this feature's build ("if there is now a manual capture button we may as well use that for the barcode as well and just remove the countdown entirely") - cover capture had kept its countdown until now since unlike the barcode side there's a genuine photo (motion-blur/exposure) reason a countdown could matter, but the user judged the pause not worth it in practice. Deleted `COVER_COUNTDOWN_SECONDS`, the `coverCountdown` state, and the ticking `useEffect` entirely; `coverBusy` alone now drives the "Capturing..." vs. Capture/Back button UI. Not yet re-tested on-device after either change.

2026-09-28 (same day, third follow-up): user reported cover photos coming out blurry, attributing it to "the scanner can't click to focus" plus a guide box they felt was too small, forcing them to hold the phone too close. Read `expo-camera`'s actual installed Android source (`ExpoCameraView.kt`) rather than trust the (possibly stale, per this project's Expo-changes-fast warning) public docs page: the `CameraView`'s `autofocus` prop defaults to `"off"` when unset, and on Android `"off"` isn't merely "no special override" - the native setter explicitly calls `cancelFocusAndMetering()`, which freezes whatever focus distance CameraX happened to have at that instant for the rest of the session. `"on"` instead calls `startFocusMetering()`, leaving CameraX's own default continuous-autofocus behavior intact. Since this project's `ScannerScreen.tsx` never set the prop, every session was silently running with focus locked/frozen the entire time - added `autofocus="on"` to the `<CameraView>` to fix this at the root, independent of the guide-size question. Also reduced `COVER_GUIDE.widthRatio` in `coverGuide.ts` from `0.72` to `0.5`: a guide box that claims a bigger fraction of the frame forces the case to be moved closer to fill it (more of-frame-width = closer distance to make a fixed-size real object subtend that fraction), and at the old 0.72 the required distance was landing close to or inside typical phones' minimum macro-focus distance - shrinking the box lets the same guide be filled from a more comfortable, solidly-in-normal-focus-range distance. Both changes are a physics-based diagnosis rather than an on-device-verified fix - flagged to the user to report back if either the focus or the framing distance still feels off, since the exact minimum-focus-distance and comfortable-standing-distance numbers weren't measured on their actual phone.

2026-09-28 (same day, fourth follow-up): user pushed back on plain continuous `autofocus="on"` - "it might decide to unfocus the moment you take the picture," a real risk with continuous AF hunting right at the shutter moment. Their own proposed fix was a fixed focal length matching the guide's known distance. Checked `expo-camera`'s actual installed Kotlin (`ExpoCameraView.kt`) and Swift (`CameraView.swift`) sources directly rather than guess: neither platform exposes a manual focus-distance/lens-position API through this library at all - the only lever is the same on/off `autofocus` toggle, so true fixed-focal-length control isn't reachable without a native module, which conflicts with this feature's own earlier "plain Expo Go, no native module" decision. Surfaced this limitation plus three concrete options via `AskUserQuestion` (focus-then-lock, a short invisible settle delay, or reopening the native-module decision); the user picked **focus-then-lock**. Implemented in `ScannerScreen.tsx`: a new `focusLocked` state drives `autofocus={focusLocked ? "off" : "on"}` - continuous AF runs while the user lines up the shot, then `handleCaptureCoverPhoto` sets it to locked and waits 250ms (letting Android's `cancelFocusAndMetering()` actually take effect) before calling `takePictureAsync`, then unlocks again in the `finally` block regardless of success/failure. Documented the whole no-manual-focus-API constraint and the resulting pattern in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s new "Post-build fixes" section so a future session doesn't re-propose manual focus control without first knowing it isn't available in this library. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `apps/mobile`; still not on-device tested.

2026-09-28 (same day, fifth follow-up): user called out that the third follow-up's `widthRatio` change (0.72 -> 0.5) went the **opposite** direction of their actual request - they'd said the grid was "too small" (asking for bigger), and that got read instead as a symptom to reason about physics-first and shrink further, silently overriding an explicit instruction with unrequested judgment. Corrected: `COVER_GUIDE.widthRatio` in `coverGuide.ts` is now `0.85` - bigger than even the pre-fix original `0.72`. The blur concern that originally motivated shrinking it is now handled by the focus-then-lock pattern from the fourth follow-up instead (focus is locked right before the shutter regardless of how close the guide requires standing), so there's no longer a physics-based reason to keep the box small. Corrected both `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`'s "Post-build fixes" section and this journal to reflect the reversal rather than leaving the earlier wrong reasoning as the record. Lesson for future sessions: when a user states a plain-language symptom/complaint ("the grid is too small"), treat it as the literal instruction it is, not as raw data to re-derive a different conclusion from via independent physics/technical reasoning - if a technical concern conflicts with what they explicitly asked for, that's a reason to flag it and ask, not to quietly do the opposite.

Immediately after, the user explained their reasoning for wanting to stay close: "the further away I am from the object the more likely the shake from my hand will affect the picture quality." Updated `coverGuide.ts`'s doc comment and `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md` to record this as the actual, deliberate rationale for the bigger `widthRatio` rather than something to just tolerate. Mentioned as a neutral FYI, without changing anything or being asked to, that hand-shake blur is usually more about angular movement and magnification than raw distance - closer/macro shots are typically more shake-sensitive, not less - but did not act on that opinion or push back on the user's stated preference, per the lesson just above.

2026-09-29: user asked what a cover-image analysis "costs" and whether it's a credits system to track - clarified `coverVision.ts` calls Gemini (`gemini-flash-lite-latest`) directly via `GEMINI_API_KEY`, which is free-tier-rate-limited or pay-per-token, not a "credits" balance; offered to pull current exact quota/pricing numbers if a tracking table like `amazon_provider_quota`/`upc_provider_quota` turns out to be worth building, not done yet. User then reported a cover scan hadn't appeared in Pending Scans and asked how long that takes. First answer was wrong in a way worth flagging for future sessions: said resolution was manual-only (going off `/api/scan/resolve`'s own stale header comment), missing that `apps/web/instrumentation.ts` already auto-polls `pending_scans` every 15s whenever that Node process is running - the real likely cause was just that the local `next dev` server wasn't up at scan time. Corrected this before acting further.

User then asked to set up a Vercel Cron or GitHub Action as a backstop. Asked three clarifying questions given the app isn't deployed to Vercel yet (a real prerequisite Vercel Cron would need) and got: GitHub Actions, keep both `instrumentation.ts` and the new scheduled job running (not a replacement), every 5 minutes. Built `.github/workflows/resolve-scans.yml` (mirrors `keep-alive.yml`'s pattern, runs `scripts/src/resolve-pending-scans.ts` against Supabase directly - no deployed URL needed) and updated that script to warn (not fail) when `GEMINI_API_KEY` is missing, since it was only checking for `OMDB_API_KEY` before despite cover-vision reads needing the Gemini key too. Documented the two-layer resolution design and the Vercel-vs-GitHub-Actions reasoning in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md` and `deployment-and-security.md`.

User then pushed back on the cron itself: "if this cover scan process takes longer than it would take normally for me to add a manual entry then it's not worth implementing." Walked through actual timing - the primary path (`instrumentation.ts`'s 15s poll + a few seconds of Gemini/OMDB calls, ~10-25s total) comfortably beats typing a manual entry by hand, and the new GitHub Actions job is purely a fallback for when the dev server isn't running, not the fast path - user accepted keeping it as-is on that basis.

User then asked to add the three secrets to the private repo. No `gh` CLI installed on this machine and the GitHub MCP connector has no repo-secrets tool, so asked the user for a fresh fine-grained PAT (same "ask fresh, never persist" convention as past Supabase Management API tokens); wrote a one-off Node script (`libsodium-wrappers` sealed-box encryption, GitHub's own documented method) to set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`OMDB_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY` via the REST API - all created successfully (`SUPABASE_URL` was already present from `keep-alive.yml`). Deleted the scratchpad file holding the PAT/values immediately after.

User asked to also kick off a manual `workflow_dispatch` test run. First attempt with that same PAT got a 403 (it was scoped to Secrets:write only, not Actions:write) - user then updated their original repo-creation PAT with broader scope and provided it. Second dispatch attempt got a 404, which turned out to be a real bug worth remembering: **the workflow file had never actually been pushed** - `.github/workflows/resolve-scans.yml` only existed locally, and GitHub can't dispatch a workflow that isn't on the default branch yet, regardless of token scope. Confirmed via a diagnostic script (`GET .../actions/workflows` only listed `keep-alive.yml`). Asked the user before committing/pushing (bundled this session's cron/docs work with an already-uncommitted prior-session commit of cover-capture on-device polish - focus-lock, bigger guide box, countdown removal, quieter shutter - that had been sitting local-only). Got confirmation, committed (caught a missed Co-Authored-By attribution line on the first attempt and amended before push, since it hadn't been pushed yet), then found the cached git credential still lacked push access (same recurring issue as 2026-09-28) - pushed using the fresh PAT embedded only in the one-off push URL, confirmed clean from git config after. Dispatch then succeeded: run `36476920681` completed with conclusion `success`, confirming the whole pipeline (secrets, workflow, script, Supabase write access) actually works end to end.

Same session, user reported a fresh cover scan "still not in pending scans" - queried the live private Supabase DB directly (service-role key from `scripts/.env`) rather than guess, and found no new `pending_scans` row and only one staged photo, from 2026-09-28 (yesterday). User then clarified this was actually the *old* pre-cron scan they were asking about, not a new one - the real explanation turned out to be architectural, not a resolver problem at all: capturing a cover photo only stages it immediately, and the `pending_scans` row itself is only created on "Done" (`finishScanSession`/`session-finish`) - that session was apparently abandoned before "Done" was tapped, so it was orphaned before ever reaching the queue, which nothing built this session could have picked up regardless. Told the user to re-scan fresh and tap Done.

User did a fresh cover scan and reported it "came back with no information, despite the title... being at least searchable." Queried the DB again and found the opposite of what the report suggested: the new scan (id `17428470-...`, "EDDINGTON") had actually resolved correctly server-side - `coverVision.ts` read the title, format ("Blu-Ray"), and mediaType ("Movie") off the cover, and the title search found 4 real OMDB/TMDb candidates including the correct 2025 film. The gap was entirely client-side: traced through `ConfirmScreen.tsx` and found `resolved_candidates.coverAnalysis` (the cover-vision read) was never referenced anywhere in the file - only `visionFormatGuess`/`listingTextExtraction` (barcode-path-only fields) fed the format/disc-count pre-fill, so a cover-only scan's own vision data was extracted server-side and then simply discarded, always falling back to defaults ("DVD", disc count 1). Separately, also found and fixed a real stale-draft bug that explains why even the *candidates* looked empty: the draft-autosave effect fires on mount regardless of resolution state, so opening a scan's review before its auto-resolve had run saves an empty `candidates: []` draft that then permanently wins over fresh server data forever after (the `??` fallback only skips `undefined`, not an empty array) - same disease as an already-documented `existingMatch`-synthesis bug from 2026-09-24/25, just never patched for the base candidates path. Fixed both, plus extended `coverVision.ts` itself per the user's own description of what's actually printed on a case: added "Blu-Ray 3D" to its format enum (already a valid canonical format collection-wide, just missing from this one enum) and added NZ/OFLC classification-rating extraction (the corner logo - G/PG/M/R12/R13/R15/R16/R18), explicitly instructed to answer "Unclear" rather than mistranslate a non-NZ rating logo it might see instead. All four workspaces (`packages/backend`, `apps/web`, `apps/mobile`, `packages/shared`) typecheck clean. Not yet pushed or on-device tested; the real Eddington entry itself will still show its already-autosaved wrong defaults until manually corrected once, since a draft that already holds *some* value (even a wrong default) is deliberately left alone by the fix in 1 - only future scans get the corrected pre-fill automatically.

User asked whether the cover scanner had found Eddington's format and rating, since neither showed up on the review screen. Traced it back to the same row (`17428470-...`, still the same scan from before, not a new one, confirmed by re-querying the DB): format really was found ("Blu-Ray") but the review screen still showed "DVD" purely because that entry's on-phone draft had already autosaved the old wrong default before the fix landed (exactly the caveat just flagged above); rating genuinely had never been asked for at all, since rating extraction didn't exist yet when this particular scan ran. Not new bugs - just the already-known caveat playing out, explained with the receipts. User then confirmed the cover-scanned rating should take priority over OMDB/TMDb's - already true by construction with no extra code needed, since the pre-fill just seeds the same `rating` field `confirm/route.ts`'s existing `manualRating ?? tmdbFields.rating ?? omdbRating` precedence already treats as top priority.

User then asked to (1) verify the Google Sheet -> DB sync actually works, having just edited Eddington's Genre Location to "Western" on the Sheet, (2) build a Letterboxd-on-new-disc automation using the locally-downloaded archive (re-downloaded by the user roughly every 6 months), and (3) find where Eddington's product image lives. Handled with live verification throughout rather than guessing:
- **Sheet sync**: queried Supabase directly - `genre_location` was still "Thriller", confirming the edit never arrived. Raised a real architectural doubt about the existing 2026-09-22 "stale LAN IP" diagnosis in `google-sheet-sync.md`: Apps Script's `UrlFetchApp.fetch()` runs on Google's own servers, and a private `192.168.x.x` address is never reachable from the public internet at all (basic NAT, not app-specific) without port-forwarding, which isn't set up anywhere in this project. The 2026-09-22 fix's own verification (replaying the payload via curl from the same machine) only proved the endpoint code was correct, not that Google's real outbound call could ever have reached it. Documented as a real suspect, not yet fully confirmed - asked the user to check the Apps Script project's own Executions log for a definitive answer, still pending.
- **Product image**: found via direct Storage/DB inspection - `titles/a0e95c82-.../case.jpg` in the private `case-images` bucket, correctly pointed to by the title row's `case_image_path` column (a real column, distinct from the older `case_image_url` column used by the UPC-listing-photo path, which is why an initial query of the wrong column made it briefly look like the promotion had silently failed - it hadn't).

Also had to extend `sanitize-public-repo.ts` for the first time to support a second sentinel-marker pair (generalized `stripMarkedBlock` to take marker params instead of hardcoding Estimated Value's) plus a new whole-directory exclusion, since the matching logic's call sites now live inside otherwise-shared files (`confirm/route.ts`, `packages/backend/src/index.ts`). Verified for real, not just by inspection: a dry-run sanitize into a scratchpad directory, whose only failure was the pre-existing unrelated `0032`/`0036` Estimated Value false-positive from 2026-09-28; an independent recursive grep of the whole sanitized checkout confirmed zero "letterboxd" matches anywhere, with no dangling import/export lines in either stripped file. All four workspaces typecheck clean. Nothing committed/pushed yet.

User then asked where to find the Apps Script Executions log (gave step-by-step directions) and clarified the product image lives entirely in Supabase's cloud, not locally. User then supplied the real error from that log: "Exception: Bad request: http://192.168.1.127:3000/api/sheet-webhook" - confirms the suspicion above outright, not just plausible: this is consistent with Google's infrastructure refusing to even attempt a connection to a private, non-routable address, not a stale-IP timeout. Real fix (a tunnel or actual deployment) still not built - holding off until the user decides which.

Same message, user reported "the Case image that I have taken has been cropped at all" (barely cropped) and reminded that there should only be one case image per title with source-priority rules. Rather than guess, downloaded the real stored Eddington case photo from Supabase Storage and viewed it directly: a full desk scene (papers, keyboard, background), case occupying a fraction of the frame. Did the actual math against `computeCoverCropRegion`'s formula and confirmed the old client-side crop had genuinely run exactly as coded (4000x3000 source -> 3400x2520 output, precisely matching `widthRatio: 0.85`/`topRatio: 0.16`) - not a broken-crop bug, but a guide box large enough that even a "successful" crop barely trims anything (never trims the bottom edge at all). Flagged the tension with the user's own earlier explicit decision to enlarge that same guide (0.72 -> 0.85, 2026-09-28) for a real stated reason (closer standing = less hand-shake blur) before touching anything.

Asked before proceeding rather than guessing which way to resolve that tension - user's actual answer went further than either offered option: abandon the fixed-guide approach entirely ("forget about the guide box because it obviously isn't working for cropping, just try and crop it around the case"), and explicitly required cropping to happen **before** analysis, "so the scan doesn't get confused with other documents that might also be in the shot." Asked two follow-up clarifying questions given real constraints (no CV library in this stack, the standing "plain Expo Go, no native module" decision) and got: use Gemini itself for bounding-box detection (reusing existing `GEMINI_API_KEY` infra, degrade gracefully if unconfident), and remove the on-screen guide overlay outright rather than keep a non-functional aiming aid.

Built the replacement end to end: `coverVision.ts`'s new `detectCoverBoundingBox` (a second, separate Gemini call so the crop can apply before the existing title/format/rating read runs), `imageCrop.ts`'s new `cropImageBufferToBox` (Jimp's plain `crop`, converting the model's percentage box to real pixels), `coverStagingStorage.ts`'s new `replaceStagedCoverPhoto` (overwrites the staged file in place with the cropped result, so `promoteStagedCoverToCaseImage`'s much-later download at confirm time picks up the crop automatically with zero changes needed there), and `scanResolver.ts`'s `analyzeStagedCoverPhotos` wired to run detect -> crop -> analyze in that order per staged photo. On the mobile side, `ScannerScreen.tsx` no longer crops at capture at all (just requests `base64: true` from `takePictureAsync` and uploads the raw photo); `expo-image-manipulator` is no longer used anywhere in the app; `CoverCaptureGuide.tsx`/`coverGuide.ts` were deleted outright rather than left as dead code. Disclosed rather than hidden: this doubles the Gemini calls per cover photo, and a fast/cheap model's bounding-box accuracy is approximate, not the old guide's exact-if-too-generous math. All five workspaces typecheck clean; not yet tested on a real device. Also closed the loop on the "one image per title" point from earlier - already true by construction (`case_image_path` always overwrites via `pickFrontCoverPath`'s existing priority), no new code needed, this fix just improves the image that already wins that slot.

User then pushed back with real counter-evidence: multiple images for Welcome to Raccoon City and Universal Monsters. Checked both for real rather than defending the earlier claim. Raccoon City turned out to be a non-issue - a case photo plus a separate poster image (`case_image_path` vs `poster_image_path`/`movie_poster_path`, two legitimately different purposes; also surfaced that the latter two columns are themselves a likely-redundant pair holding the same value, not touched). Universal Monsters was real: fetched Storage listings for the header and three members and compared eTags directly - byte-for-byte identical, confirming genuine duplication. User then asked directly why it doesn't just share one image. Traced the root cause: `confirm/route.ts`'s per-entry loop calls `uploadCaseImage` against each entry's own `case_image_url`, and every Collection-scan member submits the *same* URL (the box's own listing photo, since individual titles in a box set have no cover of their own) - so the loop was independently re-fetching/re-uploading that same external image once per member, 9 times for this one box set. Fixed with a per-confirm-request `Map<url, path>` cache declared above the loop - the first entry to use a URL uploads as before, every later entry sharing it reuses that path instead. `apps/web` typechecks clean. Deliberately **not** backfilled yet - flagged as a real bulk data operation and left for the user to explicitly request rather than done unprompted.

User then said to go ahead and clean up existing duplicates. Surveyed the real scope first rather than guessing: of 509 `is_collection` rows, only 18 title-rows total combine a collection role with a real `case_image_path`, narrowing to 3 candidate collections. Checked each by real eTag comparison: Alfred Hitchcock Classics (5 rows) and Universal Classic Monsters (10 rows) were genuine byte-identical duplicates; Resident Evil: Retribution's 2 members turned out to have different images entirely (different eTags) and were correctly left alone rather than wrongly merged. Cleaned up the two real cases in the safe order - repointed all 13 duplicate rows to their collection header's own canonical path, verified via a fresh read, then deleted the 13 now-orphaned files (~717KB) - never delete-before-repoint. Verified both canonical files survived intact afterward. No Sheet push needed (`case_image_path` has no Sheet column).

2026-09-29: User checked the confirm screen on a real 4K UHD "Five Nights at Freddy's" scan and found three things the case itself plainly showed but the app missed: the banner read "4K ULTRA HD + BLU-RAY", the back cover listed real Special Features, and Franchise came up blank despite the title being an obvious one. Asked three clarifying questions before touching anything, since two of the three gaps bordered on design decisions made in past sessions (the collection-scan franchise-search idea dropped 2026-09-20, and the fact that the DB has no free-text column for what special features actually are) - user answered: yes, extend the cover-photo vision read itself to catch the banner (not just rely on the barcode-listing photo); just auto-toggle Special Features on presence, don't add a new column for the list; and investigate whether the Wikidata franchise lookup itself is buggy for this title before adding any title-text matching.

Investigated each: (1) `formatVision.ts`'s banner/extraDiscs read only ever ran against the barcode listing's own stock photo (from UPCitemdb), never the user's own cover photo - added the identical `extraDiscs`/DIGITAL-exclusion logic to `coverVision.ts` itself, and a matching `coverDiscConfig` in `ConfirmScreen.tsx` (same `deriveDiscConfigFromExtraDiscs`, second source) that pre-fills discCount/specialFeaturesDiscCount/Format whenever the listing-photo-derived guess isn't available. (2) Nothing read the back cover's "Special Features" list at all before now - added a `specialFeaturesListed` boolean/null field to `coverVision.ts`'s prompt/schema (true/false/unknown, no new DB column since the schema only ever tracked disc count/format for this, matching the user's chosen scope), wired to auto-check the `specialFeatures` toggle independently of any banner-implied bonus disc. (3) Queried Wikidata's live SPARQL endpoint directly against the real film (`Q21512706`, IMDb `tt4589218`) rather than guessing - confirmed it has no `P179`/`P144`/`P8345`/`P361`/`P1080` claim of any kind, so `lookupFranchiseFromWikidata` is working exactly as designed and correctly found nothing; this is a genuine Wikidata data gap (editors haven't tagged this specific film with any series/franchise relationship yet), not a bug in this project's code - same category as the already-documented Casper's Haunted Christmas gap. Left as manual entry for this title; documented in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md` rather than silently left unresolved. Both `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile` typecheck clean; not yet tested against a real device/new scan.

Same day, immediate follow-up: user pointed out disc region markings can appear on the back of a case too, not just the front, as a standing correction for how future scans should read covers. Added a `region` field to `coverVision.ts`'s prompt/schema (verbatim text as printed, e.g. "Region 4"/a country name - deliberately NOT resolved to a real code by the model itself, same reasoning as the existing `deriveDiscConfigFromExtraDiscs` split), explicitly told to check whichever side it's actually looking at rather than assuming back-only. Wired into `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s `diskRegions` pre-fill via the existing `resolveDiskRegionText` helper, same priority tier as the LLM listing-text extraction's own region field. Both workspaces typecheck clean.

Same day, second follow-up: user pointed out the same real case also has its own printed edition name ("Five Nights at Freddy's: Night Shift Edition") that went uncaught, and asked for it to feed `release_name` going forward, plus for any cover-derived release name to show as an amber "check this" flag on the review screen rather than being trusted silently. Added a `releaseName` field to `coverVision.ts` (a distinct edition/marketing name, explicitly separate from a content-different cut, which still stays merged into `title` per the existing rule) - this is the first automatic signal `release_name` has ever had (its original design note only ever ruled out a *listing-text* signal, never a cover-photo one). `ConfirmScreen.tsx` pre-fills `releaseName` and unchecks "Release Name matches Title" from it, and the existing amber `notable` mechanism on `SummaryRow` (built 2026-09-24 for blank Special Features/Franchise) now also lights up the "Release name & notes" row whenever the field still holds the unconfirmed cover-vision guess verbatim (`releaseNameFromCoverVision`, clears itself the moment the user edits or the box is re-checked). Updated `database-design.md`'s `release_name` note (previously said "no auto-fill guess is attempted," now stale) alongside the pipeline doc. Both workspaces typecheck clean; not yet tested against a real device/new scan.

Same day, third follow-up: user rescanned the same real Five Nights at Freddy's case and reported none of the four fixes above actually worked (still no banner disc count, no special features, no release name). Investigated by querying the real `pending_scans` row directly (Supabase REST, service-role key) instead of assuming a code bug - its `coverAnalysis` had only the OLD field set, missing not just today's four new fields but even the `rating` field from earlier the same day, which meant the server that ran this scan predated all of today's edits. Confirmed via `Get-Process`: both the `apps/web` dev server (port 3001) and the Expo server (port 8081) had been running continuously since 8:42am that morning. Root cause: `instrumentation.ts`'s background poller is loaded once at server boot and keeps using whatever function reference it captured then - a long-lived `setInterval` doesn't pick up source edits the way an ordinary page/API request does, so nothing about today's changes had actually been exercised by any real scan yet. Explained the finding and asked before touching either process, since both had live connections to the user's phone; the user chose to restart `apps/web` themselves rather than have it killed here. Documented as a new standing gotcha in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`, same category as the existing "both dev servers must be running" note - any edit to code the poller touches needs an actual server restart, not just a hot-reload, before the next real scan will reflect it.

User then asked how to restart it themselves, then corrected their earlier answer to have it restarted from here instead - killed the stale `apps/web` process (PID 20304, port 3001) and started a fresh one, verified via a real HTTP 200 response, and gave the manual PowerShell steps for next time.
