Same session, user asked for a new feature: if a cover scan reveals "collection" in the title, default ConfirmScreen into its Collection form, with a way back to the normal form if it's a false positive. Investigated first and found the "way back" already exists (the Collection checkbox toggle, always overridable) - the real gap was that `scanResolver.ts`'s `isCollection` flag only ever checked a UPC barcode listing's title text (`looksLikeCollection`), never a cover photo's own vision-read title, and wasn't even computed at all on the cover-only or failed-UPC-lookup paths. Added `coverLooksLikeCollection` (checked against whichever staged photo's title is available, OR'd into the same `isCollection` flag on all three resolution paths) to `scanResolver.ts`. Mid-turn, user pointed out a box set's cover often lists its own member titles too - added `collectionMemberTitles` to `coverVision.ts`'s prompt/schema, told to trust the cover's own real visual separation (columns/lines/thumbnails) rather than guessing where one title ends and the next begins from comma-split text (the user's own correction: a naive "wrong split" risk is much smaller than assumed, since real box-set backs are laid out with genuine separation, not a run-on sentence). Asked directly whether these should auto-add or just suggest - user chose suggestions: `ConfirmScreen.tsx`'s "Titles in this set" step now shows each cover-read title as a tappable chip (`coverMemberTitleSuggestions`, filtered against titles already added), and `TitleSearchPicker` gained a new `initialQuery` prop (pre-fills and auto-runs the search once, mount-only) so tapping a chip lands straight on real candidates to review rather than auto-trusting the vision read. Also folded a second signal into `coverLooksLikeCollection`: the cover listing 2+ member titles is itself proof of a collection even when the box's own name uses none of `looksLikeCollection`'s trigger words. Restarted the `apps/web` dev server afterward (per the standing `instrumentation.ts` poller gotcha - it doesn't pick up source edits without a real restart) so this is actually live for the next test scan. Full details in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`. `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile` both typecheck clean; not yet tested against a real device/new scan.

Same session, immediate follow-up: the user's very next real scan (a "Gidget Film Collection" box set, no barcode listing) hit two real bugs from the feature just built. (1) Tapping the pending scan crashed with "Cannot read property 'some' of undefined" - `coverMemberTitleSuggestions` referenced `collectionMembers` before its own `useState` declaration several hundred lines later in the same component, a real use-before-declaration bug TypeScript didn't flag (it's inside a `.filter()` callback) but which threw at runtime since Metro's transpilation doesn't enforce TDZ. Fixed by moving the computation after `collectionMembers`'s declaration. (2) The Pending Scans list showed the plain word "Collection" instead of the real name - `scanResolver.ts`'s cover-only and failed-UPC-lookup branches never actually applied the established "never OMDB-search a collection's own title" rule (only the full barcode+UPC branch did), so `searchTitleCandidates(supabase, "Gidget Film Collection")` ran anyway and OMDB's fuzzy search, finding no full-phrase match, fell back to matching just the word "Collection" - returning real but unrelated films literally titled "Collection"/"The Collection"/"La Collection", which `scanDisplayTitle` then picked as the list preview. Fixed both branches to skip the OMDB search once `coverLooksLikeCollection` is true (status correctly stays `needs_manual`, matching the existing branch's own behavior), and added a defensive cover-derived-title fallback to both `scanDisplayTitle` (`PendingScansScreen.tsx`) and `manualTitle`'s initial state (`ConfirmScreen.tsx`), since with no barcode/UPC listing at all neither had anywhere else to fall back to. Reset the already-broken real row to `status: "pending"` so the poller could re-resolve it with the fix; verified live afterward - `omdbCandidates: []`, `status: "needs_manual"`, `isCollection: true`, all correct. Restarted `apps/web` again (this fix landed after the previous restart, so the poller still needed a fresh one). Full details in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`. `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile` both typecheck clean.

Same session, next follow-up (same Gidget scan): user asked why the scan "could not find" the "2 DISC SET" text in the cover's bottom-right corner - it actually had (`coverDiscCountGuess: 2`, confirmed in the real data on both front and back analysis), but nothing wired it into the Collection header's own `collectionDiscCount` field - only the single-title flow's `discCount` field had that fallback. Corrected to union every staged photo's own list, deduping case/whitespace-insensitively rather than trusting a single side. User then clarified the real cause: the front cover's own printed list actually named all 4 titles too - a physical rating/retailer sticker on that case was covering the 4th one in the photo, which the back cover (no sticker over that spot) read cleanly - confirming the union-merge fix is the right general policy, not a one-off patch, since a sticker/glare/crease can obscure a different, unpredictable part of either side on any given scan. Restarted `apps/web` again and reset the real row to re-resolve, but by the time it ran the row had been discarded from the queue entirely (no matching `titles` row either) - unclear whether by the user or some other path, disclosed rather than claimed as verified end-to-end; the fix itself is confirmed correct by code inspection and both `packages/backend` and `apps/mobile` typecheck clean. Full details (all three follow-ups) in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`.

2026-09-30: User's next two real scans were classic Doctor Who serials, and the first ("The Underwater Menace") surfaced a bug the user had already been warned would eventually need solving - the "deferred nested-serial-in-a-show logic" noted as explicitly-not-needed-yet back on 2026-09-19. Now it was needed: the scan correctly read the serial's real title off the cover but classified it as a plain Movie instead of the classic-serial TV convention. Root cause verified live against the real OMDB API: classic-era (1963-1989) Doctor Who serials are nested as individual episodes under the classic show's own IMDb id (`tt0056751`) and are NOT findable through OMDB's `s=` title-search endpoint at all - searching "Underwater Menace" returned zero real hits and instead false-matched an unrelated 1969 short film ("Look at Life: Underwater Menace", Type "movie"), which `scanDisplayTitle`/`manualTitle` then picked up.

User gave three rounds of clarification building the fix, each explicitly scoped to classic Doctor Who only, never generalized to "every OMDB episode-Type match" (a plain modern-show single-episode disc already guesses "TV Episode" correctly as-is): (1) detection trigger is title text containing "Doctor Who" AND the matched entry's own year being before 1990, to tell a classic-era cover apart from a same-named modern-era (2005+) story; (2) the year signal comes from the same one-time cached list being built, not a separately-extracted cover year; (3) Season 23 ("The Trial of a Time Lord") is kept as four separate serials (Mysterious Planet/Mindwarp/Terror of the Vervoids/Ultimate Foe) rather than one merged 14-part entry, "resolved the same way the Key to Time season is" (Season 16's six serials also stay separate rather than merging into one mega-serial).

Built the full classic-era index: `supabase/migrations/0039_classic_who_serial_index.sql` (a `classic_who_serial_index` table + pg_trgm fuzzy-search RPC, same pattern as `tmdb_title_index`/0006, applied to the private Supabase project only since the public demo build has no Doctor Who titles at all - same precedent as 0021's estimated-value tables), and `scripts/src/build-classic-who-serial-index.ts` (`npm run build-classic-who-serial-index`), which hand-verifies all 26 seasons/158 serials' title/episode-count/year against real documented broadcast history and then cross-checks the total against OMDB's own per-season episode count live before trusting it - every single season reconciled exactly. A web-fetched Wikipedia summary was tried first for this breakdown but turned out unreliable past Season 12 (duplicated Season 19's lineup into Season 21, mixed up the final seasons), so it was discarded in favor of the OMDB-cross-checked hand-built list instead of risking bad data in a table nothing else would ever double-check. New `packages/backend/src/classicWhoSerials.ts` (`matchClassicWhoSerial`) gates on both conditions above and, when they hit, returns the matched serial as the sole candidate (bypassing the generic search entirely) using its own clean story title rather than OMDB's awkward "Story: Episode 1" per-episode title text. Wired into `titleTextSearch.ts`'s `searchTitleCandidates` (shared by both the automatic cover-photo/barcode resolver and manual typed search) and, separately, into `scanResolver.ts`'s UPC-listing-title search path, since a UPC listing's own title text can hit the identical false-match problem. Verified live end-to-end against the real "Underwater Menace" case and a same-named modern-era title (correctly excluded by the year gate) and a plain non-Doctor-Who title (correctly untouched). `packages/backend`/`scripts` both typecheck clean.

Mid-session, unrelated bug report: "the product images in the supabase bucket aren't cropped, it appears the cropping only happened to the Eddington disk." Downloaded and visually compared several real stored `case-images` - confirmed several titles confirmed the same day as Eddington (Piece by Piece, Madame Web, TMNT) all have completely raw, uncropped phone photos as their case image (full background clutter - desk, papers, a keyboard, a leg - all still in frame), while only Eddington got the tight content-aware crop. Root cause verified live by calling Gemini directly and inspecting the raw response: `detectCoverBoundingBox`'s schema only ever marked `"found"` as `required` - `xMin`/`yMin`/`xMax`/`yMax` were nullable and optional, so the model frequently omitted `xMax`/`yMax` outright (confirmed on 3 of 4 real re-tested images), and separately, sometimes reverted to Gemini's own native 0-1000 bounding-box training scale instead of the 0-100 percentages the prompt asked for (e.g. Madame Web: `xMin: 192, yMin: 155`) - either failure tripped the existing `xMax > 100`/missing-field validation and silently fell back to "no crop," which is why this looked like an occasional miss when it was actually failing on nearly everything except Eddington's one accidentally well-formed response. Fixed by adding all four coordinates to the schema's `required` array (forces the model to always emit them) and adding `normalizeBoxScale` (rescales a 0-1000-shaped response down to 0-100 rather than discarding it) in `coverVision.ts`. Re-tested live against all four broken real images plus Eddington - all five now return complete, correctly-scaled boxes; visually confirmed the fix by cropping Piece by Piece's real stored photo with the corrected box, which now frames the case tightly. Built `scripts/src/backfill-recrop-case-images.ts` (dry-run/`--apply`, pre-filters by file size so the ~28 already-clean UPC-sourced product photos are never even downloaded) and ran it for real: 8 titles recropped (One from the Heart, Gidget Film Collection, Five Nights at Freddy's, Madame Web, TMNT, Emilia Pérez, Piece by Piece, Ant-Man and the Wasp), 28 already clean, 1 (Eddington) already tight. `packages/backend`/`scripts` typecheck clean.

Immediate follow-up: "Why is the underwater menace still matching to Movie instead of TV?" - the fix from earlier in this session was correct, but `apps/web` had been running since before it landed (the `instrumentation.ts` poller doesn't hot-reload - the recurring gotcha this project keeps hitting). Confirmed live: the real pending scan's `omdbCandidates` showed a TMDb-sourced "movie" match with a real poster URL, not the expected `Type: "episode"`/`Poster: "N/A"` classic-serial candidate. Restarted `apps/web`, reset the real scan to `pending`, and verified it re-resolved correctly this time.

Immediate follow-up, three more corrections, all explicitly scoped to Doctor Who only (never generalized): (1) "it is not TV episode it is a selection of TV episodes, so go with TV Series instead" - `omdbTypeToMovieOrTv` and the client-side `guessMovieOrTvFromType` both now check for a classic Doctor Who match (`detail.seriesID`/`candidate.classicWhoSerial`) ahead of the generic OMDB Type mapping. (2) "the season number and episode count can also be filled into the text fields" - new reactive prefill in ConfirmScreen.tsx from the same cached index data, plus Genre Location prefilled to "BOX TV Sci-Fi" (see next point). (3) "the message that tells the user what DVDs it should be put ahead of or in front of will change for doctor who serials so that they are put on the shelf in order of release instead of alphabetically" - new `who_shelf_order` column and `computeShelfLocation` branch, gated on `genre_location === "BOX TV Sci-Fi"` (the user's own dedicated Doctor Who shelf, confirmed via two clarifying questions: it's currently empty/unused by anything else, and the user gave explicit placement rules for the two hard cases - a multi-era "Assorted" compilation always sorts first, and an individual serial sorts directly before the season box set it belongs to). Backfilled all 18 pre-existing Doctor Who titles by hand (cross-checked against the real OMDB-verified classic-serial index, catching a real data quirk along the way - "the Collection Season 20"'s own `episode_count` is the season's story count, not its episode total) via `scripts/src/backfill-doctor-who-shelf-order.ts`, run for real: 18/18 rows updated, genre_location mirrored to the Sheet. Full technical details (the exact ordering scheme, all three new columns, both new/updated scripts) in `barcode-scanning-pipeline.md`. All five workspaces typecheck clean; verified live end-to-end (OMDB detail fetch -> classic-index lookup -> correct `who_shelf_order`) against the real Underwater Menace case, though the user hadn't yet tapped Confirm on their phone by session's end.

Follow-up (same scan, next message), three more real issues in one report:

1. **"I scanned one barcode but used 6 UPC lookups, then 9 after refreshing"** - root cause was in `apps/web/instrumentation.ts`, not the scan-submission flow at all: the resolver poller used a plain `setInterval` firing unconditionally every 15s, with no check for whether the previous tick's own async work had already finished. A cover-photo scan's resolution (sequential rotate + bounding-box + text-read Gemini calls per staged photo, plus the UPC lookup and OMDB/TMDb search) can easily exceed 15s, so an overlapping tick's own `status = "pending"` query happily found the SAME still-unresolved row and reprocessed it - spending a second real UPC lookup (and repeating every other API call) for as many overlapping ticks as it took to finish. Confirmed live: `upc_quota_status` genuinely showed 9/100 spent for what should have been one lookup. Fixed by converting all three of `instrumentation.ts`'s intervals (auto-resolve, tmdb-refresh, estimated-value-refresh) from `setInterval` to self-rescheduling `setTimeout` (next run only scheduled from inside `finally`, after the current one fully settles) - makes two runs overlapping the same row impossible. Only the auto-resolve one has actually been observed overlapping, but the same `setInterval` hazard existed in all three, so all three got the same fix rather than leaving two known-vulnerable copies in place.
2. **"The title shouldn't be 'The Underwater Menace Season 4', just 'The Underwater Menace', this only applies to Doctor Who serials"** - the new Season No. auto-fill (from this same session, above) collided with the pre-existing "Title + Season N" composition effect (2026-09-19, built for an ordinary show where OMDB's title is just the bare series name and needs a season suffix to tell discs apart). A classic Doctor Who serial's own matched title is already the complete, correct catalogue title - `composeSeasonTitle` (ConfirmScreen.tsx) now returns the base title unchanged whenever the selected candidate carries `classicWhoSerial`, before ever considering Season No./Part composition.
3. **"Why did it not pick up the region when the region was on the back of the case?"** - the region-guess logic itself (`coverRegionGuess` -> `resolveDiskRegionText`) was verified correct by inspection and a direct test (`resolveDiskRegionText("UK", "Blu-Ray")` -> "B", `("Region Free", ...)` -> "All", both correct). Real cause: `getConfirmDraft(scan.id)` is an in-memory draft cache keyed by the scan's own id that outlives a code fix or server-side reprocessing (by design, so in-progress edits survive Fast Refresh) - this exact scan's status was reset and reprocessed multiple times during this session's testing while the app was open, so if the confirm screen was opened even once before the back-cover region data was fully correct, an empty `diskRegions: []` draft got cached and, since the code treated *any* existing draft as fully authoritative, permanently blocked the fresher guess from ever applying. Same class of bug `candidates` already had a carve-out for (2026-09-something, "an empty draft must never permanently mask fresh server data") - applied the identical fix to `diskRegions`: an empty draft array no longer counts as "a draft exists," so a genuine region guess can still win.

All three disclosed plainly as real, found-live bugs (not guessed) - the first two reproduced and fixed by direct code inspection plus a live quota check, the third diagnosed by code-path tracing since the actual mobile draft cache isn't remotely inspectable. `apps/web`/`apps/mobile` typecheck clean; `apps/web` restarted with the poller fix.

2026-10-01: Session opened by restarting both dev servers after finding neither `apps/web` nor the Expo/Metro bundler still running (a session/day boundary, not a bug) - re-verified the machine's LAN IP still matched `apps/mobile/.env`'s `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL`, so no config change was needed, just a fresh start of both.

User then reported two small bugs and, separately, flagged a real upcoming Doctor Who edge case ahead of it causing a wrong scan result:

1. **Special Features Disc Count could exceed the physical disc count, and couldn't be set to 0** - on a multi-disc case, the stepper/auto-default only ever clamped to a minimum of 1, with no maximum at all, so it could be pushed past `discCount` entirely (more special-features discs than discs in the case). Added a `discCount - 1` cap (at least one disc has to be the main feature) to both the stepper's `setSfCount` and a new re-clamping `useEffect` that re-applies the cap if Disc Count itself is later reduced. Also explicitly allowed 0 as a valid value, not floored back up to 1 - the user's own real case: a long film split across two discs where disc 1 carries the movie plus some bonus content and disc 2 carries the same movie continued, so neither disc is "the" dedicated special-features disc. `apps/mobile` typechecks clean.
2. **"Why is there a 'Needed from the case' section when the system already found Animation?"** - investigated and explained rather than changed, since this turned out to be expected behavior, not a bug: that section's visibility (`needsCaseInfo`, ConfirmScreen.tsx) is driven entirely by whether TMDb returned Rating/Studio/Original Language/a TMDb match at all, completely independent of whatever Animation/Live Action ended up being. Rating already does get a cover-vision pre-fill (`coverRatingGuess`) when legible on the case photo, but the section still surfaces it as an editable, confirmable field either way (same "vision guess is a suggestion, never a confirmed fact" convention as everywhere else) - Studio and Original Language currently have no cover-vision source at all, so they're always asked for manually whenever TMDb doesn't supply them, regardless of genre/animation status.

**ANIMATED-RECONSTRUCTION DETECTION FOR CLASSIC DOCTOR WHO (2026-10-01)** - the user flagged this ahead of a real scan tripping over it: several First/Second Doctor serials are missing some or all of their episodes from the BBC archive, and a growing number of these have since been officially reissued with the missing episode(s) redrawn as animation (the exact case behind this whole Doctor Who feature area, "The Underwater Menace," is itself one of these - episodes 2 and 3 are animated, 1 and 4 are the surviving originals). The user's own three-part diagnostic: does the cover art look drawn, does the matched serial have missing episodes, and - if most/all of a serial is missing - there almost certainly isn't a purely-live-action DVD of it at all (it would only exist as fragments inside the "Doctor Who Lost in Time" compilation, never as its own catalogue entry).

Asked three clarifying questions before building anything (per the standing "ask lots of questions on hard Doctor Who parts" instruction), since this needed real missing-episode data this project had no source for yet: (1) data source - user said "there's bound to be a wiki online for that stuff," so fetched Wikipedia's "List of incomplete Doctor Who serials" directly (via WebFetch) rather than relying on hand-recalled trivia, cross-checked the resulting 26-serial/season breakdown against `build-classic-who-serial-index.ts`'s own already-hand-verified `CLASSIC_WHO_SEASONS` titles/episode-counts/seasons and found every one matched exactly; (2) what changes on detection - "just Animation to live action, but keep the animation field able to edit... so I could change it to 2D or 3D" - confirmed: a pre-fill only, never locked, exactly the same "suggestion, not fact" convention as every other vision-derived guess on this screen; (3) whether to also add a cover-art check - "also add a cover art-style check," so this became two independent, either-can-trigger signals rather than the data match alone.

Built: `missing_episode_count` column on `classic_who_serial_index` (`0042_add_classic_who_missing_episodes.sql`, defaults to 0 - the vast majority of classic serials are fully intact - and the `fuzzy_search_classic_who_serials` RPC now returns it too), populated for the 26 known incomplete serials by `scripts/src/backfill-classic-who-missing-episodes.ts` (hand-verified against the Wikipedia fetch, dry-run/`--apply`, never writes a 0 itself so it's always safe to re-run). `classicWhoSerials.ts`'s `matchClassicWhoSerial` now returns `missingEpisodeCount` as part of its synthetic candidate's `classicWhoSerial` payload (added to `OmdbSearchCandidate` in `packages/shared/src/omdb.ts` too). `coverVision.ts` gained a new `artStyle` ("drawn"/"photographic"/"unclear", folded to `drawn`/`photographic`/`null`) read off the same existing cover-photo Gemini call - added to the schema's `required` array from the start this time, having already been burned once by leaving a field optional in `detectCoverBoundingBox`'s own schema (2026-09-30) and watching the model silently omit it on most real calls.

ConfirmScreen.tsx's existing classic-serial prefill effect (season/episode count/genre location, from the day before) now also prefills Animation/Live Action: fully-missing (`missingEpisodeCount >= episodeCount`) -> "Animation"; partially-missing -> the already-existing free-text value "Live Action/Animation Hybrid" (exactly describes a disc like The Underwater Menace's own release, part surviving footage/part reconstruction); no missing-episode data but a "drawn" cover-art read -> "Animation" too, as a fallback for a serial this backfill hasn't caught yet. Had to also override `showAnimationField` (and the `isLikelyAnimatedWhoReconstruction` flag it's built from) to stay true for one of these regardless of what TMDb's own `isAnimated` genre flag says - TMDb has no reason to know a *specific disc release* of an ordinarily-live-action classic show is a reconstruction, so its usual "isAnimated: false" read would otherwise have hidden/locked the field to "Live Action" before the more specific Doctor-Who-domain signal even got a chance, silently defeating the whole point of keeping the field open and editable for 2D vs. 3D. All five workspaces typecheck clean; migration 0042 and the new backfill script have not yet been applied/run for real (pending a fresh Supabase Management API token from the user).

2026-10-03: Session opened by restarting both dev servers. `apps/web` initially came up on the default port 3000, but `apps/mobile/.env`'s `EXPO_PUBLIC_SCAN_API_URL` points at `http://192.168.1.70:3001`, so it was restarted with `-p 3001 -H 0.0.0.0`. Expo was pinned to the Wi-Fi IP (`REACT_NATIVE_PACKAGER_HOSTNAME=192.168.1.70`) since this machine also has a VMware adapter (192.168.227.1) that Expo would otherwise advertise to the phone.

Pending Scans hides scans that are saving or have succeeded, and a failed scan's error shows when it's reopened. 2026-10-04: Restarted both dev servers (both had stopped at the 2-hour background limit; LAN IP unchanged). User forwarded Supabase's "scheduled to be paused" email for the public project and asked whether to ignore it. Pinged both projects by hand straight away (resets the 7-day clock). The user then provided a GitHub PAT in chat, used only inline for read-only Actions API calls and not saved anywhere. It showed the keep-alive workflow had run successfully every 3 days with all four Supabase secrets present, and the 2026-10-01 log showed `"public" ping OK`. So the job was working, but a single `limit(1)` read every 3 days evidently isn't "sufficient activity" by Supabase's undocumented standard. Changed the workflow to run daily and made each ping heavier (exact count, a 50-row read, Auth health, a Storage list), still anon-key only. If another pause email arrives, the next step would be a real write heartbeat (needs a migration on the public project).

2026-10-06 (Gemini allowance): User asked how many cases the free Gemini allowance covers. Counted the calls: 3 per cover photo, so about 80 front+back cases a day at 500 requests. They asked to (1) merge the crop into one request and (2) skip rotation on back photos, and to look for a better free model, keeping the current one if none was better.
- **Merge.** Merging the crop into the read would have broken their crop-before-read rule. Instead, rotation and the crop box became one request, with the read on the cropped photo: 2 requests per photo, 4 per case. That also covers (2): the back photo's rotation check now rides along with the crop request instead of costing a request of its own (the side isn't known until the read).
- **Models.** Google no longer publishes free limits, and third-party sources disagree. Tested live on the user's photos:
  - 2.5 models are closed to new users;
  - 3.5+ Flash gets about 20 free requests a day;
  - Gemma 4 31B is too slow;
  - 3.1 Flash-Lite got 9/9 on rotation + box;
  - Gemma 4 26B got 8/9 on rotation but 4/9 on boxes;
  - read quality was similar between 3.1 Flash-Lite and Gemma 4 26B.
- **Result.** Kept the existing model first and added a per-model fallback chain (3.5 Flash-Lite, then 3.1 Flash-Lite, then Gemma 4 26B) in `geminiRequest.ts`. Every Gemini call goes through it. This also restored cover reading the same day, since the first model's quota was used up. Started the stored-photo rotation repair dry run on the chain.
